# React Aria conformance mining — interactions and overlays

## Sources

- Repo: https://github.com/adobe/react-spectrum
- Commit: `4693fcc844a341107e7dd26fe456edb1e269e44c` (2026-09-09)
- Mined: 2026-09-13
- Scope: interactions and overlays half (button, interactions, focus, overlays, dialog, tooltip, toast, switch, checkbox, radio, toggle, separator, progress, meter, tabs, disclosure, visually-hidden, ssr, utils, i18n, test-utils).

Note on repo layout: as of this commit the monorepo has been restructured. The `@react-aria/*` and `@react-spectrum/*` packages are thin re-export shims; the real hook tests live in `packages/react-aria/test/**`, the Spectrum v3 component tests in `packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/**`, Spectrum 2 in `packages/@react-spectrum/s2/test/**`, and React Aria Components in `packages/react-aria-components/test/**`.

### Nothing to mine, or deliberately left out

- **live-announcer / live-region**: there is no test file for `@react-aria/live-announcer` anywhere in the repo. The only tests that touch `announce()` belong to collections components (table, listbox, combobox, tag group, tree, number field, calendar, action bar, chat), which are the other half of this survey.
- **slider**: the slug exists in the agreed list, but slider was not in the assigned package list, so `packages/react-aria-components/test/Slider.test.js`, `packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/slider/*`, `packages/react-aria/test/slider/*`, and `packages/react-stately/test/slider/*` were not read.
- **`packages/react-aria/test/actiongroup/useActionGroup.test.ts`** and the Spectrum `ActionGroup` tests: ActionGroup is a collection component, so it belongs to the other half. Its sibling `packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx` _was_ read, because that file tests the Toolbar pattern.
- **`packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/provider/*`**: `Provider.test.tsx` and `mediaQueries.test.ts` cover Spectrum theming (color scheme, responsive style props), not locale or direction, so they fall outside the i18n scope.
- **Link, landmark, breadcrumbs, DnD, spinbutton, autocomplete, selection**: adjacent interaction packages that were not in the assigned list.

### Reading the source tags

A source tag is `(react-spectrum: <path>: "<test name>")`. The test name is the literal `it()`/`test()` string. This suite leans heavily on `it.each` with tagged-template tables, so a fair number of names contain unresolved placeholders such as `$Name`, `$type`, `contain=$contain`, or `$interactionType`; those single names cover several test instances, and the Given clause says which variants. Where the same `it` name appears in two different `describe` blocks of one file (common in the pointer-events vs mouse-events split), the Given clause is the only thing that distinguishes the bullets.

## button

### Roles and attributes

- Given useButton (bare hook layer) called with no props, when the returned buttonProps are inspected, then onClick is present as a function, with role and tabIndex left at native <button> defaults. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/button/useButton.test.js: "handles defaults")
- Given useButton (bare hook layer) called with elementType: 'a' (anchor variant), when the returned buttonProps are inspected, then role is "button", tabIndex is 0, aria-disabled is undefined, href is undefined, onKeyDown is a function, and rel is undefined. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/button/useButton.test.js: "handles elements other than button")
- Given useButton (bare hook layer) called with elementType: 'a' and isDisabled: true (disabled anchor variant), when the returned buttonProps are inspected, then role is "button", tabIndex is undefined (removed from tab order), aria-disabled is truthy, href is undefined, onKeyDown is a function, and rel is undefined. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/button/useButton.test.js: "handles elements other than button disabled")
- Given useButton (bare hook layer) called with elementType: 'a' and rel: 'noopener noreferrer', when the returned buttonProps are inspected, then rel is passed through unchanged as "noopener noreferrer". (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/button/useButton.test.js: "handles the rel attribute on anchors")
- Given useButton (bare hook layer) called with elementType: 'a' and rel: 'search', when the returned buttonProps are inspected, then rel is passed through unchanged as "search". (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/button/useButton.test.js: "handles the rel attribute as a string on anchors")
- Given useButton (bare hook layer) called with elementType: 'input' and isDisabled: true (native <input> variant), when the returned buttonProps are inspected, then role is "button", tabIndex is undefined, aria-disabled is undefined (native disabled is used instead), the native disabled attribute is truthy, href is undefined, onKeyDown is a function, and rel is undefined. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/button/useButton.test.js: "handles input elements")
- Given useButton (bare hook layer) called on a plain button element with 'aria-disabled': 'true' passed explicitly, when the returned buttonProps are inspected, then aria-disabled is truthy (passed through) while the native disabled prop remains undefined — explicit aria-disabled is not converted into native disabled. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/button/useButton.test.js: "handles aria-disabled passthrough for button elements")
- Given ActionButton (Spectrum v3) rendered with aria-label="Test", when the rendered element is queried by role button, then it carries aria-label="Test". (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "$Name supports aria-label")
- Given Button (Spectrum v3) rendered with aria-label="Test", when the rendered element is queried by role button, then it carries aria-label="Test". (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "$Name supports aria-label")
- Given ClearButton (Spectrum v3) rendered with aria-label="Test", when the rendered element is queried by role button, then it carries aria-label="Test". (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "$Name supports aria-label")
- Given LogicButton (Spectrum v3) rendered with aria-label="Test", when the rendered element is queried by role button, then it carries aria-label="Test". (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "$Name supports aria-label")
- Given ActionButton (Spectrum v3) rendered alongside a labelling <span id="test"> and aria-labelledby="test", when the rendered element is queried by role button, then it carries aria-labelledby="test". (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "$Name supports aria-labelledby")
- Given Button (Spectrum v3) rendered alongside a labelling <span id="test"> and aria-labelledby="test", when the rendered element is queried by role button, then it carries aria-labelledby="test". (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "$Name supports aria-labelledby")
- Given ClearButton (Spectrum v3) rendered alongside a labelling <span id="test"> and aria-labelledby="test", when the rendered element is queried by role button, then it carries aria-labelledby="test". (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "$Name supports aria-labelledby")
- Given LogicButton (Spectrum v3) rendered alongside a labelling <span id="test"> and aria-labelledby="test", when the rendered element is queried by role button, then it carries aria-labelledby="test". (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "$Name supports aria-labelledby")
- Given ActionButton (Spectrum v3) rendered alongside a describing <span id="test"> and aria-describedby="test", when the rendered element is queried by role button, then it carries aria-describedby="test". (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "$Name supports aria-describedby")
- Given Button (Spectrum v3) rendered alongside a describing <span id="test"> and aria-describedby="test", when the rendered element is queried by role button, then it carries aria-describedby="test". (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "$Name supports aria-describedby")
- Given ClearButton (Spectrum v3) rendered alongside a describing <span id="test"> and aria-describedby="test", when the rendered element is queried by role button, then it carries aria-describedby="test". (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "$Name supports aria-describedby")
- Given LogicButton (Spectrum v3) rendered alongside a describing <span id="test"> and aria-describedby="test", when the rendered element is queried by role button, then it carries aria-describedby="test". (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "$Name supports aria-describedby")
- Given ActionButton (Spectrum v3) rendered with elementType="a" and no href, when the rendered element is inspected, then it has tabindex="0" and does not have a type="button" attribute. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "$Name can have elementType=a")
- Given Button (Spectrum v3) rendered with elementType="a" and no href, when the rendered element is inspected, then it has tabindex="0" and does not have a type="button" attribute. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "$Name can have elementType=a")
- Given LogicButton (Spectrum v3) rendered with elementType="a" and no href, when the rendered element is inspected, then it has tabindex="0" and does not have a type="button" attribute. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "$Name can have elementType=a")
- Given ActionButton (Spectrum v3) rendered with elementType="a" and href="#only-hash-in-jsdom", when the rendered element is inspected, then it has tabindex="0" and href="#only-hash-in-jsdom". (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "$Name can have elementType=a with an href")
- Given Button (Spectrum v3) rendered with elementType="a" and href="#only-hash-in-jsdom", when the rendered element is inspected, then it has tabindex="0" and href="#only-hash-in-jsdom". (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "$Name can have elementType=a with an href")
- Given LogicButton (Spectrum v3) rendered with elementType="a" and href="#only-hash-in-jsdom", when the rendered element is inspected, then it has tabindex="0" and href="#only-hash-in-jsdom". (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "$Name can have elementType=a with an href")
- Given a Spectrum v3 Button rendered with elementType="a", href="//example.com", and isPending true, when the button is rendered, then the href attribute is removed from the anchor element while it is pending. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "removes href attribute from anchor element when isPending is true")
- Given an ActionButton (Spectrum v3) rendered with default (non-toggle) props, when the rendered button element is inspected, then it has no aria-pressed or aria-checked attribute. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/ActionButton.test.js: "$Name handles defaults")
- Given an ActionButton (Spectrum v3) rendered with an arbitrary custom DOM prop data-foo="bar", when the button is queried by role (including hidden elements), then the underlying button element has the data-foo="bar" attribute passed through. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/ActionButton.test.js: "$Name allows custom props to be passed through to the button")
- Given a v3 ClearButton rendered with a custom data-foo="bar" prop, when the button element is queried (with {hidden: true}), then it has the data-foo="bar" attribute passed through to the DOM. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/ClearButton.test.js: "$Name allows custom props to be passed through to the button")
- Given a react-aria-components Button rendered with no custom className, when the rendered button element is inspected, then it has class="react-aria-Button" as the default class name. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Button.test.js: "should render a button with default class")
- Given a react-aria-components Button rendered with className="test", when inspecting the rendered button element, then its class attribute is exactly test, overriding the default class. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Button.test.js: "should render a button with custom class")
- Given a react-aria-components Button rendered with an arbitrary data-foo="bar" prop, when inspecting the rendered button element, then the DOM prop is forwarded as the data-foo attribute. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Button.test.js: "should support DOM props")
- Given a react-aria-components Button rendered with aria-current="page", when inspecting the rendered button element, then it has the aria-current="page" attribute passed through. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Button.test.js: "should support accessibility props")
- Given a react-aria-components Button rendered with no disabled-related props, when inspecting the rendered button element, then it has no aria-disabled attribute at all. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Button.test.js: "should not have aria-disabled defined by default")
- Given a react-aria-components Button rendered with aria-disabled="true" explicitly passed, when inspecting the rendered button element, then it has aria-disabled="true", distinct from the isDisabled prop. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Button.test.js: "should support aria-disabled passthrough")
- Given a react-aria-components Button rendered with slot="test" inside a ButtonContext.Provider whose value defines slots: {test: {'aria-label': 'test'}}, when inspecting the rendered button element, then it has slot="test" and picks up aria-label="test" merged in from the matching slot configuration in context. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Button.test.js: "should support slot")
- Given a react-aria-components Button rendered with isDisabled and a className function keyed on isDisabled, when inspecting the rendered button, then it is natively disabled (toBeDisabled()) and has the disabled class applied. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Button.test.js: "should support disabled state")
- Given a react-aria-components Button rendered as a link (href="//example.com") with isPending true, when inspecting the rendered element, then it has no href attribute — pending state strips the anchor's href to prevent navigation while pending. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Button.test.js: "removes href attribute from anchor element when isPending is true")

### Keyboard

- Given ActionButton (Spectrum v3) rendered with onPress/onPressStart/onPressEnd/onPressUp/onPressChange/onKeyDown/onKeyUp handlers, when tabbed into focus and {Enter} is pressed, then onPressStart/onPress/onPressEnd/onPressUp each fire once, onPressChange fires twice, native onKeyDown fires once and onKeyUp fires twice total, and a subsequent non-activating key ("A") increments key-event counts without triggering additional press events. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "$Name keyboard press and key events")
- Given Button (Spectrum v3) rendered with onPress/onPressStart/onPressEnd/onPressUp/onPressChange/onKeyDown/onKeyUp handlers, when tabbed into focus and {Enter} is pressed, then onPressStart/onPress/onPressEnd/onPressUp each fire once, onPressChange fires twice, native onKeyDown fires once and onKeyUp fires twice total, and a subsequent non-activating key ("A") increments key-event counts without triggering additional press events. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "$Name keyboard press and key events")
- Given LogicButton (Spectrum v3) rendered with onPress/onPressStart/onPressEnd/onPressUp/onPressChange/onKeyDown/onKeyUp handlers, when tabbed into focus and {Enter} is pressed, then onPressStart/onPress/onPressEnd/onPressUp each fire once, onPressChange fires twice, native onKeyDown fires once and onKeyUp fires twice total, and a subsequent non-activating key ("A") increments key-event counts without triggering additional press events. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "$Name keyboard press and key events")
- Given ActionButton (Spectrum v3) rendered with elementType="a", an onPress handler, and no href, when the anchor is clicked then Enter is pressed then Space is pressed, then onPress fires cumulatively (1, then 2, then 3 times) — a non-<button> element (anchor without href) supports activation via both Enter and Space keys, not just click. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "$Name can have elementType=a")

(react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "$Name can have elementType=a")
- Given a default (non-submit-type) Spectrum v3 Button inside a Form with a Checkbox sibling, when the button is focused and {Enter} is pressed, then the button's native keydown and keyup events both have defaultPrevented true — the press implementation blocks the browser's native Enter handling for a non-submit button. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "prevents default for non-submit types")
- Given the same default (non-submit-type) Spectrum v3 Button inside a Form, when [Space] is subsequently pressed while the button is focused, then the native keydown and keyup events both have defaultPrevented true as well. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "prevents default for non-submit types")
- Given a type="submit" react-aria-components Button focused via Tab, when Enter is pressed before the button's deferred isPending state (set via setTimeout in onPress) takes effect, then the form submits (onSubmit is called). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Button.test.js: "should prevent explicit keyboard form submission when isPending")
- Given that same submit Button once isPending has become true, when Enter is pressed again, then onSubmit is not called — pending state blocks Enter-key-triggered form submission on a focused button. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Button.test.js: "should prevent explicit keyboard form submission when isPending")

- Given a Spectrum v3 Button with isPending state that starts false and an onPress callback that flips isPending to true, when the button is clicked once via userEvent, then aria-disabled="true" is applied to the button immediately even though the visual spinner is not yet shown. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "displays a spinner after a short delay when isPending prop is true")
- Given the same pending Spectrum v3 Button already marked aria-disabled="true" after one click, when it is clicked a second time before the spinner-visibility delay elapses, then the second click does not invoke the onPress callback again — onPressSpy remains called exactly once total after fake timers are advanced by the spinner delay. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "displays a spinner after a short delay when isPending prop is true")
- Given an ActionButton rendered with an onPress handler and default props, when a user clicks the rendered button element (mouse pointer), then onPress fires exactly once. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/ActionButton.test.js: "$Name handles defaults")
- Given a v3 ClearButton rendered with an onPress handler, when a user clicks the rendered button element (queried with {hidden: true} since ClearButton renders visually-hidden text/icon content), then onPress fires exactly once. - Given a react-aria-components Button using a custom render function keyed off isPressed, when a mouse button is pressed down and then released on the rendered button, then data-custom toggles to "pressed" while pressed and reverts to "bar" on release, confirming press state is exposed to custom render functions. - Given that same hoverable Button, when the pointer moves away, then data-hovered and the hover class are removed, onHoverEnd fires once, and onHoverChange's total call count reaches two. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Button.test.js: "should support hover")
- Given a react-aria-components Button with className derived from isPressed plus onPress/onClick/onClickCapture handlers, when a mouse button is pressed down on it, then it gains data-pressed="true" and the pressed class. - Given that same Button, when the mouse button is released, then data-pressed and the pressed class are removed, and each of onPress, onClick, and onClickCapture has fired exactly once for the single press interaction. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Button.test.js: "should support press state")
- Given a react-aria-components Button whose children render function returns 'Pressed' when isPressed else 'Test', when the mouse is pressed down on the button, then its text content becomes "Pressed"; when released, then it reverts to "Test". - Given a react-aria-components Button that has become pending after one click, when it is clicked again while pending, then the onPress handler is not invoked a second time (remains at 1 total call) — pending state prevents double-firing on pointer clicks. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Button.test.js: "displays a spinner when isPending prop is true")

### Focus management

- Given ActionButton (Spectrum v3) rendered with the autoFocus prop, when the component mounts, then the rendered button element becomes document.activeElement automatically. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "$Name supports autoFocus")
- Given Button (Spectrum v3) rendered with the autoFocus prop, when the component mounts, then the rendered button element becomes document.activeElement automatically. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "$Name supports autoFocus")
- Given LogicButton (Spectrum v3) rendered with the autoFocus prop, when the component mounts, then the rendered button element becomes document.activeElement automatically. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "$Name supports autoFocus")
- Given a v3 ClearButton with a forwarded ref, when ref.current.focus() is called imperatively, then document.activeElement becomes the button element, confirming the exposed instance supports programmatic focus(). (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/ClearButton.test.js: "$Name allows a user to forward a ref to the button")
- Given a react-aria-components Button with className derived from isFocusVisible, when it receives focus via keyboard Tab navigation, then it gains data-focus-visible="true" and the focus class, and becomes document.activeElement. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Button.test.js: "should support focus ring")
- Given a focus-visible Button that currently has keyboard focus, when Tab moves focus to the next element, then data-focus-visible and the focus class are removed from the button. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Button.test.js: "should support focus ring")
- Given a pending Button (isPending) with onFocus/onBlur handlers used as a DialogTrigger trigger, when it is clicked while pending, then onFocus is called but onBlur is not — the button retains focus after the suppressed press since no dialog opened to move focus away. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Button.test.js: "disables press when in pending state for context")
- Given that same pending DialogTrigger Button, when focus subsequently moves away by clicking document.body, then onBlur fires on the pending button, confirming it still participates in the normal focus/blur lifecycle despite suppressed press. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Button.test.js: "disables press when in pending state for context")

### State and controlled/uncontrolled

- Given a Spectrum v3 Button whose isPending prop starts false, when the button is initially rendered (before any interaction), then it has no aria-disabled attribute at all. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "displays a spinner after a short delay when isPending prop is true")
- Given the same pending Spectrum v3 Button after being clicked and after fake timers are advanced by the spinnerVisibilityDelay (1000ms), when the progressbar is queried (including hidden elements), then the spinner becomes visible while aria-disabled="true" remains on the button — the pending-state spinner appears only after the configured delay rather than immediately. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "displays a spinner after a short delay when isPending prop is true")
- Given a react-aria-components Button rendered with a controlled isPending boolean and children that swap between text and an indeterminate ProgressBar based on the isPending render-prop, when isPending is false, then the button has no aria-disabled attribute. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Button.test.js: "displays a spinner when isPending prop is true")
- Given the same pending-capable Button, when it is clicked (setting isPending to true synchronously inside the onPress handler), then the button immediately gains aria-disabled="true" even though the spinner's visual appearance is delayed. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Button.test.js: "displays a spinner when isPending prop is true")

### Forms (name, value, validation, form reset)

- Given a Spectrum v3 Button with type="submit" inside a Form (with a Checkbox sibling), when the button is focused and {Space} is pressed, then the native keyup event is NOT prevented — the library defers to the browser's native "space submits on keyup" behavior for a submit-type button. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "submit in form using space")
- Given a Spectrum v3 Button with type="submit" inside a Form (with a Checkbox sibling), when the button is focused and [Enter] is pressed, then the native keydown event is not marked as prevented by the button's own key handling — the library allows Enter to submit on keydown for a submit-type button; the test works around a jsdom gap where HTMLFormElement.prototype.requestSubmit is not implemented. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/Button.test.js: "submit in form using enter")
- Given a react-aria-components Button rendered inside a <form id="foo"> with form="foo" and formMethod="post" props, when inspecting the rendered button element, then it has form="foo" and formMethod="post" attributes forwarded, associating it with a form by id and setting its submission method. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Button.test.js: "should support form props")
- Given a type="submit" Button inside a <form> whose onPress handler defers setting isPending to true via setTimeout, when clicked once (before the timer fires), then the form's onSubmit handler is called — submission goes through before pending is applied. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Button.test.js: "should prevent explicit mouse form submission when isPending")
- Given that same submit Button once its deferred isPending state has taken effect (timers run), when it is clicked again with a mouse, then onSubmit is not called — an explicit mouse click on a pending submit button no longer submits the form. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Button.test.js: "should prevent explicit mouse form submission when isPending")
- Given a form containing two text inputs (required to avoid the browser's single-input implicit-submission special case) and a type="submit" Button whose isPending is set to true asynchronously after the Enter keyup (to correctly emulate implicit-submission timing), when Tab focuses an input and Enter is pressed before isPending takes effect, then the form implicitly submits (onSubmit is called). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Button.test.js: "should prevent implicit form submission when isPending")
- Given that same form once the submit Button's isPending has become true, when Enter is pressed again in the focused input, then implicit form submission is suppressed and onSubmit is not called again. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Button.test.js: "should prevent implicit form submission when isPending")

### Dismissal and layering

- Given a Button with isPending used as the trigger inside a DialogTrigger wrapping a Modal/Dialog (role="alertdialog"), when the pending trigger button is clicked, then the dialog does not open (queryByRole('alertdialog') returns null) — press is suppressed while the trigger button is pending, preventing the dialog-opening action. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Button.test.js: "disables press when in pending state for context")

(react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/ClearButton.test.js: "$Name doesn't accept an icon as a prop")
- Given a v3 ClearButton rendered with a React ref, when the ref is resolved via ref.current.UNSAFE_getDOMNode(), then it equals the actual rendered button DOM element, confirming ref forwarding to the underlying button. - Given a react-aria-components Button rendered with a custom render function that receives (props, {isPressed}) and spreads props onto a native <button>, when not pressed, then the rendered element reflects data-custom="bar" sourced from the render-prop state. Expected <button>, got <div>. This may break the component behavior and accessibility." - Given a react-aria-components Button whose custom render function returns a <div> without forwarding the provided ref, when the component mounts, then it calls console.warn with "Ref was not connected to DOM element returned by custom `render` function. Did you forget to pass through or merge the `ref`?", flagging a broken ref chain in custom render implementations. ## toggle

### Roles and attributes

- Given an uncontrolled ToggleButton (Spectrum v3) rendered with onPress/onChange handlers and no selection props, when it first renders, then the underlying button has aria-pressed="false". (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/ToggleButton.test.js: "handles defaults")
- Given an uncontrolled ToggleButton (Spectrum v3) rendered with defaultSelected, when it first renders, then the button has aria-pressed="true" reflecting the initial uncontrolled selection. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/ToggleButton.test.js: "supports defaultSelected")
- Given a controlled ToggleButton (Spectrum v3) rendered with isSelected, when it first renders, then the button has aria-pressed="true". (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/ToggleButton.test.js: "supports isSelected")
- Given a ToggleButton (Spectrum v3) rendered with an arbitrary data-foo="bar" prop, when rendered, then the underlying <button> element receives that DOM attribute unchanged. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/ToggleButton.test.js: "allows custom props to be passed through to the button")
- Given a React Aria Components ToggleButton with no className prop, when rendered, then the button element carries the default class "react-aria-ToggleButton". (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButton.test.js: "should render a toggle button with default class")
- Given a React Aria Components ToggleButton rendered with className="test", when rendered, then the default class is replaced entirely by the custom class "test". (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButton.test.js: "should render a toggle button with custom class")
- Given a React Aria Components ToggleButton rendered with data-foo="bar", when rendered, then the button element exposes that arbitrary DOM attribute. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButton.test.js: "should support DOM props")
- Given a React Aria Components ToggleButton whose children is a render-prop function reading isSelected, when initially rendered unselected, then the button's text content reads "Off". (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButton.test.js: "should support render props")
- Given a React Aria Components ToggleButton rendered with a custom render prop supplying its own <button> element with data-custom="bar", when rendered, then the resulting DOM element carries data-custom="bar", showing the custom render function's props are applied. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButton.test.js: "should support custom render function")
- Given a ToggleButtonContext.Provider supplying slots: {test: {'aria-label': 'test'}} and a ToggleButton with slot="test" inside it, when rendered, then the button element receives both slot="test" and the aria-label="test" merged in from the slotted context value. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButton.test.js: "should support slot")

### Pointer (mouse, touch, pen, virtual click from screen readers)

- Given a React Aria Components ToggleButton whose className is a function of isHovered, when the pointer is not hovering it, then the button has no data-hovered attribute and no hover class. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButton.test.js: "should support hover")
- Given the same hover-aware ToggleButton, when the user hovers it with the pointer, then data-hovered="true" and the hover class are applied, and when the pointer subsequently unhovers it, then both the attribute and class are removed. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButton.test.js: "should support hover")
- Given a React Aria Components ToggleButton whose className is a function of isPressed (with onPress, onClick, and onClickCapture handlers attached), when the pointer presses down on it (mouse button down), then data-pressed="true" and the pressed class are applied. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButton.test.js: "should support press state")
- Given the same press-aware ToggleButton, when the pointer is released, then data-pressed and the pressed class are removed, and each of onPress, onClick, and onClickCapture has fired exactly once for the click. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButton.test.js: "should support press state")

### Focus management

- Given a React Aria Components ToggleButton whose className is a function of isFocusVisible, when the user tabs to focus it, then document.activeElement is the button, data-focus-visible="true" is set, and the focus class is applied. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButton.test.js: "should support focus ring")
- Given the same focus-ring-aware ToggleButton, when the user tabs away from it, then data-focus-visible and the focus class are removed. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButton.test.js: "should support focus ring")

### State and controlled/uncontrolled

- Given the uncontrolled Spectrum v3 ToggleButton from "handles defaults" (starting unselected), when the user clicks it, then onPress fires once, onChange is called with true, and aria-pressed transitions to "true". (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/ToggleButton.test.js: "handles defaults")
- Given the uncontrolled Spectrum v3 ToggleButton from "supports defaultSelected" (starting selected via defaultSelected), when the user clicks it, then onPress fires once, onChange is called with false, and aria-pressed transitions to "false", toggling the uncontrolled internal state. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/ToggleButton.test.js: "supports defaultSelected")
- Given the controlled Spectrum v3 ToggleButton from "supports isSelected" (prop isSelected fixed to true), when the user clicks it, then onPress fires once and onChange is called with false, but aria-pressed stays "true" because the controlled isSelected prop was never updated by the consumer. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/button/ToggleButton.test.js: "supports isSelected")
- Given the render-prop ToggleButton from "should support render props" (React Aria Components), when the user clicks it, then its internal selection state flips to selected and the render-prop children re-render to show "On". (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButton.test.js: "should support render props")
- Given a React Aria Components ToggleButton rendered with isDisabled and a className function of isDisabled, when rendered, then the button is disabled (toBeDisabled) and carries the disabled class. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButton.test.js: "should support disabled state")
- Given a React Aria Components ToggleButton with an onChange handler and a className function of isSelected, when initially rendered unselected, then aria-pressed="false" and no selected class is applied. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButton.test.js: "should support selected state")
- Given the same selectable ToggleButton, when the user clicks it once, then onChange is last called with true, aria-pressed becomes "true", and the selected class is applied. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButton.test.js: "should support selected state")
- Given the same selectable ToggleButton now selected, when the user clicks it again, then onChange is last called with false, aria-pressed reverts to "false", and the selected class is removed. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButton.test.js: "should support selected state")

## toggle-group

### Roles and attributes

- Given a ToggleButtonGroup (react-aria-components) with default props containing two toggle buttons, when queried by its group role, then it renders with role "radiogroup" and carries the default class "react-aria-ToggleButtonGroup". (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButtonGroup.test.js: "should render a toggle button group with default class")
- Given a ToggleButtonGroup (react-aria-components) rendered with isDisabled and a className render-prop function keyed on isDisabled, when queried, then the group element carries class "disabled", data-disabled="true", and aria-disabled="true". (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButtonGroup.test.js: "should support disabled state")
- Given a ToggleButtonGroup (react-aria-components) with selectionMode="single" and no initial selectedKeys (uncontrolled), when rendered, then the group exposes role "radiogroup" and each child button has role "radio" with aria-checked="false" and no aria-pressed attribute. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButtonGroup.test.js: "should support uncontrolled single selection")
- Given a ToggleButtonGroup (react-aria-components) with selectionMode="single" and a controlled empty selectedKeys array, when rendered, then all radios start with aria-checked="false" and no aria-pressed attribute. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButtonGroup.test.js: "should support controlled single selection")
- Given a ToggleButtonGroup (react-aria-components) with selectionMode="multiple" and no initial selectedKeys (uncontrolled), when rendered, then the group exposes role "toolbar" and each child button (role "button") has aria-pressed="false" instead of aria-checked. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButtonGroup.test.js: "should support uncontrolled multiple selection")
- Given a ToggleButtonGroup (react-aria-components) with default (horizontal) orientation, when queried, then the group has aria-orientation="horizontal". (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButtonGroup.test.js: "should support horizontal keyboard navigation")
- Given a ToggleButtonGroup (react-aria-components) rendered with orientation="vertical", when queried, then the group has aria-orientation="vertical" instead of horizontal. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButtonGroup.test.js: "should support vertical keyboard navigation")
- Given a ToggleButtonGroup (react-aria-components) rendered with aria-label="Test" in single-selection mode, when queried, then the radiogroup element exposes aria-label="Test"; and given the same aria-label with selectionMode="multiple", then the resulting toolbar element also exposes aria-label="Test" — the label is applied regardless of whether the container role is radiogroup or toolbar. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButtonGroup.test.js: "supports aria labeling")

### Keyboard

- Given a horizontal ToggleButtonGroup (react-aria-components, single-selection radiogroup), when the user tabs into the group and presses ArrowRight then ArrowLeft, then focus (roving tabindex) moves from the first radio to the second and back to the first along the horizontal axis. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButtonGroup.test.js: "should support horizontal keyboard navigation")
- Given a vertical ToggleButtonGroup (react-aria-components, orientation="vertical"), when the user tabs into the group and presses ArrowDown then ArrowUp, then focus moves from the first radio to the second and back to the first along the vertical axis instead of using left/right arrows. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButtonGroup.test.js: "should support vertical keyboard navigation")

### Pointer (mouse, touch, pen, virtual click from screen readers)

- Given a disabled ToggleButtonGroup (react-aria-components) with a className render-prop keyed on isHovered, under jsdom's simulated PointerEvent support (installPointerEvent mock, since jsdom lacks native pointer events), when the user hovers each disabled radio button, then neither button gains the data-hovered attribute nor the hover class — disabled buttons suppress hover styling entirely. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButtonGroup.test.js: "should not show hover state when disabled")
- Given an uncontrolled single-selection ToggleButtonGroup (react-aria-components) where both radios start unchecked, when the user clicks the second radio, then onSelectionChange fires once with a Set containing only that key ('bar') and aria-checked flips to true on the clicked radio while the other stays false. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButtonGroup.test.js: "should support uncontrolled single selection")
- Given that same group with 'bar' now selected, when the user clicks the first radio, then onSelectionChange fires again with a Set containing only 'foo' — clicking a new radio in single-selection mode deselects the previously selected one, with aria-checked flipping on both radios accordingly. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButtonGroup.test.js: "should support uncontrolled single selection")
- Given a controlled single-selection ToggleButtonGroup (selectedKeys=[] passed explicitly), when the user clicks the second radio, then onSelectionChange fires once with a Set containing 'bar', but because selectedKeys remains unchanged by the controlling component, both radios stay aria-checked="false" — the visual state does not update without the consumer applying the new selectedKeys. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButtonGroup.test.js: "should support controlled single selection")
- Given an uncontrolled multiple-selection ToggleButtonGroup, when the user clicks the second button and then the first button, then onSelectionChange fires with Set(['bar']) and then Set(['bar','foo']) respectively, and aria-pressed becomes "true" on each clicked button while a previously-pressed button stays pressed — multiple selection allows more than one button pressed simultaneously. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButtonGroup.test.js: "should support uncontrolled multiple selection")
- Given a controlled multiple-selection ToggleButtonGroup with selectedKeys=['foo'] (so the first button starts aria-pressed="true" and the second "false"), when the user clicks the second (unselected) button, then onSelectionChange fires once with a Set containing both 'foo' and 'bar', while the rendered aria-pressed values remain governed by the unchanged controlled selectedKeys prop rather than updating on their own. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButtonGroup.test.js: "should support controlled multiple selection")

### State and controlled/uncontrolled

- Given an uncontrolled single-selection ToggleButtonGroup (react-aria-components), when selection changes via user interaction, then the group manages its own selectedKeys state internally and reflects it through aria-checked without the consumer supplying selectedKeys. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButtonGroup.test.js: "should support uncontrolled single selection")
- Given a controlled single-selection ToggleButtonGroup that is passed a fixed selectedKeys=[] and an onSelectionChange handler, when the user clicks a radio, then onSelectionChange is invoked with the new Set the consumer would need to apply, but the rendered aria-checked state remains unchanged since the group defers to the (static) controlled prop. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButtonGroup.test.js: "should support controlled single selection")
- Given a controlled multiple-selection ToggleButtonGroup with selectedKeys=['foo'], when the user toggles the second button, then onSelectionChange receives a Set of both keys, demonstrating that controlled multiple selection unions the newly toggled key with the existing controlled selection in the callback payload even though the DOM's aria-pressed doesn't move on its own. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButtonGroup.test.js: "should support controlled multiple selection")

### Positioning

- Given a Spectrum 2 ButtonGroup with three buttons whose labels contain no wrappable space, placed inside a wrapper that starts 500px wide, run in a real browser environment (ButtonGroup.browser.test.tsx uses vitest's browser mode with real layout/ResizeObserver rather than jsdom) so getComputedStyle reflects true flex layout, when the wrapper is shrunk to 50px, then the ButtonGroup's computed flexDirection changes from "row" to "column" to stack the buttons vertically and avoid overflow. [test is skipped in the suite] (react-spectrum: packages/@react-spectrum/s2/test/ButtonGroup.browser.test.tsx: "stacks vertically when the container shrinks below the width of buttons with no-space labels")
- Given that same real-browser ButtonGroup test after it has stacked vertically at 50px width, when the wrapper width is restored to 500px, then the computed flexDirection reverts from "column" back to "row", returning to horizontal layout. [test is skipped in the suite] (react-spectrum: packages/@react-spectrum/s2/test/ButtonGroup.browser.test.tsx: "stacks vertically when the container shrinks below the width of buttons with no-space labels")

### Notes and edge cases

- Given a ToggleButtonGroup (react-aria-components) rendered with a custom className prop, when queried, then the group element's class attribute reflects that custom class instead of the library's default class. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButtonGroup.test.js: "should render a toggle button group with a custom class")
- Given a ToggleButtonGroup (react-aria-components) rendered with an arbitrary DOM prop such as data-foo, when queried, then that prop is spread onto the rendered group element (render-props/data attribute passthrough). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButtonGroup.test.js: "should support render props")
- Given a ToggleButtonGroup (react-aria-components) rendered with a custom render prop supplying an alternate host element with an extra attribute, when queried, then the custom render function is used and its extra attribute (data-custom="true") appears on the resulting group element. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButtonGroup.test.js: "should support custom render function")
- Given a disabled ToggleButtonGroup (react-aria-components, isDisabled on the group), when inspecting each child toggle button (radio role), then every one of them is individually disabled — the group's isDisabled propagates down to disable all member buttons. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ToggleButtonGroup.test.js: "should support disabled state")
- Given a Spectrum 2 ToggleButtonGroup rendered with isDisabled and three child ToggleButtons, when queried by role, then every child button (radio role) is disabled — group-level isDisabled disables all individual toggle buttons in the Spectrum 2 layer as well. (react-spectrum: packages/@react-spectrum/s2/test/ToggleButtonGroup.test.tsx: "can disable all buttons from the group")
- Given a Spectrum 2 ActionButtonGroup rendered with isDisabled and three child ActionButtons, when queried by role "button", then every child action button is disabled — group-level isDisabled propagates to all non-toggle action buttons too. (react-spectrum: packages/@react-spectrum/s2/test/ActionButtonGroup.test.tsx: "can disable all buttons from the group")
- Given a Spectrum 2 ActionButtonGroup without a group-level isDisabled, when one child ActionButton is given isDisabled individually, then only that button is disabled while its sibling buttons remain enabled — per-button disabled state is independent of the group when the group itself isn't disabled. (react-spectrum: packages/@react-spectrum/s2/test/ActionButtonGroup.test.tsx: "can set disable individually")
- Given a Spectrum 2 ActionButtonGroup rendered with isDisabled at the group level, when one child ActionButton explicitly sets isDisabled={false}, then that button overrides the group and stays enabled while its siblings (which don't override) remain disabled — an individual button's explicit isDisabled can override the group's disabled state. (react-spectrum: packages/@react-spectrum/s2/test/ActionButtonGroup.test.tsx: "can override the group disable")

## toolbar

### Roles and attributes

- Given a Toolbar (react-aria-components) wrapping three Button children, when it renders, then it exposes role="toolbar", renders all three buttons, and applies the default react-aria-Toolbar class. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "renders")
- Given a Toolbar (react-aria-components) rendered with a custom className and a data-testid prop, when it renders, then the toolbar element uses the custom class name (overriding the default) and forwards the data-testid attribute. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "renders a custom classname and data-attributes")
- Given a Toolbar (react-aria-components) nested in a ToolbarContext.Provider with a slots map keyed test, when the toolbar is given slot="test", then it renders with slot="test" and picks up the slot-provided aria-label of "test label". (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports slots")
- Given a Toolbar (react-aria-components) containing <hr> elements between buttons, when it renders in horizontal orientation, then each <hr> is exposed with role="separator" (two separators found). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "renders dividers")
- Given the same Toolbar (react-aria-components) with <hr> dividers re-rendered with orientation="vertical", when it renders, then the <hr> elements still expose role="separator" (two separators found) regardless of orientation. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "renders dividers")
- Given a Toolbar (react-aria-components) with only aria-label="Toolbar aria-label" set, when it renders, then the element is queryable by that label text and does not carry an aria-labelledby attribute. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "sets aria-label")
- Given a second Toolbar (react-aria-components) given both aria-label="Toolbar aria-label 2" and aria-labelledby="toolbar-id" (an id that does not match any rendered element), when it renders, then the element is queryable by its aria-label text and still does not end up with an aria-labelledby attribute on the DOM node. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "sets aria-label")
- Given a Toolbar (Spectrum v3 layer) wrapping an ActionGroup whose items have keys and text children, when rendered, then each item renders as a button (three buttons found by role="button"). (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "renders action buttons for items with keys and children")
- Given a Toolbar (Spectrum v3) with default (horizontal) orientation containing multiple ActionGroups separated by Divider components, when rendered, then two separator elements appear and each carries aria-orientation="vertical". (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "renders dividers")
- Given the same Toolbar/Divider structure (Spectrum v3) re-rendered with orientation="vertical" on the Toolbar, when rendered, then the separator elements no longer carry an aria-orientation attribute. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "renders dividers")
- Given two Toolbars (Spectrum v3), one passed only aria-label and one passed both aria-label and aria-labelledby, when queried by accessible label text, then each toolbar is found by its aria-label value and does not also expose an aria-labelledby attribute (aria-label wins/aria-labelledby is not simultaneously set). (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "sets aria-label")

### Keyboard

- Given a horizontally-oriented Toolbar (react-aria-components) containing two nested toolbars ("Align text" and "Zoom") separated by an <hr> divider, when the first nested toolbar item has focus and ArrowRight is pressed, then focus moves to the next button (Align center), and when ArrowDown is pressed instead, then focus does not move (no-op in horizontal orientation). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation")
- Given the same horizontal nested-toolbar layout (react-aria-components), when ArrowRight is pressed repeatedly from the last item of the "Align text" group, then focus crosses the <hr> divider into the first item (Zoom in) of the next nested toolbar group, and ArrowLeft moves focus back across the divider into the previous group's last item (Align right). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation")
- Given focus is on a button inside the horizontal toolbar (react-aria-components), when ArrowLeft is pressed, then focus moves to the previous button, and when ArrowUp is pressed instead, then focus does not move (no-op in horizontal orientation). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation")
- Given focus is on the first item of the horizontal toolbar (Align left) or the last item of the last group (Zoom out, reached via pointer click), when ArrowLeft or ArrowRight respectively is pressed at that boundary, then focus does not wrap around to the opposite end of the collection. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation")
- Given focus is on a toolbar button (react-aria-components), when a non-navigation key such as Enter is pressed, then focus does not move away from the current button. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation")
- Given a vertically-oriented Toolbar (react-aria-components, orientation="vertical") with the same nested "Align text"/"Zoom" groups, when ArrowDown is pressed, then focus moves to the next button (Align center), and when ArrowRight is pressed instead, then focus does not move (no-op in vertical orientation). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation with orientation vertical")
- Given the same vertical nested-toolbar layout (react-aria-components), when ArrowDown is pressed repeatedly from the last item of the "Align text" group, then focus crosses the <hr> divider into the first item (Zoom in) of the next group, and ArrowUp moves focus back across the divider to the previous group's last item (Align right). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation with orientation vertical")
- Given focus is on a button inside the vertical toolbar (react-aria-components), when ArrowUp is pressed, then focus moves to the previous button, and when ArrowLeft is pressed instead, then focus does not move (no-op in vertical orientation). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation with orientation vertical")
- Given focus is on the first item (Align left) or the pointer-focused last item (Zoom out) of the vertical toolbar (react-aria-components), when ArrowUp or ArrowDown respectively is pressed at that boundary, then focus does not wrap around to the opposite end of the collection. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation with orientation vertical")
- Given focus is on a vertical toolbar button (react-aria-components), when a non-navigation key such as Enter is pressed, then focus does not move away from the current button. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation with orientation vertical")
- Given a story-driven toolbar (react-aria-components) containing mixed interactive children (a text input before the toolbar, bold/underline/italic buttons, a checkbox, and a link), when Tab is pressed from the preceding input then ArrowRight is pressed repeatedly, then focus advances in sequence across the mixed control types (button to button to checkbox to link) as a single roving-tabindex group. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports all the aria example children")
- Given focus has reached the last item (the Help link) in that mixed-children toolbar (react-aria-components), when ArrowRight is pressed again, then focus stays on the last item and does not wrap. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports all the aria example children")
- Given a Toolbar (Spectrum v3) containing an ActionGroup with an onAction handler and, after a divider, a single-selection ActionGroup with onSelectionChange, when the first item is focused via Tab and Enter is pressed, then onAction fires exactly once with the pressed item's key ("alignleft"). (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "support actions and selection")
- Given that same Toolbar (Spectrum v3), when ArrowRight is pressed three times to move focus into the selection ActionGroup onto the "grid" item and Enter is pressed, then onAction is not called (selection-mode ActionGroup does not fire onAction) but onSelectionChange fires exactly once. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "support actions and selection")
- Given a horizontal Toolbar (Spectrum v3) with two ActionGroups (labeled "Align text" and "Zoom") separated by a Divider, flanked by external ActionButtons ("Before"/"After"), when Tab is pressed to enter the toolbar, then focus lands on the first action button ("Align left") — roving tabindex places the single stop at the first item. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation")
- Given focus on "Align left" in that horizontal toolbar (Spectrum v3), when ArrowRight is pressed, then focus moves to "Align center" (arrow-right navigates to the next action button within the group). (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation")
- Given focus on "Align center" (Spectrum v3), when ArrowDown is pressed twice, then focus moves to "Align right" then wraps to "Align left" — the vertical arrow (perpendicular to the horizontal toolbar's axis) is handled by the child ActionGroup itself and is allowed to wrap, unlike the toolbar's own horizontal axis key. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation")
- Given focus on "Align left" (Spectrum v3), when ArrowUp is pressed, then focus moves to "Align right" (child-controlled perpendicular-axis wrap in the reverse direction). (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation")
- Given focus on "Align right" (last item of the first ActionGroup, Spectrum v3) with a Divider and a second ActionGroup ("Zoom") following, when ArrowRight is pressed, then focus crosses the divider and lands on "Zoom in" (arrow keys navigate across dividers between sibling action groups in the same toolbar). (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation")
- Given focus on "Zoom in" (Spectrum v3), when ArrowLeft is pressed, then focus moves back across the divider to "Align right" in the previous ActionGroup. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation")
- Given focus on "Align right" inside the toolbar (Spectrum v3) with an "After" ActionButton following it outside the toolbar, when Tab is pressed (simulated by manually firing keyDown/keyUp on 'Tab' and then manually focusing the After button, as a workaround because jsdom does not natively take over tabbing out of the toolbar once focusFirst()/focusLast() has been invoked), then focus exits the toolbar to the "After" button. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation")
- Given focus on "After" outside the toolbar (Spectrum v3), when Shift+Tab is pressed, then focus re-enters the toolbar at the last focused action button ("Align right"), demonstrating roving-tabindex memory of the last-focused item. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation")
- Given focus on "Align right" (Spectrum v3) inside the toolbar with a "Before" ActionButton preceding it, when Shift+Tab is pressed (again via manual keyDown/keyUp plus manual focus() as a jsdom workaround) then Tab is pressed again, then focus exits backward to "Before" and forward-tabbing back in restores focus to "Align right" (the same last-focused item). (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation")
- Given focus on "Align right" (Spectrum v3), when ArrowLeft is pressed twice, then focus moves to "Align center" then to "Align left" (reverse-direction horizontal navigation along the toolbar axis). (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation")
- Given focus blurred off "Align left" (Spectrum v3, via fireEvent.blur) and moved to the external "Before" button, when Tab is pressed, then focus re-enters the toolbar at the last focused action button ("Align left") rather than the first item — blur alone does not reset the roving-tabindex memory. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation")
- Given focus on "Align left" (the first item of its ActionGroup within the toolbar, Spectrum v3), when ArrowLeft is pressed, then focus stays on "Align left" — arrow keys do not wrap across the toolbar-level collection of grouped items at its start boundary. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation")
- Given "Zoom out" (the last item of the last ActionGroup, Spectrum v3) is focused via pointer click, when ArrowRight is pressed, then focus remains on "Zoom out" — arrow keys do not wrap past the end of the toolbar's collection either. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation")
- Given focus on "Zoom out" (Spectrum v3), when Enter is pressed, then focus remains on "Zoom out" — non-navigation keys do not move focus within the toolbar. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation")
- Given a vertically-oriented Toolbar (Spectrum v3, orientation="vertical") with the same two ActionGroups/divider structure and flanking ActionButtons, when Tab is pressed to enter the toolbar, then focus lands on the first action button ("Align left"). (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation with orientation vertical")
- Given focus on "Align left" in the vertical toolbar (Spectrum v3), when ArrowDown is pressed, then focus moves to "Align center" (down arrow is the primary navigation axis for a vertical toolbar). (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation with orientation vertical")
- Given focus on "Align center" in the vertical toolbar (Spectrum v3), when ArrowRight is pressed twice, then focus moves to "Align right" then wraps to "Align left" — the perpendicular-axis arrow key (right) is handled by the child ActionGroup and is allowed to wrap in a vertical toolbar. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation with orientation vertical")
- Given focus on "Align left" in the vertical toolbar (Spectrum v3), when ArrowLeft is pressed, then focus moves to "Align right" (child-controlled perpendicular-axis wrap in reverse). (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation with orientation vertical")
- Given focus on "Align right" (last item, with a Divider and a following "Zoom" ActionGroup) in the vertical toolbar (Spectrum v3), when ArrowDown is pressed, then focus crosses the divider to "Zoom in" (arrow keys traverse dividers along the toolbar's primary vertical axis). (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation with orientation vertical")
- Given focus on "Zoom in" in the vertical toolbar (Spectrum v3), when ArrowUp is pressed, then focus moves back across the divider to "Align right". (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation with orientation vertical")
- Given focus on "Align right" inside the vertical toolbar (Spectrum v3) with "After" outside it, when Tab is pressed (via the same manual keyDown/keyUp + manual focus() jsdom workaround), then focus exits to "After". (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation with orientation vertical")
- Given focus on "After" (Spectrum v3), when Shift+Tab is pressed, then focus re-enters the vertical toolbar at the last-focused item ("Align right"). (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation with orientation vertical")
- Given focus on "Align right" with "Before" preceding the toolbar (Spectrum v3), when Shift+Tab then Tab are pressed (manual event workaround as above), then focus exits to "Before" and re-entering restores focus to "Align right". (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation with orientation vertical")
- Given focus on "Align right" in the vertical toolbar (Spectrum v3), when ArrowUp is pressed, then focus moves to "Align center" (reverse primary-axis navigation). (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation with orientation vertical")
- Given focus blurred off "Align left" and moved to "Before" (Spectrum v3), when Tab is pressed, then focus re-enters the vertical toolbar at the last-focused item ("Align left"), not the first item. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation with orientation vertical")
- Given focus on "Align left" (first item) in the vertical toolbar (Spectrum v3), when ArrowUp is pressed, then focus stays on "Align left" — no wrap at the start boundary of the vertical toolbar's collection. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation with orientation vertical")
- Given "Zoom out" (last item, Spectrum v3) focused via pointer click, when ArrowDown is pressed, then focus remains on "Zoom out" — no wrap at the end boundary. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation with orientation vertical")
- Given focus on "Zoom out" in the vertical toolbar (Spectrum v3), when Enter is pressed, then focus remains on "Zoom out" — non-navigation keys do not move focus. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation with orientation vertical")

### Pointer (mouse, touch, pen, virtual click from screen readers)

- Given a horizontal toolbar (Spectrum v3) with "Align left"/"Zoom" ActionGroups, when "Zoom out" is clicked with the pointer, then subsequent ArrowRight keeps focus on "Zoom out" (click sets focus/active item used as the arrow-key wrap boundary check). (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation")
- Given a vertical toolbar (Spectrum v3) with the same structure, when "Zoom out" is clicked with the pointer, then subsequent ArrowDown keeps focus on "Zoom out" (click-set focus used to verify the vertical toolbar's end-of-collection wrap boundary). (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation with orientation vertical")

### Focus management

- Given a Toolbar (react-aria-components) rendered with a forwarded ref, when it mounts, then the ref resolves to the toolbar's root DOM element. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "renders")
- Given a button positioned before a horizontal Toolbar (react-aria-components) containing nested toolbar groups, when Tab is pressed from that preceding button, then focus enters the toolbar landing on its first focusable item (Align left) as a single tab stop. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation")
- Given focus is on the last item of a horizontal toolbar (react-aria-components), when Tab is pressed, then focus exits the toolbar entirely to the next focusable element after it ("After" button); this is verified by manually firing keyDown/keyUp for Tab and then manually focusing the "After"/"Before" buttons, since jsdom does not correctly take over native tab-out-of-toolbar behavior once focusFirst()/focusLast() has been called. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation")
- Given a horizontal toolbar (react-aria-components) was previously focused and then focus moved away, when Shift+Tab is pressed to move backward into the toolbar, then focus lands on the last-focused button (roving tabindex remembers position) rather than the first item. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation")
- Given a toolbar item (react-aria-components) was focused and then blurred (via fireEvent.blur) while focus moves elsewhere, when Tab is subsequently pressed to re-enter the toolbar, then focus returns to the last-focused item rather than the first item. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation")
- Given a button before a vertical Toolbar (react-aria-components) containing nested toolbar groups, when Tab is pressed, then focus enters the toolbar landing on its first focusable item (Align left) as a single tab stop. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation with orientation vertical")
- Given focus is on the last item of a vertical toolbar (react-aria-components), when Tab is pressed, then focus exits the toolbar to the next focusable element after it, verified via the same manual keyDown/keyUp workaround because jsdom does not natively hand off tab-out-of-toolbar focus once focusFirst()/focusLast() has run. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation with orientation vertical")
- Given a vertical toolbar (react-aria-components) previously focused and then focus moved away, when Shift+Tab is pressed to move backward into the toolbar, then focus lands on the last-focused button rather than the first item. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation with orientation vertical")
- Given a vertical toolbar item (react-aria-components) was focused and then blurred while focus moves elsewhere, when Tab is subsequently pressed to re-enter the toolbar, then focus returns to the last-focused item rather than the first item. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation with orientation vertical")
- Given a horizontal toolbar (Spectrum v3) embedded between two external ActionButtons, when Tab is used to enter/exit the toolbar and Shift+Tab to re-enter (using manual keyDown/keyUp firing plus manual .focus() calls to work around jsdom's incomplete native tab-order handling once focusFirst()/focusLast() has run), then the toolbar consistently restores focus to the last-focused action button rather than always the first, both from tabbing forward past it and shift-tabbing back into it. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation")
- Given focus previously on "Align left" (Spectrum v3) is blurred via fireEvent.blur (not moved elsewhere by user interaction) and focus is placed on an external button, when Tab re-enters the toolbar, then focus returns to "Align left" (the roving-tabindex "last focused" record survives a plain blur event). (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation")
- Given the same setup under orientation="vertical" (Spectrum v3), when Tab is used to exit/enter and Shift+Tab to re-enter the toolbar (same manual-event jsdom workaround), then focus restoration to the last-focused item behaves identically to the horizontal case. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation with orientation vertical")
- Given focus on "Align left" is blurred via fireEvent.blur in the vertical toolbar (Spectrum v3) and an external button is focused, when Tab re-enters, then focus returns to "Align left" rather than the first item by default order. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports keyboard navigation with orientation vertical")

### State and controlled/uncontrolled

- Given an ActionGroup within a Toolbar (Spectrum v3) configured with selectionMode="single" and a controlled-style onSelectionChange callback, when an item is activated via Enter after navigating to it, then onSelectionChange is called exactly once and no onAction fires for that group. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "support actions and selection")

### RTL and i18n

- Given a horizontal Toolbar (react-aria-components) rendered inside an I18nProvider with locale="he-IL" (RTL), when ArrowLeft is pressed, then focus moves to the visually-next button (Align center), mirroring the arrow-key direction for RTL, and ArrowUp is a no-op. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports RTL")
- Given the same RTL horizontal toolbar (react-aria-components), when ArrowRight is pressed, then focus moves to the visually-previous button (the mirrored "previous" direction), and ArrowDown is a no-op. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports RTL")
- Given a vertically-oriented Toolbar (react-aria-components) rendered inside an I18nProvider with locale="he-IL" (RTL), when ArrowDown is pressed, then focus moves to the next button (Align center) exactly as in LTR (vertical navigation is not mirrored by RTL), and ArrowUp moves back to the previous button (Align left). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports RTL with orientation vertical")
- Given the same RTL vertical toolbar (react-aria-components), when ArrowLeft or ArrowRight is pressed, then focus does not move (both are no-ops in vertical orientation regardless of text direction). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports RTL with orientation vertical")
- Given a Toolbar (Spectrum v3) with two ActionGroups rendered under the he-IL (Hebrew, RTL) locale via the Provider, when Tab is pressed to enter, then focus lands on "Align left" as the visually-first item per the mirrored layout. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports RTL")
- Given that RTL toolbar (Spectrum v3), when ArrowLeft is pressed, then focus moves to "Align center" — ArrowLeft is mirrored to mean "next" in RTL, opposite of its LTR meaning. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports RTL")
- Given that RTL toolbar (Spectrum v3), when ArrowUp then ArrowDown are pressed, then focus moves to "Align left" then back to "Align center" — the perpendicular axis (up/down) is unaffected by RTL mirroring and retains its normal direction. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports RTL")
- Given that RTL toolbar (Spectrum v3) with focus moved via ArrowLeft to "Align left" edge, when ArrowRight is pressed, then focus moves to "Align center" — ArrowRight is mirrored to mean "previous" in RTL. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/actiongroup/Toolbar.test.tsx: "supports RTL")

### Notes and edge cases
