- Given the RAC RadioGroup rendered isRequired with a FieldError inside a native form, when rendered unchecked, then neither the group nor any radio is marked invalid and each radio input has the required attribute (not aria-required); when the form's checkValidity is invoked, then the group gains aria-describedby with "Constraints not satisfied" and data-invalid, and focus moves to the first radio; when that radio is then clicked, then all invalid markers clear. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/RadioGroup.test.js: "supports validation errors")
- Given the same required RadioGroup/FieldError setup but with the last radio individually disabled, when the form's checkValidity is invoked, then the group still gains aria-describedby with "Constraints not satisfied" and focus still moves to the first, enabled radio; when that radio is then clicked, then the invalid state clears, showing a disabled trailing radio does not interfere with required-group validation or focus targeting. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/RadioGroup.test.js: "supports validation errors when last radio is disabled")
- Given the RAC RadioGroup rendered with a form="test" prop, when inspected, then every radio input carries a form attribute of "test". (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/RadioGroup.test.js: "should support form prop")

### SSR and hydration

- Given a Spectrum v3 RadioGroup with two Radio children rendered through the SSR test harness, when server-rendering `<RadioGroup label="radio"><Radio>One</Radio><Radio>Two</Radio></RadioGroup>`, then it renders without errors. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/radio/Radio.ssr.test.js: "should render without errors")

### Notes and edge cases

- Given a Spectrum v3 RadioGroup with a data-testid prop, when rendered, then the group carries that attribute. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/radio/Radio.test.js: "v3 RadioGroup supports custom props")
- Given a Spectrum v3 Radio given a data-testid prop, when rendered, then that radio's input carries the attribute. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/radio/Radio.test.js: "v3 Radios supports custom props")
- Given a Spectrum v3 RadioGroup with isReadOnly passed to an individual Radio instead of the group, when rendered, then no radio input receives a readonly attribute and clicking that radio still fires the group's onChange, since read-only is a group-level concept only. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/radio/Radio.test.js: "$Name individual radios cannot be readonly")
- Given the RAC RadioGroup and its Radio/RadioField children rendered with no custom classes, when inspected, then the group has class "react-aria-RadioGroup" with aria-labelledby resolving to its Label text, and each radio root carries the default "react-aria-Radio" or "react-aria-RadioField" class. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/RadioGroup.test.js: "should render a radio group with default classes")
- Given the RAC RadioGroup and its Radio/RadioField children rendered with custom className strings on the group and on each radio, when inspected, then the group and each radio root use the custom classes instead of the defaults. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/RadioGroup.test.js: "should render a radio group with custom classes")
- Given the RAC RadioGroup and its Radio/RadioField children rendered with arbitrary DOM props (data-foo on the group, data-test on each radio), when inspected, then the group and each radio root carry those attributes. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/RadioGroup.test.js: "should support DOM props")
- Given the RAC RadioGroup and its Radio/RadioField children rendered with render functions supplying custom root elements for both the group and each radio, when inspected, then those custom roots receive the computed props including a data-custom attribute. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/RadioGroup.test.js: "should support custom render function")
- Given the RAC RadioGroup rendered isRequired with a render-prop function as its own children keyed on isRequired, when inspected, then the rendered Label element reflects data-required="true" supplied by the render prop. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/RadioGroup.test.js: "should support render props")
- Given the RAC RadioGroup rendered inside nested context providers mapping a group slot to extra props (an aria-label) and an item context supplying data-test, when the group is given slot="test", then the group carries slot="test" and the mapped aria-label, and every radio root carries the item context's data-test. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/RadioGroup.test.js: "should support slot")
- Given the RAC RadioGroup rendered with a data-testid on the group and on one Radio, when inspected, then the Radio's data-testid lands on its outer label (or div for RadioField) element rather than the input, and the group's data-testid lands on the radiogroup element itself. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/RadioGroup.test.js: "should render data- attributes only on the outer Radio element or RadioGroup")
- Given the RAC RadioGroup rendered with a ref on the group and a ref on a Radio, when inspected, then the group ref points at the radiogroup element and the radio ref points at its outer label element. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/RadioGroup.test.js: "should support refs")
- Given the RAC RadioGroup rendered with an inputRef on a Radio, when inspected, then the ref points at the native radio input element. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/RadioGroup.test.js: "should support input ref")
- Given the RAC RadioGroup rendered with a callback inputRef returning a cleanup function, when the component mounts then unmounts, then the callback fires with the input element on mount and its returned cleanup function runs once on unmount. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/RadioGroup.test.js: "should support callback ref")
- Given the RAC RadioGroup rendered with both a context-level inputRef and a prop-level inputRef on a Radio, when inspected, then both refs are populated with the same native radio input element. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/RadioGroup.test.js: "should support and merge input ref on context")

## switch

### Roles and attributes

- Given a Spectrum v3 Switch rendered with an aria-label and no visible text, when queried by role="switch", then the switch carries that aria-label. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/switch/Switch.test.js: "$Name can have a non-visible label")
- Given a Spectrum v3 Switch rendered next to an external labelling span with aria-labelledby, when queried by role="switch", then the switch exposes that aria-labelledby value. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/switch/Switch.test.js: "$Name supports aria-labelledby")
- Given a Spectrum v3 Switch rendered next to an external describing span with aria-describedby, when queried by role="switch", then the switch exposes that aria-describedby value. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/switch/Switch.test.js: "$Name supports aria-describedby")
- Given the RAC Switch and SwitchField layers rendered isReadOnly with a className function keyed on isReadOnly, when inspected, then the switch has aria-readonly="true" and the label and its wrapper carry data-readonly and the readonly class. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Switch.test.js: "should support read only state")
- Given the RAC SwitchField layer rendered with a description prop, when inspected, then the switch has aria-describedby pointing at an element whose text content is the description. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Switch.test.js: "should support description")
- Given the RAC SwitchField layer rendered without a description, when the description prop is added then removed via rerender, then aria-describedby is added on the switch and then removed again. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Switch.test.js: "should update aria-describedby when changing description prop")

### Keyboard

- Given the RAC Switch and SwitchField layers rendered with a className function keyed on isPressed and onPress/onClick handlers, when tabbing to the switch and pressing then releasing Space, then data-pressed and the pressed class appear while Space is held and clear on release, and onPress/onClick each fire once. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Switch.test.js: "should support press state with keyboard")
- Given the RAC Switch layer rendered inside a native form with a submit button, when the switch is clicked to focus it and Enter is pressed, then the form submits once via implicit submission. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Switch.test.js: "should support implicit form submission from a focused switch on Enter")

### Pointer (mouse, touch, pen, virtual click from screen readers)

- Given the RAC Switch and SwitchField layers rendered with onHoverStart/onHoverChange/onHoverEnd handlers and a className function keyed on isHovered, when hovering then unhovering the label, then data-hovered and the hover class toggle on and off and each hover callback fires the expected number of times. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Switch.test.js: "should support hover")
- Given the RAC Switch and SwitchField layers rendered with a className function keyed on isPressed and onPress/onClick/onClickCapture handlers, when pressing then releasing the mouse button on the label, then data-pressed and the pressed class toggle across the press and each handler fires once. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Switch.test.js: "should support press state")

### Focus management

- Given a Spectrum v3 Switch rendered with excludeFromTabOrder, when queried by role="switch", then the switch input has tabIndex="-1". (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/switch/Switch.test.js: "$Name supports excludeFromTabOrder")
- Given the RAC Switch and SwitchField layers rendered with a className function keyed on isFocusVisible, when tabbing onto then away from the switch, then the label gains data-focus-visible and the focus class while keyboard-focused and loses both once focus moves on. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Switch.test.js: "should support focus ring")
- Given the RAC Switch and SwitchField layers rendered with onFocus/onFocusChange/onBlur handlers next to another focusable button, when tabbing onto the switch then tabbing to the button, then onFocus and onFocusChange(true) fire once on entry and onBlur and onFocusChange(false) fire once on exit. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Switch.test.js: "should support focus events")

### State and controlled/uncontrolled

- Given a Spectrum v3 Switch (plain and isEmphasized variants) rendered unchecked by default, when clicked twice, then it becomes checked with onChange called with true, then unchecked with onChange called with false. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/switch/Switch.test.js: "$Name default unchecked can be checked")
- Given a Spectrum v3 Switch (plain and isEmphasized variants) rendered with defaultSelected and value="newsletter", when inspected then clicked, then the native input's value is "newsletter" and starts checked, and clicking unchecks it while onChange fires with false. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/switch/Switch.test.js: "$Name can be default checked")
- Given a Spectrum v3 Switch (plain and isEmphasized variants) rendered controlled with isSelected=true, when clicked, then it remains checked because the controlled prop does not change, while onChange still fires with false. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/switch/Switch.test.js: "$Name can be controlled checked")
- Given a Spectrum v3 Switch (plain and isEmphasized variants) rendered controlled with isSelected=false, when clicked, then it remains unchecked because the controlled prop does not change, while onChange still fires with true. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/switch/Switch.test.js: "$Name can be controlled unchecked")
- Given a Spectrum v3 Switch (plain and isEmphasized variants) rendered with isDisabled, when clicked, then it stays unchecked and onChange is never called. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/switch/Switch.test.js: "$Name can be disabled")
- Given a Spectrum v3 Switch rendered controlled with isSelected=true and isReadOnly, when clicked, then it stays checked and onChange is never called. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/switch/Switch.test.js: "$Name supports readOnly")
- Given the RAC Switch and SwitchField layers rendered with isDisabled and a className function keyed on isDisabled, when inspected, then the switch is disabled and the label and its wrapper carry data-disabled="true" and the disabled class. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Switch.test.js: "should support disabled state")
- Given the RAC Switch and SwitchField layers rendered uncontrolled with onChange and a className function keyed on isSelected, when clicked twice, then checked state, data-selected, and the selected class toggle on then off, and onChange is called with true then false. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Switch.test.js: "should support selected state")

### Forms (name, value, validation, form reset)

- Given a Spectrum v3 Switch inside a native form with a reset button, when the switch is clicked to check it and the form is then reset, then the switch reverts to unchecked. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/switch/Switch.test.js: "supports form reset")
- Given React 19's useActionState driving a form action prop (only run when React version is 19 or newer) with a Switch bound to defaultSelected from the action state, when the submit button runs the form action, then the switch becomes checked to reflect the updated defaultSelected value. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/switch/Switch.test.js: "resets to defaultSelected when submitting form action")
- Given the RAC Switch layer rendered with a form="test" prop, when inspected, then the switch input carries a form attribute of "test". (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Switch.test.js: "should support form prop")
- Given the RAC SwitchField layer rendered isRequired inside a Form with a className function keyed on isRequired, when inspected, then the switch has the required attribute and the label and its parent field carry data-required="true" and the required class. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Switch.test.js: "should support required state")
- Given the same required RAC SwitchField, when the form's requestSubmit is called without turning on the switch, then the switch, label, and field all gain aria-invalid/data-invalid and the switch gains aria-describedby referencing a "Constraints not satisfied" message; when the label is then clicked to turn the switch on, then all invalid and aria-describedby markers are cleared. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Switch.test.js: "should support required state")

### SSR and hydration

- Given a Spectrum v3 Switch rendered through the SSR test harness, when server-rendering `<Switch>Switching</Switch>`, then it renders without errors. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/switch/Switch.ssr.test.js: "should render without errors")

### Notes and edge cases

- Given a Spectrum v3 Switch rendered with a data-testid prop, when queried by test id, then the rendered label element (the switch's root, not its input) carries the extra DOM prop, since attributes like aria-hidden and other passthrough props land on the outer label rather than the switch input for this component. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/switch/Switch.test.js: "$Name supports additional props")
- Given the RAC Switch and SwitchField layers rendered with no custom class, when inspected, then the wrapper element carries the default class "react-aria-Switch" or "react-aria-SwitchField" respectively. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Switch.test.js: "should render a s with default class")
- Given the RAC Switch and SwitchField layers rendered with a custom className string, when inspected, then the wrapper element's class is replaced by that custom class. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Switch.test.js: "should render a s with custom class")
- Given the RAC Switch and SwitchField layers rendered with a data-foo prop, when inspected, then the wrapper label receives that attribute but the switch input itself does not. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Switch.test.js: "should support data- props on label element")
- Given the RAC Switch and SwitchField layers rendered with a custom render function supplying the root element, when inspected, then the custom root element receives the computed props including a data-custom attribute. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Switch.test.js: "should support custom render function")
- Given the RAC Switch and SwitchField layers rendered with a render-prop function as children keyed on isSelected, when the switch is clicked, then the rendered text updates from "Off" to "On" as it becomes checked. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Switch.test.js: "should support render props")
- Given the RAC Switch and SwitchField layers rendered inside a context provider that maps a named slot to extra props (an aria-label), when the component is given slot="test", then the wrapper carries slot="test" and the switch receives the slot's aria-label. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Switch.test.js: "should support slot")
- Given the RAC Switch and SwitchField layers rendered with a data-testid prop, when inspected, then exactly one element carries that test id and it is the outer wrapper with the default react-aria class, not the input. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Switch.test.js: "should render data- attributes only on the outer element")
- Given the RAC Switch and SwitchField layers rendered with a ref, when inspected, then the ref points at the root label (or SwitchField wrapper) element. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Switch.test.js: "should support refs")
- Given the RAC Switch and SwitchField layers rendered with an inputRef, when inspected, then the ref points at the native switch input element. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Switch.test.js: "should support input ref")
- Given the RAC Switch and SwitchField layers rendered with a callback inputRef returning a cleanup function, when the component mounts then unmounts, then the callback fires with the input element on mount and its returned cleanup function runs once on unmount. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Switch.test.js: "should support callback ref")
- Given the RAC Switch and SwitchField layers rendered with both a context-level inputRef and a prop-level inputRef, when inspected, then both refs are populated with the same native switch input element. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Switch.test.js: "should support and merge input ref on context")

## tabs

### Roles and attributes

- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs with default horizontal orientation, when rendered, then the tablist has `aria-orientation="horizontal"`, each tab has a `tabindex` and `aria-selected`, the selected tab exposes `aria-controls` pointing at a tabpanel with matching `aria-labelledby`, `role="tabpanel"`, the correct text content, and there is exactly one tabpanel. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "renders properly")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs, when a ref is passed, then the ref resolves to the outer wrapping div containing the tablist. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "attaches a user provided ref to the outer div")
- Given two separate Spectrum v3 Tabs instances rendered together, when inspecting generated tab ids, then the ids do not collide between the two instances. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "does not generate conflicting ids between multiple tabs instances")
- Given a Spectrum v3 TabPanel containing an `<input>`, when the panel has no focusable elements (input disabled), then the tabpanel has no `tabindex`, and when it does contain a focusable input, then the tabpanel has `tabindex="0"`. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "tabpanel should have tabIndex=0 only when there are no focusable elements")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs with an `aria-label` on an individual Item, when queried by label text, then that tab is retrievable and matches the first rendered tab. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "Tabs can be aria-labelled")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs, when custom props like `data-testid` are passed to the parent Tabs element, then they are applied to the rendered root element. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "supports custom props for parent tabs element")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs, when custom props including `data-instance-id` and `id` are passed to tab Items, then `data-instance-id` is forwarded to each rendered tab item but a colliding `id` prop is not applied verbatim. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "supports custom props for tab items")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs configured with link-based tab items (`<LinksExample>`), when rendered, then each tab renders as an `<a>` element with the expected `href`. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "should support tabs as links")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs whose `TabList`/`TabPanels` children are wrapped in fragments (children directly in fragment, leading fragment sibling, middle fragment sibling, trailing fragment sibling, list-fragment-first mismatched positions, panel-fragment-first mismatched positions, fragment with renderer function, fragment with `.map` mapper), when rendered, then the correct number of tabs render in the tablist with proper `aria-orientation`, `tabindex`, `aria-selected`, `aria-controls`, matching tabpanel `aria-labelledby`/`role="tabpanel"`/text content. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "renders fragment with children properly")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs whose `TabList`/`TabPanels` children are wrapped in fragments, when the TabList has a leading fragment containing the first Item, then rendering still produces correctly wired tabs/tabpanel. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "renders beginning fragment sibling properly")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs whose `TabList`/`TabPanels` children are wrapped in fragments, when a fragment wraps only the middle Item, then rendering still produces correctly wired tabs/tabpanel. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "renders middle fragment sibling properly")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs whose `TabList`/`TabPanels` children are wrapped in fragments, when a fragment wraps only the trailing Item, then rendering still produces correctly wired tabs/tabpanel. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "renders ending fragment sibling properly")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs whose TabList fragment placement does not match the TabPanels fragment placement (list wraps the first item in a fragment while panels do not), when rendered, then tabs/tabpanel wiring is still correct. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "renders list and panel fragment siblings in non-matching positions properly, list fragment first")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs whose TabPanels fragment placement does not match the TabList fragment placement (panels wrap the first item in a fragment while list does not), when rendered, then tabs/tabpanel wiring is still correct. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "renders list and panel fragment siblings in non-matching positions properly, panel fragment first")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs using a fragment wrapping a renderer function for both TabList and TabPanels, when rendered from `items`, then tabs/tabpanel wiring is correct. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "renders fragment with renderer properly")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs using a fragment wrapping a `.map()`-produced list of Items for both TabList and TabPanels, when rendered, then tabs/tabpanel wiring is correct. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "renders fragment with mapper properly")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs rendered with default props, when inspecting DOM, then the TabList has class `react-aria-TabList` and `aria-label`, each tab has class `react-aria-Tab`, and the tabpanel has class `react-aria-TabPanel`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "should render tabs with default classes")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs, when custom `className` props are supplied to Tabs/TabList/Tab/TabPanel, then each rendered element carries the custom class instead of the default. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "should render tabs with custom classes")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs, when arbitrary DOM props (`data-test`) are passed to Tabs/TabList/Tab/TabPanel, then each rendered element carries the corresponding `data-test` attribute. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "should support DOM props")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs, when `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, and `aria-details` are passed to Tab, then each tab element carries those exact ARIA attributes. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "should support aria props on the tabs")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs, when a custom `render` function is supplied for Tabs/TabList/Tab/TabPanel, then the custom-rendered elements receive the merged props including a custom `data-custom` attribute. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "should support custom render function")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs, when a Tab's custom `render` function renders an `<a href>` element, then the resulting tab element retains both the `href` and the custom `data-custom` attribute. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "should support custom render function as a link")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs, when Tabs is given a function-as-children render prop, then it receives the current `orientation` and can use it to compute the TabList's `aria-label`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "should support render props")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs, when `isDisabled` is set on the whole Tabs, then the first tab carries `aria-disabled="true"` and a `disabled`-derived class. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "should support disabled state on all tabs")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs, when a specific key is included in `disabledKeys`, then only that tab carries `aria-disabled="true"` and the corresponding class. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "should support disabled state on tab")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs, when `orientation="vertical"` is set, then the tablist has `aria-orientation="vertical"` and a corresponding class, and the Tabs wrapper element gets `data-orientation="vertical"` and a matching class. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "should support orientation")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs, when refs are attached to Tabs, TabList, Tab, and TabPanel, then each ref resolves to the corresponding HTMLElement. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "should support refs")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs rendered via the router-integrated `TabsExample` story with link tabs, when rendered, then each tab is an `<a>` with the expected `href` and the first tab is initially selected. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "should support tabs as links")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs, when a Tab is rendered with its own `aria-label`, then each tab carries that exact `aria-label`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "should render tab with aria-label")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs nested inside another Tabs' TabPanel, when rendered, then `getAllByRole('tablist')`/`tabpanel` resolve independently for the outer and inner tab sets with the correct tab counts and text content (uses a MutationObserver-based `useHasTabbableChild` internally, so the test awaits a tick for it to fire and avoid an act() warning). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "supports nested tabs")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs, when a `TabPanels` element is given `aria-label`, then the rendered tabpanels container carries that `aria-label`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "should support tabpanels with aria-labels")

### Keyboard

- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs with horizontal orientation, when ArrowRight/ArrowLeft are pressed on a focused tab, then selection moves to the next/previous tab and the keydown event's `defaultPrevented` is true, but ArrowUp/ArrowDown do not change selection and leave `defaultPrevented` false. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "allows user to change tab item select via arrow keys with horizontal tabs")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs with vertical orientation, when ArrowDown/ArrowUp are pressed, then selection moves to next/previous tab, and ArrowLeft/ArrowRight also change selection despite the vertical orientation. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "allows user to change tab item select via arrow keys with vertical tabs")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs with horizontal orientation, when ArrowLeft is pressed on the first tab, then selection wraps to the last tab, and when ArrowRight is pressed on the last tab, then selection wraps back to the first tab. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "wraps focus from first to last/last to first item")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs with vertical orientation, when End is pressed, then the last tab is selected, and when Home is pressed, then the first tab is selected. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "select last item via end key / select first item via home key")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs with `keyboardActivation="manual"`, when ArrowRight is pressed, then focus moves to the next tab but `aria-selected` does not change and `onSelectionChange` is not called, and when Enter is pressed on the focused tab, then that tab becomes selected and `onSelectionChange` fires exactly once. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "not select via left / right keys if keyboardActivation is manual, select on enter / spacebar")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs rendered with disabled keys, when ArrowRight is pressed while focus is on a disabled tab, then selection still advances past the disabled tab to the next enabled tab (`onSelectionChange` called with the tab after the disabled one). (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "disabled tabs cannot be keyboard navigated to")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs rendered via `<LinksExample>` link tabs, when clicking a tab and then pressing ArrowRight, then selection moves as expected across link-based tabs. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "should support tabs as links")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs collapsed into a picker button, when clicking the picker to open a listbox and selecting a disabled tab's option, then `onSelectionChange` is not called; when selecting an enabled tab's option afterward, then selection updates and `onSelectionChange` fires with the new key. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "disabled tabs cannot be selected via collapse picker")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs test-utils tester across `it.each` variants of locale/orientation combos (ltr+vertical, rtl+vertical, ltr+horizontal, rtl+horizontal) with disabled keys 'Tab 2' and 'Tab 3', when triggering tab selection via keyboard by index, by tab element, and via mouse (by index and by text), then the tester correctly resolves and selects the right tab and skips disabled ones regardless of orientation/direction. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "$Name should select the correct radio via keyboard regardless of orientation and disabled radios")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs with `isDisabled` set on one Tab, when tabbing in then pressing ArrowRight, then focus/selection skips the disabled tab and lands on the next enabled tab. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "should support isDisabled prop on tab")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs with the first Tab disabled, when tabbing in then pressing ArrowRight, then initial focus goes to the first non-disabled tab and ArrowRight moves to the following tab. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "finds the first non-disabled tab")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs with vertical orientation, when using the test-utils tester parameterized over `it.each` interactionType mouse/keyboard/touch, then triggering each tab in sequence selects it and updates the active tabpanel's `aria-labelledby` regardless of interaction type. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "should support changing the selected tab regardless of interaction type, interactionType: $interactionType ")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs with `keyboardActivation="manual"`, when the keyboard tester moves focus between tabs without pressing Enter/Space, then `aria-selected` does not change and focus moves visibly; when `manualActivation: true` triggers activation on the focused tab, then that tab becomes selected and `onSelectionChange` fires exactly once. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "should support keyboardActivation=manual")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs rendered via the router-integrated `TabsExample` link tabs, when clicking a tab and then pressing ArrowRight via `fireEvent.keyDown`, then selection moves to the next linked tab. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "should support tabs as links")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs with dynamically added/removed tab items and a controlled `selectedKey` tied to state, when tabbing in and pressing ArrowRight then Tab then Enter, then the newly-added tab remains selected and `onSelectionChange` is not called merely by focusing/pressing Enter on an already-selected tab (each Enter/Tab sequence re-verified after add). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "can add tabs and keep the current selected key")
- Given a React Aria Components Tab wrapped in a `TooltipTrigger`, when the tab is hovered, then the associated tooltip becomes visible with the expected text content. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "supports tooltips")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs test-utils tester parameterized over `it.each` interactionType mouse/keyboard with `keyboardActivation="manual"`, when a tab is triggered with `manualActivation: true`, then `onPressStart`, `onPressEnd`, `onPress`, and `onClick` each fire exactly once for that tab. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "should support press events on items when using $interactionType")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs test-utils tester parameterized over `it.each` interactionType mouse/keyboard in a real browser (vitest-browser-react, not jsdom), when triggering the second tab, then it becomes the selected tab. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.browser.test.tsx: "triggers a tab via $interactionType")

### Pointer (mouse, touch, pen, virtual click from screen readers)

- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs with `keyboardActivation="manual"`, when a user clicks a different tab, then that tab is selected, `aria-controls` points to the correct tabpanel, and the tabpanel content/attributes match the clicked tab. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "supports using click to change tab")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs rendered with disabled keys, when a user clicks a disabled tab, then `onSelectionChange` is not called. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "disabled tabs cannot be pressed")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs, when a user clicks the already-selected/current tab, then `onSelectionChange` still fires once with that same key. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "fires onSelectionChange when clicking on the current tab")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs collapsed into a picker button because tabs can't fit horizontally (mocked `getBoundingClientRect` widths), when clicking the picker and selecting an option from the opened listbox, then `onSelectionChange` fires with the selected key and the visible tabpanel updates to match, with the picker's `aria-label`/`aria-labelledby` reflecting the external label and current selection. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "collapses when it can't render all the tabs horizontally")
- Given a Spectrum v3 TabPanel with focusable input elements, when clicking a tab whose panel has no focusable input, then the tabpanel gains `tabindex="0"`, and when clicking back to a tab whose panel has a focusable input, then the tabpanel has no `tabindex`. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "tabpanel should have tabIndex=0 only when there are no focusable elements")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs with per-panel `<input>` elements, when typing into the first panel's input then clicking to switch to the second tab, then the second panel's input does not share the first panel's typed value. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "TabPanel children do not share values between panels")
- Given React Aria Components Tab with hover handlers, when the pointer hovers then unhovers a tab, then `data-hovered`/hover class toggle and `onHoverStart`/`onHoverChange`/`onHoverEnd` fire the expected number of times. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "should support hover")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs with all tabs disabled via `disabledKeys`, when the pointer hovers a disabled tab, then no hover state or hover callbacks fire. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "should not show hover state when item is not interactive")
- Given React Aria Components Tab, when pressed down and released with the mouse, then `data-pressed`/pressed class toggle on press and clear on release. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "should support press state")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs, when `shouldForceMount` is set on TabPanel, then all tabpanels remain mounted in the DOM and inactive ones carry an `inert` attribute that moves to the previously-active panel after clicking a different tab. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "should support shouldForceMount")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs with link tabs wrapped in a `RouterProvider`, when a `mousedown`+`mouseleave`+`mouseenter` sequence occurs without a completed click, then router `navigate` is not called; when a full `mouseup`+`click` sequence occurs, then `navigate` is called exactly once with the tab's href. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "should navigate linked tabs on click rather than mouse down")

### Focus management

- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs with a `defaultSelectedKey` set to a non-first tab, when the user tabs into the component for the first time, then focus lands on the selected tab. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "should focus the selected tab when tabbing in for the first time")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs with `isDisabled` set on the whole Tabs component, when the user tabs into the component, then focus skips the tablist and lands on the tabpanel instead, and clicking a tab afterward is a no-op for focus purposes. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "should not focus any tabs when isDisabled tabbing in for the first time")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs with a disabled key equal to the initially selected key, when the tree rerenders such that only that disabled tab's item remains selectable candidates change, then `onSelectionChange` is called to move selection to the next non-disabled tab as items are pruned. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "finds the first non-disabled tab if the currently selected one is removed")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs where every tab key is disabled, when the user tabs in, then the first tab is nonetheless marked `aria-selected="true"`, `onSelectionChange` fires with the first tab's key, and focus lands on the tabpanel (not a tab). (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "selects first tab if all tabs are disabled")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs, when the `selectedKey` prop is changed programmatically (controlled), then `tabindex` moves from the old selected tab (`-1`) to the new selected tab (`0`) via roving tabindex. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "updates the tab index of the selected tab if programmatically changed")
- Given React Aria Components Tab with focus-visible styling hooks, when tabbing to and away from a tab, then `data-focus-visible`/`data-focused`/focus class toggle appropriately and `onFocus`/`onFocusChange`/`onBlur` fire the expected number of times. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "should support focus ring")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs where all tabs are disabled, when tabbing in, then the tester's selected tab is the first tab and focus lands on the tabpanel instead of any tab. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "selects first tab if all tabs are disabled")

### State and controlled/uncontrolled

- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs collapsed to a picker, when tabs are dynamically added such that they no longer fit, then the tablist is replaced by a picker button reflecting the currently selected tab and its tabpanel `aria-labelledby`; when tabs are removed such that they fit again, then the tablist reappears (also verified across an orientation change to vertical mid-sequence). (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "dynamically collapses and expands on tab addition/subtraction")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs sized to fit all tabs horizontally (mocked `getBoundingClientRect`), when rendered, then no collapse picker button appears and the tablist is used directly. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "doesn't collapse when it can render all the tabs horizontally")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs with an `onSelectionChange` handler and a controlling test-utils tester, when different tabs are triggered, then `aria-selected`/selected class move to the newly selected tab and `onSelectionChange` is called with the corresponding key each time. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "should support selected state")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs, when the currently selected tab changes, then the active tabpanel's `id` updates to match the newly selected tab's key, and `onSelectionChange` is called on each change. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "should update TabPanel ID when current tab is changed")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs with dynamically added/removed items and controlled `selectedKey` state updated by the app's own `onSelectionChange` wrapper, when a tab is added via a button press, then the tabs list grows and the newly selected tab reflects the app-controlled selection; navigation and re-selection continue to work correctly afterward. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "can add tabs and keep the current selected key")

### RTL and i18n

- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs horizontal orientation rendered in an RTL locale, when navigating via the keyboard tester, then arrow key mirroring is applied (moving "forward" through tabs sends `ArrowLeft`, moving "backward" sends `ArrowRight`). (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "allows user to change tab item select via arrow keys with horizontal tabs (rtl)")
- Given Spectrum v3 Tabs test-utils tester parameterized across ltr/rtl and vertical/horizontal combinations with disabled keys, when selecting tabs by keyboard and mouse, then the correct tab is selected in every locale/orientation combination. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/tabs/Tabs.test.js: "$Name should select the correct radio via keyboard regardless of orientation and disabled radios")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs with vertical orientation inside an RTL `I18nProvider` (`ar-AE`), when arrow keys are pressed, then ArrowDown/ArrowUp move selection normally and ArrowLeft/ArrowRight also move selection without RTL mirroring since orientation is vertical. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "allows user to change tab item selection via arrow keys with vertical tabs (rtl)")

### SSR and hydration

- Given a React Aria Components Tabs tree rendered through `testSSR`, when the server-rendered HTML is inspected before hydration, then tabs render with correct text content and the first tabpanel has the correct content and `aria-labelledby`; when hydration completes, then the same assertions still hold against the live DOM. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.ssr.test.js: "should render without errors")

### Notes and edge cases

- Given a React Aria Components Tabs with `TabPanels` given inline `style`, when rendered, then the computed style on the tabpanels container reflects the passed style object. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "should support tabpanels with custom styles")
- Given a React Aria Components `TabPanels` container whose computed `transition` includes `block-size` (mocked `window.getComputedStyle`), when switching tabs, then a `--tab-panel-height` CSS custom property is set on the container to support height-transition animation, working around a jsdom gap where real CSS transition detection isn't otherwise available. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "should detect block-size in transition for TabPanels")
- Given a React Aria Components `TabPanels` container whose computed `transition` includes `inline-size` (mocked `window.getComputedStyle`), when switching tabs, then a `--tab-panel-width` CSS custom property is set on the container. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "should detect inline-size in transition for TabPanels")
- Given React Aria Components Tabs rendered under React 19 wrapped in `<React.Activity mode="hidden">`, when the Activity is hidden, then no tabs are found in the DOM (`queryAllByRole('tab')` returns empty), and when a button toggles the Activity to visible, then all 3 tabs appear — this test only runs when `React.version` starts with '19'. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "supports Activity")
- Given a `MenuTrigger`/`Menu` rendered as a sibling of a `TabList` inside Tabs, when the menu trigger button is opened, then the menu tester reports the expected number of menu options and the menu closes correctly, without interfering with the Tabs tester's tab count. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "supports Menu inside Tabs")
- Given a `Select` rendered as a sibling of a `TabList` inside Tabs, when the select trigger button is opened, then the select tester reports the expected number of options and closes correctly, without interfering with the Tabs tester's tab count. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "supports Select inside Tabs")
- Given a `ComboBox` rendered as a sibling of a `TabList` inside Tabs, when the combobox is opened via keyboard interaction, then the combobox tester reports the expected number of options and closes correctly, without interfering with the Tabs tester's tab count. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "supports ComboBox inside Tabs")
- Given a `DialogTrigger` rendered as a sibling of a `TabList` inside Tabs, when the dialog trigger button is clicked, then a dialog opens containing the expected number of menu items, without interfering with the Tabs tester's tab count. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Tabs.test.js: "supports DialogTrigger inside Tabs")
- Given Spectrum 2 Tabs rendered inside a React Aria Components `DropZone` with a controlled `selectedKey` and a `TabPanel` containing a `Skeleton`+`Text`, when the dropzone is hovered, then no "Maximum update depth exceeded" React error is logged — a regression test guarding against an infinite render loop in this specific composition. (react-spectrum: packages/@react-spectrum/s2/test/Tabs.test.tsx: "should not infinite-loop when inside DropZone with a controlled Tabs + Skeleton + Text")

## accordion

### Roles and attributes

- Given Spectrum v3 Accordion with three Disclosure items, when rendered, then each item's title renders as a `heading` containing a `button` with `aria-expanded`, and the expanded item's button has `aria-controls` pointing to a region element with matching `aria-labelledby`, `role="region"`, and the correct panel text content. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/accordion/Accordion.test.js: "renders properly")
- Given Spectrum v3 Accordion with `isDisabled` set on the whole Accordion, when rendered, then every Disclosure's button carries the `disabled` attribute. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/accordion/Accordion.test.js: "supports isDisabled on Accordion")
- Given Spectrum v3 Accordion with `isDisabled` set on one individual Disclosure, when rendered, then only that Disclosure's button carries the `disabled` attribute while the others do not. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/accordion/Accordion.test.js: "supports isDisabled on individual Disclosures")

### Keyboard

- Given Spectrum v3 Accordion, when Enter is pressed on a focused disclosure button, then the disclosure toggles open (`aria-expanded="true"`, `onExpandedChange` called once), and pressing Enter again toggles it closed (`aria-expanded="false"`, `onExpandedChange` called a second time). (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/accordion/Accordion.test.js: "allows users to open and close disclosure with enter / space key")
- Given Spectrum v3 Accordion with three Disclosure buttons, when Tab/Shift+Tab are pressed repeatedly, then focus moves forward through each disclosure button in order and past the last button out of the accordion, and Shift+Tab from outside returns focus to the last button. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/accordion/Accordion.test.js: "allows users to navigate accordion headers through the tab key")

### Pointer (mouse, touch, pen, virtual click from screen readers)

- Given Spectrum v3 Accordion, when a disclosure button is clicked, then the disclosure expands (`aria-expanded="true"`, `onExpandedChange` called once), and clicking it again collapses it (`aria-expanded="false"`, `onExpandedChange` called a second time). (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/accordion/Accordion.test.js: "toggle accordion on mouse click")
- Given Spectrum v3 Accordion with a Disclosure whose panel contains a text input, when the disclosure's button is clicked to expand it and the input is focused, then typing into the input updates its value correctly. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/accordion/Accordion.test.js: "allows users to type inside disclosures")

### State and controlled/uncontrolled

- Given Spectrum v3 Accordion with `defaultExpandedKeys={['one']}` (uncontrolled), when rendered, then the corresponding disclosure's button starts with `aria-expanded="true"`. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/accordion/Accordion.test.js: "supports defaultExpandedKeys")
- Given Spectrum v3 Accordion with a controlled `expandedKeys={['one']}` prop and `onExpandedChange`, when the expanded disclosure's button is clicked, then `onExpandedChange` is called once but `aria-expanded` remains `true` because the parent does not update the controlled prop. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/accordion/Accordion.test.js: "supports controlled expandedKeys")

### SSR and hydration

- Given a Spectrum v3 Accordion with two Disclosure items rendered through `testSSR`, when the component is server-rendered, then it renders without throwing errors. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/accordion/Accordion.ssr.test.js: "should render without errors")

## collapsible

### Roles and attributes

- Given the bare `useDisclosure` hook in react-aria backed by `useDisclosureState` with default (collapsed) state, when the hook's returned props are inspected, then `buttonProps['aria-expanded']` is `false` and `panelProps['aria-hidden']` is `true`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/disclosure/useDisclosure.test.ts: "should return correct aria attributes when collapsed")
- Given the bare `useDisclosure` hook backed by `useDisclosureState({defaultExpanded: true})`, when the hook's returned props are inspected, then `buttonProps['aria-expanded']` is `true` and `panelProps['aria-hidden']` is `false`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/disclosure/useDisclosure.test.ts: "should return correct aria attributes when expanded")
- Given the bare `useDisclosure` hook, when the returned `buttonProps`/`panelProps` ids are inspected, then `buttonProps['aria-controls']` equals `panelProps.id` and `panelProps['aria-labelledby']` equals `buttonProps.id`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/disclosure/useDisclosure.test.ts: "should set correct IDs for accessibility")
- Given React Aria Components `Disclosure` with default children, when rendered, then the root element carries the `react-aria-Disclosure` class. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Disclosure.test.js: "should render a Disclosure with default class")
- Given React Aria Components `Disclosure`, when a custom `className` is supplied, then the rendered root element carries that class. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Disclosure.test.js: "should render a Disclosure with custom class")
- Given React Aria Components `Disclosure`, when arbitrary DOM props (`data-foo`) are passed, then the rendered root element carries that attribute. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Disclosure.test.js: "should support DOM props")
- Given React Aria Components `Disclosure` and `DisclosurePanel`, when both are given a custom `render` function, then the resulting elements carry the merged custom `data-custom` attribute. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Disclosure.test.js: "should support custom render function")
- Given React Aria Components `Disclosure` with `aria-label` set on its `Heading` and `DisclosurePanel`, when rendered, then the heading element and the panel element each carry their respective `aria-label`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Disclosure.test.js: "should support aria label")
- Given React Aria Components `Disclosure` with `isDisabled`, when rendered, then the disclosure root carries `data-disabled="true"`, the trigger button has the `disabled` attribute and `data-disabled="true"`, and the panel content is not visible. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Disclosure.test.js: "should support disabled state")
- Given React Aria Components `DisclosureGroup` with default children, when rendered, then the root element carries the `react-aria-DisclosureGroup` class. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Disclosure.test.js: "should render a DisclosureGroup with default class")
- Given React Aria Components `DisclosureGroup` with `isDisabled`, when rendered, then every child Disclosure's trigger button carries `data-disabled="true"` and the `disabled` attribute. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Disclosure.test.js: "should disable all Disclosures when DisclosureGroup is disabled")
- Given a React Aria Components `DisclosureGroup` with two `Disclosure`s SSR'd via `testSSR`, when the server-rendered HTML is inspected, then both trigger buttons and both panels (queried with `role="group", {hidden: true}`) have the expected text content, both before and after hydration. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Disclosure.ssr.test.js: "should render without errors")

### Keyboard

- Given the bare `useDisclosure` hook, when `buttonProps.onPressStart` is invoked with a `pointerType: 'keyboard'` press event, then the disclosure's state becomes expanded. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/disclosure/useDisclosure.test.ts: "should handle expanding on press start event (with keyboard)")
- Given React Aria Components `Disclosure`, when Enter is held down for 4 keydown events and then released (simulating key repeat), then only a single toggle occurs on the full press-and-release, not once per keydown repeat, and `onExpandedChange` fires exactly once per toggle. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Disclosure.test.js: "should not expand or collapse on repeat keydown events")

### Pointer (mouse, touch, pen, virtual click from screen readers)

- Given the bare `useDisclosure` hook, when `buttonProps.onPress` is invoked with a `pointerType: 'mouse'` press event, then the disclosure's state becomes expanded. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/disclosure/useDisclosure.test.ts: "should handle expanding on press event (with mouse)")
- Given the bare `useDisclosure` hook configured with `isDisabled: true`, when `buttonProps.onPress` is invoked with a mouse press event, then the disclosure's state remains collapsed (does not toggle). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/disclosure/useDisclosure.test.ts: "should not toggle when disabled")
- Given React Aria Components `Disclosure`, when the trigger button is clicked, then the disclosure expands (`data-expanded="true"`, panel becomes visible), and clicking it again collapses it (`data-expanded` removed, panel hidden). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Disclosure.test.js: "should toggle expanded state when trigger is clicked")
- Given React Aria Components `Disclosure` whose render-prop children expose `isExpanded`, when the trigger button is clicked, then the button's text content switches from "Expand" to "Collapse" and the panel becomes visible. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Disclosure.test.js: "should support render props")
- Given React Aria Components `Disclosure` with a `MenuTrigger`+`Menu` rendered adjacent to its heading, when the menu trigger button is clicked to open the menu and then the document body is clicked to close it, then the disclosure itself remains collapsed throughout; when the disclosure's own trigger is then clicked, then the disclosure expands/collapses correctly without affecting the menu's visibility. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Disclosure.test.js: "should support interactive elements adjacent to heading")
- Given React Aria Components nested `Disclosure`s (an inner Disclosure inside an outer Disclosure's panel), when the outer trigger is clicked, then the inner disclosure's panel remains hidden; when the inner trigger is clicked, then the inner panel toggles visible/hidden independently of the outer disclosure's state. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Disclosure.test.js: "should support nested Disclosures")
- Given React Aria Components `DisclosureGroup` (default, single-expansion) with two Disclosures, when each trigger is clicked in turn, then only the most recently clicked Disclosure's panel is visible, and clicking the currently-open one again collapses it, leaving both closed. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Disclosure.test.js: "should only allow one Disclosure to be expanded at a time by default")
- Given React Aria Components `DisclosureGroup` with `allowsMultipleExpanded`, when both triggers are clicked in turn, then both panels remain visible simultaneously. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Disclosure.test.js: "should allow multiple Disclosures to be expanded when allowsMultipleExpanded is true")
- Given React Aria Components `DisclosureGroup` with a controlled `expandedKeys` prop driven by external state, when an external button click changes the controlled `expandedKeys` from `['item1']` to `['item2']`, then panel1 becomes hidden and panel2 becomes visible accordingly. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Disclosure.test.js: "should support controlled expandedKeys prop")
- Given nested React Aria Components `DisclosureGroup`s (an inner group inside an outer group's Disclosure panel), when the outer trigger is clicked, then the inner disclosure's panel remains hidden; when the inner trigger is clicked, then the inner panel toggles visible/hidden. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Disclosure.test.js: "should support nested DisclosureGroups")

### Focus management

- Given React Aria Components `Disclosure` with a `isFocusVisibleWithin`-driven className, when the trigger button is tabbed to, then the disclosure root gets `data-focus-visible-within="true"` and the focus class; tabbing away removes both. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Disclosure.test.js: "should support focus ring")

### State and controlled/uncontrolled

- Given the bare `useDisclosureState` hook with no props, when initialized, then `isExpanded` defaults to `false`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-stately/test/disclosure/useDisclosureState.test.ts: "should initialize as not expanded by default")
- Given the bare `useDisclosureState` hook with `defaultExpanded: true`, when initialized, then `isExpanded` is `true`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-stately/test/disclosure/useDisclosureState.test.ts: "should initialize with defaultExpanded when provided")
- Given the bare `useDisclosureState` hook with a controlled `isExpanded` prop, when the prop is changed via rerender from `true` to `false`, then the hook's `isExpanded` reflects the new controlled value. (react-spectrum: packages/react-stately/test/disclosure/useDisclosureState.test.ts: "should allow controlled isExpanded prop")
- Given the bare `useDisclosureState` hook with an `onExpandedChange` callback, when `expand()` is called, then `onExpandedChange` is called with `true`; when `collapse()` is then called, then `onExpandedChange` is called with `false`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-stately/test/disclosure/useDisclosureState.test.ts: "should call onExpandedChange when expanded state changes")
- Given the bare `useDisclosureState` hook (uncontrolled), when `toggle()` is called twice, then `isExpanded` flips to `true` then back to `false`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-stately/test/disclosure/useDisclosureState.test.ts: "should toggle expanded state correctly")
- Given the bare `useDisclosureState` hook with a controlled `isExpanded: false` and `onExpandedChange`, when `expand()` is called, then `isExpanded` stays `false` (state does not change itself) while `onExpandedChange` is still called with `true`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-stately/test/disclosure/useDisclosureState.test.ts: "should not change state when controlled")
- Given the bare `useDisclosureGroupState` hook with no props, when initialized, then `expandedKeys` is an empty set. (react-spectrum: packages/react-stately/test/disclosure/useDisclosureGroupState.test.ts: "should initialize with empty expandedKeys when not provided")
- Given the bare `useDisclosureGroupState` hook with `defaultExpandedKeys: ['item1']`, when initialized, then `expandedKeys` contains `'item1'` but not `'item2'`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-stately/test/disclosure/useDisclosureGroupState.test.ts: "should initialize with defaultExpandedKeys when provided")
- Given the bare `useDisclosureGroupState` hook with `defaultExpandedKeys: ['item1', 'item2']` and `allowsMultipleExpanded: true`, when initialized, then `expandedKeys` contains both `'item1'` and `'item2'`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-stately/test/disclosure/useDisclosureGroupState.test.ts: "should initialize with multiple defaultExpandedKeys when provided, and if allowsMultipleExpanded is true")
- Given the bare `useDisclosureGroupState` hook with a controlled `expandedKeys` prop, when the prop is changed via rerender from `['item1']` to `['item2']`, then `expandedKeys` reflects only the new controlled key. (react-spectrum: packages/react-stately/test/disclosure/useDisclosureGroupState.test.ts: "should allow controlled expandedKeys prop")
- Given the bare `useDisclosureGroupState` hook with `allowsMultipleExpanded` false (default), when `toggleKey('item1')` then `toggleKey('item2')` are called, then only `'item2'` ends up expanded (`'item1'` is closed when a different key opens). (react-spectrum: packages/react-stately/test/disclosure/useDisclosureGroupState.test.ts: "should toggle key correctly when allowsMultipleExpanded is false")
- Given the bare `useDisclosureGroupState` hook with `allowsMultipleExpanded: true`, when `toggleKey('item1')` then `toggleKey('item2')` are called, then both `'item1'` and `'item2'` remain expanded simultaneously. (react-spectrum: packages/react-stately/test/disclosure/useDisclosureGroupState.test.ts: "should toggle key correctly when allowsMultipleExpanded is true")
- Given the bare `useDisclosureGroupState` hook with an `onExpandedChange` callback, when `toggleKey('item1')` is called, then `onExpandedChange` is called with a `Set` containing `'item1'`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-stately/test/disclosure/useDisclosureGroupState.test.ts: "should call onExpandedChange when expanded keys change")
- Given the bare `useDisclosureGroupState` hook with `allowsMultipleExpanded` false (default), when `toggleKey('item1')` and `toggleKey('item2')` are both called within the same act batch, then `expandedKeys.size` is `1` and only `'item2'` is present. (react-spectrum: packages/react-stately/test/disclosure/useDisclosureGroupState.test.ts: "should not expand more than one key when allowsMultipleExpanded is false")
- Given the bare `useDisclosureGroupState` hook with `isDisabled: true`, when initialized, then the hook's returned `isDisabled` is `true`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-stately/test/disclosure/useDisclosureGroupState.test.ts: "should respect isDisabled prop")
- Given the bare `useDisclosure` hook with `isDisabled: true` toggled via rerender, when `isDisabled` changes from `false` to `true`, then the panel ref's `hidden` property becomes `true`; when `isDisabled` is then toggled back to `false`, then the panel ref's `hidden` property remains `true` (does not automatically re-show). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/disclosure/useDisclosure.test.ts: "should keep panel hidden when toggling disabled state")
- Given React Aria Components `Disclosure` with `isDisabled` and `isExpanded` both set, when rendered, then the disclosure root still carries `data-disabled="true"` but the panel content is visible (an explicit `isExpanded` overrides the disabled collapse). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Disclosure.test.js: "should expand a disabled disclosure via isExpanded")
- Given React Aria Components `Disclosure` with a controlled `isExpanded` prop and `onExpandedChange`, when the trigger button is clicked, then `onExpandedChange` is called with `false` but the disclosure stays expanded (`data-expanded="true"`, panel still visible) since the controlling parent did not update the prop. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Disclosure.test.js: "should support controlled isExpanded prop")
- Given React Aria Components `DisclosureGroup` with a single Disclosure and an `onExpandedChange` callback, when the trigger is clicked, then `onExpandedChange` is called once with a `Set` containing that Disclosure's key. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Disclosure.test.js: "should call onExpandedChange when a Disclosure is toggled")

### Notes and edge cases

- Given the bare `useDisclosure` hook on a target element, when a native `beforematch` event is dispatched on the (initially collapsed, uncontrolled) panel element with `onbeforematch` support mocked onto `document.body`, then the disclosure's state becomes expanded and the panel's `hidden` attribute is removed — this exercises the browser "find in page" auto-expand behavior via the `until-found` hidden state. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/disclosure/useDisclosure.test.ts: "should expand when beforematch event occurs")
- Given the bare `useDisclosure` hook with a controlled `isExpanded: false` and `onExpandedChange`, when a native `beforematch` event is dispatched on the panel element, then the panel stays collapsed (`hidden="until-found"` remains) since the controlling parent doesn't update the prop, but `onExpandedChange` is still called once with `true` to request the change. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/disclosure/useDisclosure.test.ts: "should not expand when beforematch event occurs if controlled and closed")

## dialog

### Roles and attributes

- Given `useDialog` with an `aria-label`, when rendered, then the element gets `role="dialog"`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/dialog/useDialog.test.js: "should have role=\"dialog\" by default")
- Given `useDialog({role: 'alertdialog'})`, when rendered, then the element gets `role="alertdialog"`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/dialog/useDialog.test.js: "should accept role=\"alertdialog\"")
- Given a `useDialog` alert dialog rendering the returned `contentProps` on a paragraph, when rendered, then the dialog's `aria-describedby` points at that paragraph's generated id. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/dialog/useDialog.test.js: "should set aria-describedby on alertdialog when content is rendered")
- Given a plain `useDialog` (role dialog) rendering `contentProps` on a paragraph, when rendered, then no `aria-describedby` is emitted even though `contentProps.id` is still provided, so only alert dialogs auto-wire a description. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/dialog/useDialog.test.js: "should not auto-wire aria-describedby on regular dialog, but contentProps.id is still provided")
- Given a `useDialog` alert dialog with an explicit `aria-describedby="custom-id"`, when rendered, then the author's value wins. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/dialog/useDialog.test.js: "should allow aria-describedby override on alertdialog")
- Given a `useDialog` alert dialog with an explicit `aria-describedby`, when rendered, then `contentProps` carries no generated `id`, so no unused id is emitted. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/dialog/useDialog.test.js: "should not generate contentProps.id when aria-describedby is provided")
- Given a Spectrum v3 `Dialog` inside a `ModalProvider` and a modal `DialogContext`, whose child is a `Heading` wrapping a `Header`, when rendered, then the dialog's `aria-labelledby` points at the heading's id. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/dialog/Dialog.test.js: "should be labelled by its header")
- Given the same Spectrum `Dialog` with an explicit `aria-labelledby="batman"`, when rendered, then the author's value wins over the auto-generated heading id. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/dialog/Dialog.test.js: "if aria-labelledby is specified, then that takes precedence over the title")
- Given the same Spectrum `Dialog` with an explicit `aria-label="robin"`, when rendered, then `aria-labelledby` is omitted entirely and the `aria-label` is used. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/dialog/Dialog.test.js: "if aria-label is specified, then that takes precedence over the title")
- Given a React Aria Components `Dialog` with a `Heading slot="title"`, when rendered, then the element has class `react-aria-Dialog` and the `data-rac` marker attribute. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Dialog.test.js: "should have a base default set of attributes")
- Given a RAC `DialogTrigger` with a `Modal` and a `Dialog role="alertdialog"`, when opened via the test-utils Dialog tester, then the dialog has `role="alertdialog"`, `aria-labelledby` pointing at the `Heading slot="title"`, its own passthrough `data-test`, a `.react-aria-Modal` ancestor carrying the modal's `data-test`, and a `.react-aria-ModalOverlay` wrapper; pressing the close button removes the dialog. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Dialog.test.js: "works with modal")
- Given a RAC `Dialog role="alertdialog"` containing a `Text slot="description"`, when opened, then `aria-describedby` resolves to that description element's text. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Dialog.test.js: "should set aria-describedby when Text slot=\"description\" is used in alertdialog")
- Given a RAC `DialogTrigger` wrapping an explicit `ModalOverlay` and `Modal` with custom classes, when opened, then the dialog is labelled by the heading and both the modal and the custom underlay carry their own passthrough `data-test` attributes; pressing close removes the dialog. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Dialog.test.js: "works with modal and custom underlay")
- Given a RAC `DialogTrigger` with a `Popover`, an `OverlayArrow`, and a `Dialog`, when opened via the popover tester, then the trigger gains `data-pressed`, the dialog is labelled by the heading, and the `.react-aria-Popover` and `.react-aria-OverlayArrow` elements both get `position: absolute` plus their passthrough `data-test` attributes. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Dialog.test.js: "works with popover")
- Given a RAC `DialogTrigger` whose dialog has no title, when opened, then the dialog's `aria-labelledby` falls back to the trigger button's id. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Dialog.test.js: "should get default aria label from trigger")

### Focus management

- Given `useDialog` with no autofocused child, when it mounts, then the dialog element has `tabIndex="-1"` and becomes the active element. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/dialog/useDialog.test.js: "should focus the overlay on mount")
- Given `useDialog` containing an `autoFocus` input, when it mounts, then the input keeps focus and the dialog does not steal it. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/dialog/useDialog.test.js: "should not focus the overlay if something inside is auto focused")
- Given a Spectrum v3 `Dialog` with two plain inputs, when rendered, then focus lands on the dialog element itself rather than the first input, and no `aria-labelledby` is emitted because there is no heading. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/dialog/Dialog.test.js: "does not auto focus anything inside")
- Given a Spectrum v3 `Dialog` with only text content, when rendered, then the dialog itself is focused. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/dialog/Dialog.test.js: "auto focuses the dialog itself if there is no focusable child")
- Given a Spectrum v3 `Dialog` whose second input has `autoFocus`, when rendered, then that input is focused. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/dialog/Dialog.test.js: "autofocuses any element that has autofocus inside")
- Given a `MenuTrigger` whose menu item opens a `ModalOverlay` containing a `TextField autoFocus`, when the menu is opened with Enter and the item activated with Enter, then focus ends up in the autofocused input inside the modal. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Dialog.test.js: "ensure Input autoFocus works when opening Modal from MenuItem via keyboard")
- Given a RAC `DialogTrigger` with a `Modal` and an `autoFocus` close button, when opened with either mouse or keyboard via the Dialog tester, then the dialog exists and contains the active element, and closing it removes the dialog. Runs in a real browser via vitest-browser-react, parameterised over mouse and keyboard. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Dialog.browser.test.tsx: "opens via $interactionType and closes")

### State and controlled/uncontrolled

- Given a RAC `Modal isDismissable isOpen onOpenChange` used standalone with no `DialogTrigger`, when the body is clicked, then `onOpenChange(false)` fires once. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Dialog.test.js: "should support Modal being used standalone")
- Given a RAC `Modal isOpen` nested inside a `DialogTrigger`, when rendered, then the modal's own `isOpen` wins over the trigger's context state and an outside click calls the modal's `onOpenChange(false)`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Dialog.test.js: "isOpen and defaultOpen should override state from context")
- Given a RAC `Dialog` with a function child receiving `{close}`, when the rendered dismiss button is pressed, then the dialog is removed. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Dialog.test.js: "should support render props")

### Dismissal and layering

- Given a RAC `Modal isDismissable`, when opened, then a button labelled `Dismiss` is rendered, and clicking it closes the dialog. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Dialog.test.js: "has dismiss button when isDismissable")
- Given a RAC `DialogTrigger` with a `Popover`, when the body is clicked while the popover dialog is open, then the dialog is removed. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Dialog.test.js: "works with popover")
- Given a RAC popover dialog labelled by its trigger, when the body is clicked, then the dialog is removed. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Dialog.test.js: "should get default aria label from trigger")
- Given a RAC `Modal` containing a `DateRangePicker` whose calendar opens in a nested `Popover` with `data-trigger="DateRangePicker"`, when a date cell is clicked and then the body is clicked, then only the popover closes and the modal stays mounted, so an outside click dismisses only the top layer. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Dialog.test.js: "should not close Modal when DateRangePicker is dismissed by outside click")

### Animation and presence
