- Given the bare `useToastState` hook configured with `maxVisibleToasts: 3`, when a "First Toast" (`timeout: 0`), then "Second Toast" (`timeout: 1000`, after `resumeAll()` is called), then "Third Toast" (`timeout: 0`) are added, then all three are visible in newest-first order; when enough time passes for the 1000ms timer on "Second Toast" to elapse, then it is automatically removed from `visibleToasts` even though it is not the oldest toast, leaving "Third Toast" and "First Toast" visible. (react-spectrum: packages/react-stately/test/toast/useToastState.test.js: "should be able to display three toasts and remove the middle toast via timeout then the visible toast")
- Given the same three-toast `maxVisibleToasts: 3` setup after the middle toast's timeout has auto-removed it, when the remaining front toast is closed via `close(key)`, then only "First Toast" remains visible. (react-spectrum: packages/react-stately/test/toast/useToastState.test.js: "should be able to display three toasts and remove the middle toast via timeout then the visible toast")
- Given the bare `useToastState` hook with default (single-visible) settings, when three toasts are added one after another, each add makes only the newest toast visible while earlier ones are pushed into an internal, non-visible queue. (react-spectrum: packages/react-stately/test/toast/useToastState.test.js: "should be able to display one toast, add multiple toasts")
- Given the bare `useToastState` hook with default settings where three toasts have been added (only the third is visible) and the key of the previously-queued, non-visible second toast is captured, when `close` is called with that non-visible toast's key, then the currently visible toast is unaffected. (react-spectrum: packages/react-stately/test/toast/useToastState.test.js: "should be able to display one toast, add multiple toasts, and remove the middle not visible one programmatically")
- Given that setup after the non-visible middle toast has been closed by key, when the now-visible "Third Toast" is closed via its key, then "First Toast" (skipping the already-removed "Second Toast") becomes the visible toast. (react-spectrum: packages/react-stately/test/toast/useToastState.test.js: "should be able to display one toast, add multiple toasts, and remove the middle not visible one programmatically")
- Given the bare `useToastState` hook configured with `maxVisibleToasts: 3`, when "First", "Second", then "Third" toasts are added, then `visibleToasts` reflects newest-first order `[Third, Second, First]`; when the middle toast ("Second") is closed by key, then the remaining toasts keep their relative order as `[Third, First]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-stately/test/toast/useToastState.test.js: "should maintain the toast queue order on close")
- Given the bare `useToastState` hook with one toast added, when `close` is called with its key, then `visibleToasts` becomes an empty array. (react-spectrum: packages/react-stately/test/toast/useToastState.test.js: "should close a toast")
- Given the bare `useToastState` hook with default (single-visible) settings, when a first toast is added and then a second toast is added, then only the second is visible; when the visible (second) toast is closed, then the first, previously queued toast becomes visible again. (react-spectrum: packages/react-stately/test/toast/useToastState.test.js: "should queue toasts")
- Given `useToast({toast: {key: 1}}, state)`, when `closeButtonProps.onPress()` is invoked, then the state's `close` is called once with the toast's key `1`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/toast/useToast.test.js: "handles close button")

### Dismissal and layering

- Given a React Aria Components `ToastRegion` rendered inside an `UNSAFE_PortalProvider` whose `getContainer` points at a custom DOM node, when a toast is added, then the alertdialog renders inside that custom container element rather than the default portal location. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Toast.test.js: "should render the toast region in the portal container provided by PortalProvider")
- Given two Spectrum v3 `ToastContainer` elements mounted simultaneously (keyed "first" and "second") sharing the same global toast queue, when a toast is triggered and the containers are rerendered through several combinations (only "second", only "first", both again), then exactly one region and one alert are ever rendered at a time, regardless of how many `ToastContainer` instances are mounted. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/toast/ToastContainer.test.js: "should only render one ToastContainer")

### SSR and hydration

- Given a Spectrum v3 `Provider`/`ToastContainer` tree, when it is rendered via the `testSSR` server-rendering harness with no toasts triggered, then it renders without throwing any errors. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/toast/ToastContainer.ssr.test.js: "should render without errors")

### Notes and edge cases

- Given the bare `useToastState` hook configured with a `wrapUpdate` function, when a toast is added, then added again, then closed, then `wrapUpdate` is called once for each of those three state-mutating operations (three calls total). (react-spectrum: packages/react-stately/test/toast/useToastState.test.js: "should use provided wrapUpdate")
- Given a Spectrum v3 `ToastContainer` with a `window` listener registered for the `react-spectrum-toast` custom event, when `ToastQueue.neutral(...)` is called to trigger a toast, then the event fires once with `detail` equal to `{children: 'Toast is default', variant: 'neutral', options: {}}`; when the listener calls `event.preventDefault()` on it, then no alert role ever renders, letting host applications intercept and suppress the default toast queue. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/toast/ToastContainer.test.js: "should support custom toast events")
- Given `useToast({toast: {}, 'data-test-id': 'toast'}, state)`, when the returned props are inspected, then `toastProps['data-test-id']` is `toast`, so arbitrary data attributes pass through. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/toast/useToast.test.js: "passes through data attributes")

## progress

### Roles and attributes

- Given `useProgressBar({})` with no props, when rendered, then `role` is `progressbar` with `aria-valuemin=0`, `aria-valuemax=100`, `aria-valuenow=0`, `aria-valuetext="0%"`, no `aria-label`/`aria-labelledby`, and a console warning that a visible label or `aria-label`/`aria-labelledby` is required. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/progress/useProgressBar.test.js: "with default props if no props are provided")
- Given `useProgressBar({label: 'Test'})`, when rendered, then `labelProps.id` is generated and `progressBarProps['aria-labelledby']` points at it. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/progress/useProgressBar.test.js: "supports labeling")
- Given `useProgressBar({value: 25})`, when rendered, then `aria-valuenow` is 25 and `aria-valuetext` is `"25%"`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/progress/useProgressBar.test.js: "with value of 25%")
- Given `useProgressBar({isIndeterminate: true})`, when rendered, then `aria-valuemin`/`aria-valuemax` stay 0/100 but both `aria-valuenow` and `aria-valuetext` are omitted. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/progress/useProgressBar.test.js: "with indeterminate prop")
- Given `useProgressBar({value: 25, valueLabel: '¥25'})`, when rendered, then `aria-valuenow` stays 25 while `aria-valuetext` becomes the custom string `"¥25"`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/progress/useProgressBar.test.js: "with custom text value")
- Given `useProgressBar({label: 'React test', value: 25})`, when rendered, then `aria-labelledby` matches the generated label id. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/progress/useProgressBar.test.js: "with custom label")
- Given a Spectrum v3 `ProgressBar label="Progress Bar"`, when rendered, then `role="progressbar"` with `aria-valuemin=0`, `aria-valuemax=100`, `aria-valuenow=0`, `aria-valuetext="0%"`, and `aria-labelledby` resolving to an element containing the label text. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/progress/ProgressBar.test.js: "$Name handles defaults")
- Given a Spectrum v3 `ProgressBar value={30}`, when rendered, then `aria-valuenow` is 30 and `aria-valuetext` is `"30%"`. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/progress/ProgressBar.test.js: "$Name update all fileds by value")
- Given a Spectrum v3 `ProgressBar value={-1}`, when rendered, then the value clamps to `aria-valuenow=0` and `aria-valuetext="0%"`. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/progress/ProgressBar.test.js: "$Name clamps values to 0")
- Given a Spectrum v3 `ProgressBar value={1000}`, when rendered, then the value clamps to `aria-valuenow=100` and `aria-valuetext="100%"`. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/progress/ProgressBar.test.js: "$Name clamps values to 100")
- Given a Spectrum v3 `ProgressBar value={0} minValue={-5} maxValue={5}`, when rendered, then `aria-valuenow` is 0 while `aria-valuetext` is `"50%"`, so the percentage is computed across the custom range. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/progress/ProgressBar.test.js: "Can handle negative values")
- Given a Spectrum v3 `ProgressCircle aria-label="Progress"` with no value, when rendered, then `role="progressbar"` with `aria-valuemin=0`, `aria-valuemax=100`, `aria-valuenow=0`. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/progress/ProgressCircle.test.js: "$Name handles defaults")
- Given `ProgressCircle isIndeterminate`, when rendered, then min and max stay 0/100 and `aria-valuenow` is absent. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/progress/ProgressCircle.test.js: "$Name handles indeterminate")
- Given `ProgressCircle value={30}`, when rendered, then `aria-valuenow` is 30. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/progress/ProgressCircle.test.js: "$Name handles controlled value")
- Given `ProgressCircle value={-1}`, when rendered, then `aria-valuenow` clamps to 0. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/progress/ProgressCircle.test.js: "$Name clamps values to 0")
- Given `ProgressCircle value={1000}`, when rendered, then `aria-valuenow` clamps to 100. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/progress/ProgressCircle.test.js: "$Name clamps values to 100")
- Given `ProgressCircle value={0} minValue={-5} maxValue={5}`, when rendered, then `aria-valuenow` is 0 and `aria-valuetext` is `"50%"`. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/progress/ProgressCircle.test.js: "can handle negative values with minValue and maxValue")
- Given a React Aria Components `ProgressBar value={25}` with a render-prop child, when rendered, then the element has class `react-aria-ProgressBar`, `aria-valuenow=25`, `aria-labelledby` resolving to the `Label` text, the passthrough `data-foo="bar"`, a render-prop `valueText` of `"25%"`, and a bar styled `width: 25%`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ProgressBar.test.js: "renders")
- Given a RAC `ProgressBar value={3} minValue={0} maxValue={6}`, when rendered, then `aria-valuemin=0`, `aria-valuemax=6`, `aria-valuenow=3`, `aria-valuetext="50%"`, and the render prop supplies `percentage` 50. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ProgressBar.test.js: "supports a custom range")
- Given a RAC `ProgressBar value={0} minValue={0} maxValue={0}` (a zero-width range), when rendered, then `aria-valuetext` is `"0%"` rather than `"NaN%"` and the render-prop percentage is 0. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ProgressBar.test.js: "renders 0 percent for an empty range")
- Given a RAC `ProgressBar value={5} minValue={5} maxValue={5}` (a zero-width range at a non-zero bound), when rendered, then `aria-valuetext` is `"0%"` and the percentage is 0. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ProgressBar.test.js: "renders 0 percent for an empty range with a non-zero bound")
- Given a RAC `ProgressBar isIndeterminate` with a function `className`, when rendered, then the class function receives `isIndeterminate: true`, `aria-valuenow` is omitted, the render-prop `percentage` is `undefined`, and the bar's inline width is left empty. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ProgressBar.test.js: "supports indeterminate state")

### State and controlled/uncontrolled

- Given a Spectrum v3 `ProgressCircle value={0}`, when rendered, then both fill submasks (`fillSubMask1`, `fillSubMask2`) exist with no inline style. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/progress/ProgressCircle.test.js: "handles submask defaults")
- Given `ProgressCircle value={0}`, when rendered, then neither submask has an inline transform, so none of the circle is filled. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/progress/ProgressCircle.test.js: "shows none of the circle for 0%")
- Given `ProgressCircle value={25}`, when rendered, then submask 1 is `rotate(-90deg)` and submask 2 is `rotate(-180deg)`. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/progress/ProgressCircle.test.js: "shows quarter of the circle for 25%")
- Given `ProgressCircle value={50}`, when rendered, then submask 1 is `rotate(0deg)` and submask 2 is `rotate(-180deg)`. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/progress/ProgressCircle.test.js: "shows half the circle for 50%")
- Given `ProgressCircle value={75}`, when rendered, then submask 1 is `rotate(0deg)` and submask 2 is `rotate(-90deg)`. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/progress/ProgressCircle.test.js: "shows quarter of the circle for 75%")
- Given `ProgressCircle value={100}`, when rendered, then both submasks are `rotate(0deg)`. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/progress/ProgressCircle.test.js: "shows all of the circle for 100%")

### SSR and hydration

- Given a Spectrum v3 `ProgressBar aria-label="progress"`, when server-rendered and hydrated, then no errors occur. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/progress/ProgressBar.ssr.test.js: "should render without errors")
- Given a Spectrum v3 `ProgressCircle aria-label="progress"`, when server-rendered and hydrated, then no errors occur. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/progress/ProgressCircle.ssr.test.js: "should render without errors")

### Accessibility tooling

- Given a Spectrum v3 `ProgressBar value={25}` with no label at all, when rendered, then a console warning names the missing `aria-label`/`aria-labelledby`. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/progress/ProgressBar.test.js: "warns user if no aria-label is provided")
- Given a Spectrum v3 `ProgressCircle value={25}` with no label, when rendered, then a console warning says `ProgressCircle requires an aria-label or aria-labelledby attribute for accessibility`. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/progress/ProgressCircle.test.js: "warns user if no aria-label is provided")

### Notes and edge cases

- Given a Spectrum v3 `ProgressBar size="S" UNSAFE_className="testClass"`, when rendered, then the custom class appears in the rendered `class` attribute. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/progress/ProgressBar.test.js: "$Name supports UNSAFE_className")
- Given a Spectrum v3 `ProgressBar data-testid="test"`, when rendered, then arbitrary DOM props pass through. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/progress/ProgressBar.test.js: "supports custom DOM props")
- Given a Spectrum v3 `ProgressCircle UNSAFE_className="testClass"`, when rendered, then the custom class appears in `class`. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/progress/ProgressCircle.test.js: "$Name supports UNSAFE_className")
- Given a Spectrum v3 `ProgressCircle data-testid="test"`, when rendered, then arbitrary DOM props pass through. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/progress/ProgressCircle.test.js: "supports custom DOM props")
- Given a RAC `ProgressBar slot="test"` under a `ProgressBarContext.Provider` that maps the `test` slot to `aria-label="test"`, when rendered, then the element carries both `slot="test"` and the slot-provided `aria-label`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ProgressBar.test.js: "should support slot")
- Given a RAC `ProgressBar render={props => <div {...props} data-custom="bar" />}`, when rendered, then the custom render function's extra props land on the element. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ProgressBar.test.js: "should support custom render function")

## meter

### Roles and attributes

- Given a Spectrum v3 `Meter label="Meter"`, when rendered, then the element answers to both `meter` and (via fallback) `progressbar` roles with `aria-valuemin=0`, `aria-valuemax=100`, `aria-valuenow=0`, `aria-valuetext="0%"`, and `aria-labelledby` resolving to the label text. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/meter/Meter.test.js: "handles defaults")
- Given a Spectrum v3 `Meter value={30}`, when rendered, then `aria-valuenow` is 30 and `aria-valuetext` is `"30%"`. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/meter/Meter.test.js: "updates all fields by value")
- Given a Spectrum v3 `Meter value={-1}`, when rendered, then the value clamps to `aria-valuenow=0` with `aria-valuetext="0%"`. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/meter/Meter.test.js: "clamps values to 0")
- Given a Spectrum v3 `Meter value={1000}`, when rendered, then the value clamps to `aria-valuenow=100` with `aria-valuetext="100%"`. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/meter/Meter.test.js: "clamps values to 100")
- Given a Spectrum v3 `Meter value={0} minValue={-5} maxValue={5}`, when rendered, then `aria-valuenow` is 0, `aria-valuetext` is `"50%"`, and the `role` attribute is literally `"meter progressbar"` so browsers without `meter` support fall back to `progressbar`. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/meter/Meter.test.js: "can handle negative values")
- Given a Spectrum v3 `Meter aria-label="Meter"`, when rendered, then the `aria-label` is applied directly. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/meter/Meter.test.js: "supports aria-label")
- Given a RAC `Meter value={25}` with a render-prop child, when rendered, then it has class `react-aria-Meter`, `aria-valuenow=25`, `aria-labelledby` pointing at the `Label`, the passthrough `data-foo="bar"`, a `valueText` of `"25%"`, and a bar styled `width: 25%`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Meter.test.js: "renders")
- Given a RAC `Meter value={3} minValue={0} maxValue={6}`, when rendered, then `aria-valuemin=0`, `aria-valuemax=6`, `aria-valuenow=3`, `aria-valuetext="50%"`, and the render prop's `percentage` is 50. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Meter.test.js: "supports a custom range")
- Given a RAC `Meter value={0} minValue={0} maxValue={0}`, when rendered, then `aria-valuetext` is `"0%"` and not `"NaN%"`, and the render-prop percentage is 0. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Meter.test.js: "renders 0 percent for an empty range")

### SSR and hydration

- Given a Spectrum v3 `Meter label="Storage space" variant="positive"`, when server-rendered and hydrated, then no errors occur. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/meter/Meter.ssr.test.js: "should render without errors")

### Notes and edge cases

- Given a Spectrum v3 `Meter size="S" UNSAFE_className="testClass"`, when rendered, then the custom class appears in `class`. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/meter/Meter.test.js: "supports UNSAFE_className")
- Given a Spectrum v3 `Meter data-testid="test"`, when rendered, then arbitrary DOM props pass through. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/meter/Meter.test.js: "supports custom DOM props")
- Given a RAC `Meter slot="test"` under a `MeterContext.Provider` mapping that slot to `aria-label="test"`, when rendered, then the element carries both `slot="test"` and the slot `aria-label`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Meter.test.js: "should support slot")
- Given a RAC `Meter render={...}`, when rendered, then the custom render function's extra props land on the element. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Meter.test.js: "should support custom render function")

## separator

### Roles and attributes

- Given a Spectrum v3 `Divider aria-label="divides"` at default, medium, and small sizes, when rendered, then it exposes `role="separator"`, carries the `aria-label`, and omits `aria-orientation` for the implicit horizontal orientation. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/divider/Divider.test.js: "$Name handles defaults")
- Given a Spectrum v3 `Divider orientation="vertical"` at default, medium, and small sizes, when rendered, then `aria-orientation="vertical"` is set alongside the `aria-label`. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/divider/Divider.test.js: "$Name can be vertical")
- Given a vertical `Divider aria-labelledby="test"` at each size, when rendered, then `aria-labelledby` points at the external element. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/divider/Divider.test.js: "$Name supports aria-labelledby")
- Given a Spectrum v3 `Divider aria-label="divides"`, when rendered horizontally, then no implicit `aria-orientation` is emitted. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/divider/Divider.test.js: "$Name should not include implicit attributes")
- Given a RAC `Separator`, when rendered, then the element is an `<hr>` with class `react-aria-Separator` and `role="separator"`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Separator.test.js: "should render a separator with default class")
- Given a RAC `Separator className="test"`, when rendered, then the default class is replaced by the supplied one. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Separator.test.js: "should render a separator with custom class")
- Given a RAC `Separator aria-label="label"`, when rendered, then accessibility props pass through. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Separator.test.js: "should support accessibility props")

### SSR and hydration

- Given a Spectrum v3 `Divider`, when server-rendered and hydrated, then no errors occur. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/divider/Divider.ssr.test.js: "should render without errors")

### Notes and edge cases

- Given a vertical `Divider data-testid="test"` at each size, when rendered, then custom data attributes pass through to the separator element. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/divider/Divider.test.js: "$Name supports custom data attributes")
- Given a Spectrum v3 `Divider` with a ref, when rendered, then `ref.current.UNSAFE_getDOMNode()` is the separator element. (react-spectrum: packages/@adobe/react-spectrum/test/divider/Divider.test.js: "$Name forwards the ref")
- Given a RAC `Separator data-foo="bar"`, when rendered, then arbitrary DOM props pass through. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Separator.test.js: "should support DOM props")
- Given a RAC `Separator render={props => <hr {...props} data-custom="true" />}`, when rendered, then the custom render function's extra props land on the element. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Separator.test.js: "should support custom render function")
- Given a RAC `Separator slot="test"` under a `SeparatorContext.Provider` mapping that slot to `aria-label="test"`, when rendered, then the element carries both `slot="test"` and the slot `aria-label`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/Separator.test.js: "should support slot")

## visually-hidden

### Focus management

- Given a `VisuallyHidden` wrapper around a button, sitting between two visible buttons, when the user tabs to the hidden button, then it does receive focus but the wrapper's clipping inline style is unchanged, so the text stays visually hidden. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/visually-hidden/VisuallyHidden.test.tsx: "hides element")
- Given `VisuallyHidden isFocusable` around a button, when the user tabs to that button, then the wrapper's inline style is cleared to an empty string, revealing the content while focused. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/visually-hidden/VisuallyHidden.test.tsx: "unhides element if focused and isFocusable")

## overlay

### Roles and attributes

- Given a `useModal()` modal rendered inside an `OverlayContainer` under an `OverlayProvider`, when the modal mounts, then every ancestor provider gets `aria-hidden="true"` while the modal's own provider does not, and on unmount the `aria-hidden` is removed from the ancestors. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useModal.test.js: "should set aria-hidden on parent providers on mount and remove on unmount")
- Given nested modals, when an inner modal mounts, then both the root provider and the outer modal's provider are `aria-hidden` and only the innermost provider is left visible; unmounting the inner modal un-hides the outer provider again, and unmounting both un-hides the root. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useModal.test.js: "should work with nested modals")
- Given `OverlayContainer` with an explicit `portalContainer` other than `document.body`, when modals mount into it, then the same `aria-hidden` layering of ancestor providers applies. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useModal.test.js: "can specify a different container from the default document.body")

### Keyboard

- Given an open overlay from `useOverlay`, when Escape is pressed on the overlay element, then `onClose` is called once. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useOverlay.test.js: "should hide the overlay when pressing the escape key")
- Given an open overlay from `useOverlay` with `isDismissable={false}`, when Escape is pressed, then `onClose` is still called once, so Escape dismissal is independent of `isDismissable`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useOverlay.test.js: "should still hide the overlay when pressing the escape key if isDismissable is false")

### Focus management

- Given an open `useOverlay` overlay containing an `autoFocus` input, when the overlay mounts, then the overlay container does not steal focus and the child input stays focused. Runs in all three event models (mouse events, pointer events, touch events). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useOverlay.test.js: "should not focus the overlay if a child is focused")

### Dismissal and layering

- Given an open `useOverlay` overlay with `isDismissable`, when a press starts and ends on `document.body` followed by a click, then `onClose` is called once. Asserted separately for mouse events, pointer events, and touch events. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useOverlay.test.js: "should hide the overlay when clicking outside if isDismissble is true")
- Given `isDismissable` plus `shouldCloseOnInteractOutside` that returns true for the pressed target, when the outside press completes, then `onClose` is called once. Asserted for mouse, pointer, and touch event models. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useOverlay.test.js: "should hide the overlay when clicking outside if shouldCloseOnInteractOutside returns true")
- Given `isDismissable` plus `shouldCloseOnInteractOutside` that returns false for the pressed target, when the outside press completes, then `onClose` is never called. Asserted for mouse, pointer, and touch event models. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useOverlay.test.js: "should not hide the overlay when clicking outside if shouldCloseOnInteractOutside returns false")
- Given `isDismissable={false}`, when an outside press completes, then `onClose` is never called. Asserted for mouse, pointer, and touch event models. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useOverlay.test.js: "should not hide the overlay when clicking outside if isDismissable is false")
- Given two open dismissable overlays, when an outside press completes, then only the most recently mounted overlay's `onClose` fires; after unmounting that overlay, the next outside press closes the remaining one. This pins a global overlay stack shared across React roots. Asserted for mouse, pointer, and touch event models. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useOverlay.test.js: "should only hide the top-most overlay")
- Given a `useModalOverlay` modal with `isDismissable` and `shouldCloseOnInteractOutside` returning true, when an outside press completes, then `onOpenChange(false)` is called. Asserted for mouse, pointer, and touch event models. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useModalOverlay.test.js: "should hide the overlay when clicking outside if shouldCloseOnInteractOutside returns true")
- Given a `useModalOverlay` modal whose `shouldCloseOnInteractOutside` returns false, when an outside press completes, then `onOpenChange` is never called. Asserted for mouse, pointer, and touch event models. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useModalOverlay.test.js: "should not hide the overlay when clicking outside if shouldCloseOnInteractOutside returns false")
- Given a `useOverlay` overlay portalled into a shadow root with `shouldCloseOnInteractOutside` matching an element inside that shadow root, when a press starts and ends on the shadow-root element, then `onClose` fires, so outside-interaction detection retargets composed events through shadow boundaries. Requires the `enableShadowDOM` feature flag; asserted for mouse, pointer, and touch event models. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useOverlay.shadow.test.js: "should close the overlay when clicking outside if shouldCloseOnInteractOutside returns true")
- Given the same shadow-DOM setup with `shouldCloseOnInteractOutside` returning false for the shadow element, when the press completes, then `onClose` does not fire. Asserted for mouse, pointer, and touch event models. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useOverlay.shadow.test.js: "should not close the overlay when clicking outside if shouldCloseOnInteractOutside returns false")

### Positioning

- Given an open overlay wired with both `useOverlayTrigger` and `useOverlayPosition`, when a scrollable ancestor of the trigger scrolls, then `onOpenChange(false)` is called once. Kept for backward compatibility. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useOverlayTrigger.test.js: "should close the overlay when the trigger scrolls with useOverlayPosition for backward compatibility")
- Given the same setup, when an adjacent scrollable region that is not an ancestor of the trigger scrolls, then `onOpenChange` is not called. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useOverlayTrigger.test.js: "should not close the overlay when an adjacent scrollable region scrolls with useOverlayPosition for backward compatibility")
- Given the same setup, when `document.body` scrolls, then `onOpenChange(false)` is called once. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useOverlayTrigger.test.js: "should close the overlay when the body scrolls with useOverlayPosition for backward compatibility")
- Given a popover wired with `usePopover` (rather than raw `useOverlayPosition`), when `document.body` scrolls, then `onOpenChange` is not called, so `usePopover` deliberately does not inherit the legacy close-on-scroll behavior. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/usePopover.test.tsx: "should not close popover on scroll")

### SSR and hydration

- Given `OverlayProvider` wrapping an `OverlayContainer` and modal content, when rendered on the server and hydrated, then no errors or hydration mismatches occur. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useModal.ssr.test.js: "should render without errors")

### Notes and edge cases

- Given a modal whose `portalContainer` is a DOM node nested inside another modal's container, when it mounts, then `useModal` throws, so containers may not be nested. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useModal.test.js: "if inside another container, throws an error")
- Given a popover using `UNSAFE_PortalProvider` to portal into a shadow root, when the trigger is clicked, then the popover content renders inside the shadow root; clicking a button inside the popover runs its handler and leaves the popover open; clicking the close button closes it. Guarded to React 17+ and requires the `enableShadowDOM` flag. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/usePopover.shadow.test.tsx: "should handle popover interactions with UNSAFE_PortalProvider in shadow DOM")
- Given React 16, when the shadow-DOM popover suite loads, then the whole suite is replaced with a single trivially passing test because portals into shadow roots are unsupported there. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/usePopover.shadow.test.tsx: "should pass")

## overlay-position

### Roles and attributes

- Given `useOverlayPosition` with an arrow element spread with `arrowProps`, when rendered, then the arrow has `aria-hidden="true"` and `role="presentation"` so screen readers skip it. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useOverlayPosition.test.tsx: "arrow should be hidden when using assistive technologies")

### Dismissal and layering

- Given an open positioned overlay inside a scrollable ancestor, when that ancestor fires `scroll`, then `onClose` is called once. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useOverlayPosition.test.tsx: "should close the overlay when the trigger scrolls")
- Given an open positioned overlay with a sibling scrollable region, when the sibling fires `scroll`, then `onClose` is not called. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useOverlayPosition.test.tsx: "should not close the overlay when an adjacent scrollable region scrolls")
- Given an open positioned overlay, when `document.body` fires `scroll`, then `onClose` is called once. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useOverlayPosition.test.tsx: "should close the overlay when the body scrolls")
- Given an open positioned overlay, when `document` fires `scroll`, then `onClose` is called once. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useOverlayPosition.test.tsx: "should close the overlay when the document scrolls")
- Given an open positioned overlay, when `window` is the target of the `scroll` event, then `onClose` is called once, covering the case where the scroll event target is not an element. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useOverlayPosition.test.tsx: "should close the overlay when target is window in a scroll event")

### Positioning

- Given a 100x100 trigger at top 250 in a 500x768 viewport with a 300x200 overlay and `arrowSize: 8`, when positioned with the default placement, then the overlay gets `position: absolute; z-index: 100000; left: 12px; top: 350px; max-height: 406px`, the resolved `placement` is `bottom`, and the arrow is centered on the trigger at `left: 48px` (trigger left + trigger width / 2 - overlay left). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useOverlayPosition.test.tsx: "should position the overlay relative to the trigger")
- Given the same fixture with `placement="top"`, when positioned, then the overlay is anchored by `bottom: 350px` rather than `top`, gets `left: 12px; max-height: 238px`, reports `placement: top`, and keeps the arrow at `left: 48px`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useOverlayPosition.test.tsx: "should position the overlay relative to the trigger at top")
- Given the overlay positioned below a trigger at top 400 (`top: 500px; max-height: 256px`), when the document client height and `visualViewport.height` grow to 1000 and a window `resize` event fires, then `max-height` is recomputed to 488px while `left`/`top` and the `bottom` placement stay the same. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useOverlayPosition.test.tsx: "should update the position on window resize")
- Given a positioned overlay, when the hook's props change (adding `offset={20}`), then the position recomputes to `top: 370px` with `max-height: 386px` without any DOM event. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useOverlayPosition.test.tsx: "should update the position on props change")
- Given a `maxHeight` prop larger than the available viewport space (450), when positioned, then the computed available height wins (`max-height: 406px`); re-rendering with a smaller `maxHeight` (150) makes the prop win (`max-height: 150px`). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useOverlayPosition.test.tsx: "should update the overlay's maxHeight by the given one if it's smaller than available viewport height.")
- Given an overlay whose container's `offsetParent` is a `position: relative` container offset by top 150, when positioned with the default placement, then `top` is 200px rather than 350px because the offsets are resolved relative to the positioned ancestor. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useOverlayPosition.test.tsx: "should position the overlay relative to the trigger")
- Given the same positioned-container fixture with `placement="top"` and a 400-tall container, when positioned, then the overlay is anchored `bottom: 300px` (trigger top 250 minus container top 150 gives 100 from the container top, so 300 from its bottom) with `max-height: 238px`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/useOverlayPosition.test.tsx: "should position the overlay relative to the trigger at top")

All `calculatePosition` cases below share one fixture: a 100x100 target, a 200x200 overlay with zero margins, a 600x600 relatively-positioned container, a 600x600 boundary element, `padding: 50`, `arrowSize: 8`, `visualViewport` 600 tall. Expected values are given as `[main-axis coord, cross-axis coord, arrowOffsetLeft, arrowOffsetTop, maxHeight]`; the test also asserts the returned `placement` and a `triggerAnchorPoint` derived from the arrow offsets. Each bullet is one `it` named "Should calculate the correct position" (or the provider variant) nested under `describe('placement = <placement>')` and a scenario describe.

- Given `placement="left"` with the target at (250,250), when position is calculated with no offsets, then the result is `[50, 200, undefined, 100, 350]` with placement `left`, expressed as a `right` coordinate of 350. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="left"` with the target at (250,250) and a provider whose boundary starts 50px down the screen, when position is calculated, then the same `[50, 200, undefined, 100, 350]` result is produced with `maxHeight` reduced by the 50px provider offset. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position when provider does not start at top of screen")
- Given `placement="left"` with the target flush at (0,0) so the overlay overflows the start of the viewport, when position is calculated, then the result clamps to `[-200, 50, undefined, 4, 500]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="left"` with the target at (500,500) so the overlay overflows the end of the viewport, when position is calculated, then the result clamps to `[300, 350, undefined, 196, 200]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="left"` with `offset: 10` on the main axis, when position is calculated, then the result shifts to `[40, 200, undefined, 100, 350]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="left"` with `crossOffset: 10`, when position is calculated, then the result shifts to `[50, 210, undefined, 90, 340]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="left"` with `crossOffset: -10`, when position is calculated, then the result shifts to `[50, 190, undefined, 110, 360]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="left"` with an extreme `crossOffset: 1000` and `arrowBoundaryOffset: 20`, when position is calculated, then the overlay clamps to `[50, 322, undefined, 24, 228]` so the arrow is kept at least 20px from the overlay edge. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="left top"` with the target at (250,250), when position is calculated with no offsets, then the result is `[50, 250, undefined, 50, 300]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="left top"` with a provider offset of 50, when position is calculated, then the result is `[50, 250, undefined, 50, 300]` with `maxHeight` reduced by the provider offset. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position when provider does not start at top of screen")
- Given `placement="left top"` with the target at (0,0), when position is calculated, then the result clamps to `[-200, 50, undefined, 4, 500]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="left top"` with the target at (500,500), when position is calculated, then the result clamps to `[300, 350, undefined, 196, 200]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="left top"` with `offset: 10`, when position is calculated, then the result is `[40, 250, undefined, 50, 300]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="left top"` with `crossOffset: 10`, when position is calculated, then the result is `[50, 260, undefined, 40, 290]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="left top"` with `crossOffset: -10`, when position is calculated, then the result is `[50, 240, undefined, 60, 310]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="left top"` with `crossOffset: 1000` and `arrowBoundaryOffset: 20`, when position is calculated, then the result clamps to `[50, 322, undefined, 24, 228]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="left bottom"` (the overlay's bottom aligns with the trigger's bottom so it grows upward) with the target at (250,250), when position is calculated with no offsets, then the result is `[50, 150, undefined, 150, 300]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="left bottom"` with a provider offset of 50, when position is calculated, then the result is `[50, 150, undefined, 150, 300]` and the provider offset is not subtracted from `maxHeight` because a `bottom` cross axis grows toward the top. - Given `placement="right"` with `crossOffset: 1000` and `arrowBoundaryOffset: 20`, when position is calculated, then the result clamps to `[350, 322, undefined, 24, 228]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="right top"` with the target at (250,250), when position is calculated with no offsets, then the result is `[350, 250, undefined, 50, 300]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="right top"` with a provider offset of 50, when position is calculated, then the result is `[350, 250, undefined, 50, 300]` with `maxHeight` reduced by the provider offset. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position when provider does not start at top of screen")
- Given `placement="right top"` with the target at (0,0), when position is calculated, then the result is `[100, 50, undefined, 4, 500]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="right top"` with the target at (500,500), when position is calculated, then the result is `[600, 350, undefined, 196, 200]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="right top"` with `offset: 10`, when position is calculated, then the result is `[360, 250, undefined, 50, 300]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="right top"` with `crossOffset: 10`, when position is calculated, then the result is `[350, 260, undefined, 40, 290]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="right top"` with `crossOffset: -10`, when position is calculated, then the result is `[350, 240, undefined, 60, 310]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="right top"` with `crossOffset: 1000` and `arrowBoundaryOffset: 20`, when position is calculated, then the result clamps to `[350, 322, undefined, 24, 228]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="right bottom"` with the target at (250,250), when position is calculated with no offsets, then the result is `[350, 150, undefined, 150, 300]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="right bottom"` with a provider offset of 50, when position is calculated, then the result is `[350, 150, undefined, 150, 300]` and `maxHeight` is not reduced, because a `bottom` cross axis grows upward. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position when provider does not start at top of screen")
- Given `placement="right bottom"` with the target at (0,0), when position is calculated, then the result is `[100, 50, undefined, 4, 200]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="right bottom"` with the target at (500,500), when position is calculated, then the result is `[600, 350, undefined, 196, 500]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="right bottom"` with `offset: 10`, when position is calculated, then the result is `[360, 150, undefined, 150, 300]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="right bottom"` with `crossOffset: 10`, when position is calculated, then the result is `[350, 160, undefined, 140, 310]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="right bottom"` with `crossOffset: -10`, when position is calculated, then the result is `[350, 140, undefined, 160, 290]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="right bottom"` with `crossOffset: 1000` and `arrowBoundaryOffset: 20`, when position is calculated, then the result clamps to `[350, 322, undefined, 24, 472]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="left"` with `shouldFlip: true` and the target flush at (0,0) so there is no room on the left, when position is calculated, then the overlay flips to `right` and produces the same numbers as the un-flipped `right` case at that target, `[100, 50, undefined, 4, 500]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="left"` with `shouldFlip: true`, `offset: 10`, and the target at (0,250), when position is calculated, then the overlay flips to `right` and lands at `[110, 200, undefined, 100, 350]`, so the main-axis offset is applied after flipping. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given `placement="right"` and a target taller and wider than the overlay (300x300 vs 200x200), when position is calculated, then the overlay is centered on the target's cross axis at `[550, 300, undefined, 100, 250]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "Should calculate the correct position")
- Given a target element with a 20px margin and `placement="bottom"` with zero padding, offset, and arrow size, when position is calculated, then `position.top` is 0, so the target's own margin does not leak into the computed offset. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/calculatePosition.test.ts: "checks if overlay positions correctly")

## prevent-scroll

### Notes and edge cases

- Given `usePreventScroll()` mounted, when it mounts, then `document.documentElement` gets `overflow: hidden`, and on unmount the style is removed. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/usePreventScroll.test.js: "should set overflow: hidden on the body on mount and remove on unmount")
- Given two components calling `usePreventScroll()`, when the second unmounts, then `overflow: hidden` remains; only after the first also unmounts is it removed, so the lock is reference counted. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/usePreventScroll.test.js: "should work with nested modals")
- Given two components calling `usePreventScroll()`, when they unmount in the opposite order from mounting, then the lock is still released only after the last one unmounts. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/usePreventScroll.test.js: "should work with nested/multiple modals regardless of unmount order")
- Given `usePreventScroll()` active, when it is re-rendered with `isDisabled: true`, then `overflow: hidden` is removed without unmounting. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/usePreventScroll.test.js: "should remove overflow: hidden when isDisabled option is true")

## dismiss-button

### Roles and attributes

- Given a bare `DismissButton`, when rendered, then it is a visually hidden `button` (only findable with `hidden: true`) carrying `aria-label="Dismiss"`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/DismissButton.test.tsx: "should have a default aria-label")
- Given `DismissButton aria-label="foo"`, when rendered, then the label override is used instead of the default. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/DismissButton.test.tsx: "should accept an aria-label")
- Given `DismissButton aria-labelledby="span-id"`, when rendered, then `aria-labelledby` points at the external span and no `aria-label` is emitted. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/DismissButton.test.tsx: "should accept an aria-labelledby")
- Given `DismissButton` with both `aria-labelledby="span-id"` and `aria-label="foo"` and its own `id="self"`, when rendered, then `aria-labelledby` becomes `"self span-id"` (the button's own id first) and `aria-label` is kept. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/DismissButton.test.tsx: "should accept an aria-labelledby and aria-label")

## aria-hide-outside

### Dismissal and layering

- Given `ariaHideOutside` called twice with different target sets (first `[button, ...radios]`, then `[button]`), when the inner call is active, then the radios are hidden too; reverting the inner call restores the radios while the button stays the only visible element for the outer call; reverting the outer call restores everything. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/ariaHideOutside.test.js: "work when called multiple times")
- Given two nested `ariaHideOutside` calls, when the revert functions are called out of order (outer first, then inner), then nothing is restored prematurely and everything is restored only after the last revert. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/ariaHideOutside.test.js: "work when called multiple times and restored out of order")

### Accessibility tooling

- Given a button between two checkboxes, when `ariaHideOutside([button])` runs, then both checkboxes get `aria-hidden="true"`, the button does not, and the checkboxes disappear from role queries; calling the returned revert function restores the original state exactly. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/ariaHideOutside.test.js: "should hide everything except the provided element [button]")
- Given several target elements passed at once, when `ariaHideOutside(checkboxes)` runs, then all listed targets stay visible and the non-target button is hidden; revert restores everything. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/ariaHideOutside.test.js: "should hide everything except multiple elements")
- Given a checkbox nested inside a wrapper div outside the target, when hiding runs, then `aria-hidden` is placed on the outer wrapper only and the walk does not descend into it to hide the child individually. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/ariaHideOutside.test.js: "should not traverse into an already hidden container")
- Given an element that already had an author-supplied `aria-hidden="true"`, when hiding runs and then reverts, then the element's own `aria-hidden` is left intact rather than being stripped on revert. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/ariaHideOutside.test.js: "should not overwrite an existing aria-hidden prop")
- Given a grid where a single `role="row"` is the target, when hiding runs, then `aria-hidden` is applied to both the sibling row and its `gridcell` child individually rather than relying on `aria-hidden` inheritance. This pins a WebKit bug (https://bugs.webkit.org/show_bug.cgi?id=222623) where `aria-hidden` is not recursive for table/grid roles. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/ariaHideOutside.test.js: "should hide everything except the provided element [row]")

### Notes and edge cases

- Given `ariaHideOutside` active, when new sibling elements are added to the DOM outside the target, then the MutationObserver hides them asynchronously and revert un-hides them. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/ariaHideOutside.test.js: "should handle when a new element is added outside while active")
- Given `ariaHideOutside` active with an already-hidden container, when a new child is added inside that container, then the child is not redundantly given `aria-hidden` (the container's attribute already covers it) while a new element added at the top level does get it. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/ariaHideOutside.test.js: "should handle when a new element is added to an already hidden container")
- Given `ariaHideOutside` active, when new content that includes an element marked `data-react-aria-top-layer="true"` is added, then the top-layer element stays visible (its `role="alert"` remains queryable) while the rest of the new content is hidden. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/ariaHideOutside.test.js: "should handle when a new element is added along with a top layer element")
- Given `ariaHideOutside` active, when a dynamically added element carries `data-react-aria-top-layer=""` (empty value, attribute presence only), then it is still treated as top layer and left unhidden while a sibling checkbox is hidden. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/ariaHideOutside.test.js: "should recognize dynamically added top layer element with data-react-aria-top-layer=\"\" (attribute presence)")
- Given a target container, when a new element is added inside that target, then the new element stays visible while outside siblings stay hidden. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/ariaHideOutside.test.js: "should handle when a new element is added inside a target element")
- Given a mutation sequence that produces a mutation record for a disconnected parent whose child is nonetheless connected through a new parent (append, remove parent, re-append child under a new parent inside the target), when the observer flushes, then the reparented list item stays visible. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/ariaHideOutside.test.js: "should handle when a new element is added and then reparented")
- Given the same reparenting dance but into a hidden sibling container, when the observer flushes, then the reparented list item is correctly hidden. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/ariaHideOutside.test.js: "should handle when a new element is added and then reparented to a hidden container")
- Given `ariaHideOutside([button])` active over a list of `role="presentation"`-wrapped rows, when the list is reordered twice, then after revert no stale `aria-hidden="true"` is left on the moved row's wrapper. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/ariaHideOutside.test.js: "should unhide after item reorder")
- Given content portalled into a shadow root with the target button inside it, when `ariaHideOutside([button])` runs (with the `enableShadowDOM` flag), then sibling checkboxes inside the shadow root are hidden while both the button and the shadow host stay unhidden. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/ariaHideOutside.test.js: "should hide everything except the provided element [button]")
- Given a modal inside a shadow root nested two divs deep, when `ariaHideOutside([modal], shadowRoot)` runs, then neither the direct parent chain of the shadow host nor `document.body` ends up effectively hidden. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/ariaHideOutside.test.js: "should not apply aria-hidden to direct parents of the shadow root")
- Given a shadow root containing both the modal and other content, plus content outside in the light DOM, when hiding runs, then sibling content inside the shadow root and the outside light-DOM content are both effectively hidden while the ancestor chain and the modal are not. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/ariaHideOutside.test.js: "should correctly apply aria-hidden based on shadow DOM structure")
- Given a `<header>` sibling in the light DOM and a modal inside a shadow root, when `ariaHideOutside([modal])` runs without passing the shadow root, then the header is effectively hidden while the shadow host's ancestor divs are not. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/ariaHideOutside.test.js: "should hide non-direct parent elements like header when modal is in Shadow DOM")
- Given a modal inside an inner shadow root nested within an outer shadow root, when `ariaHideOutside([modal], innerShadowRoot)` runs, then both light-DOM outside content and sibling content in the outer shadow root are effectively hidden while the modal is not. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/ariaHideOutside.test.js: "should handle a modal inside nested Shadow DOM structures and hide sibling content in the outer shadow root")
- Given a modal at the bottom of three levels of nested shadow roots, when `ariaHideOutside([modal])` runs, then the modal itself has no `aria-hidden` and is not effectively hidden. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/ariaHideOutside.test.js: "should handle a modal inside deeply nested Shadow DOM structures")
- Given `ariaHideOutside` active over a shadow root, when extra content is rendered into that shadow root afterwards, then the MutationObserver observing inside the shadow root gives the new element `aria-hidden="true"` after a macrotask. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/ariaHideOutside.test.js: "should handle dynamic content added to the shadow DOM after ariaHideOutside is applied")
- Given three levels of shadow roots with a modal at the deepest level plus a second light-DOM target, when `ariaHideOutside([modal, c1])` runs, then both targets and their whole ancestor chains (including shadow hosts) stay visible while unrelated siblings both in the light DOM and inside intermediate shadow roots are hidden. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/ariaHideOutside.test.js: "should hide appropriate elements including those in nested shadow roots without targets")
- Given a combobox-style pattern where an input and its popup live in separate containers inside the same shadow root, when both are passed as targets, then both targets and both of their direct containers stay visible, an unrelated sibling container is hidden, and the shadow host and its light-DOM parent stay visible. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/overlays/ariaHideOutside.test.js: "should handle input and popup pattern in shadow DOM")

## aria-modal-polyfill

### Accessibility tooling

- Given `watchModals()` installed and a Spectrum `DialogTrigger` next to an `<hr>`, when the trigger is clicked, then the dialog becomes visible, the separator is no longer reachable by role queries (the rest of the tree is made inaccessible), focus moves to the dialog, and Escape closes it and restores the separator after the exit animation. The polyfill exists because Safari/VoiceOver ignores `aria-modal`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/aria-modal-polyfill/index.test.tsx: "should hide everything except the modal")
- Given `watchModals()` and a dialog containing a nested `DialogTrigger`, when the outer dialog opens, then only the nested trigger button is reachable by role and the separator is not; when the inner dialog opens, then no button is reachable at all; pressing Escape restores the outer dialog's button, and a second Escape restores the original trigger and the separator. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/aria-modal-polyfill/index.test.tsx: "should handle nested modals")
- Given `watchModals()` and a `MenuTrigger`, when the menu opens, then the outside separator is hidden from role queries, focus is on the menu, and Escape closes the menu and restores the separator. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/aria-modal-polyfill/index.test.tsx: "should hide around Menus")
- Given `watchModals()` with `simulateMobile()` so the menu renders as a Tray, when the trigger is clicked, then the same hide/focus/Escape contract holds for the tray. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/aria-modal-polyfill/index.test.tsx: "should hide around Tray")
- Given `watchModals()` and a `DialogTrigger type="popover"`, when the popover opens, then the rest of the tree is hidden from role queries, focus is on the dialog, and Escape closes it and restores the separator. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/aria-modal-polyfill/index.test.tsx: "should hide around Popover")

## focus-scope

### Keyboard

- Given `FocusScope contain` around three inputs, when Tab is pressed repeatedly from the first input, then focus cycles input1 -> input2 -> input3 -> input1, and Shift+Tab cycles back in reverse, so Tab wraps inside the scope. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should contain focus within the scope")
- Given `FocusScope contain` whose inputs are nested at different depths, when tabbing forward and backward, then the wrap order still follows document order across the nesting. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should work with nested elements")
- Given `FocusScope contain` containing a plain div, a `hidden` input, inputs with `display: none`, `visibility: hidden`, and `visibility: collapse`, a `tabIndex={-1}` div, and a `disabled` input with `tabIndex={0}`, when tabbing, then all of those are skipped and only the three genuinely tabbable inputs are visited in both directions. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should skip non-tabbable elements")
- Given `FocusScope contain` with spans using `contentEditable`, `contentEditable="false"`, `contentEditable={false}`, and `contentEditable="plaintext-only"`, when tabbing, then the truthy `contentEditable` span and the `plaintext-only` span are tabbable while the two false variants are skipped. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should only skip content editable which are false")
- Given `FocusScope contain` with focus on the first input, when a Tab keydown arrives with `altKey: true`, then focus does not move, so modified Tab presses are left to the browser. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should do nothing if a modifier key is pressed")
- Given two sibling `FocusScope contain` scopes, when focus starts in the first scope, then tabbing wraps only within that scope; programmatically focusing an input in the second scope is rejected and focus snaps back to the first scope's last focused input, because the first scope is still the active one. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should work with multiple focus scopes")
- Given `FocusScope contain` around inputs with default values, when Tab moves focus into the next input, then the input's text is fully selected so a subsequent Delete clears the whole value. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should select all text in input when tabbing")

### Focus management

- Given `FocusScope contain` with an input outside it, when the focused input inside the scope is blurred (simulating leaving the browser) and later an outside input is focused, then focus is pushed back to the last focused element inside the scope. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should restore focus to the last focused element in the scope when re-entering the browser")
- Given `FocusScope contain`, when a `focusout` fires on the focused element inside the scope, then focus stays on that same element rather than escaping. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should restore focus to the last focused element in the scope on focus out")
- Given `FocusScope contain` and a blur with `relatedTarget: null` (which is what happens when focus moves into a child iframe), when the blur fires after `document.activeElement` has already moved to the second input, then focus is left on the second input rather than being yanked back, because the blur handler falls back to reading `document.activeElement`. Pins the iframe-click quirk where `relatedTarget` is null. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "focus properly moves into child iframe on click")
- Given `FocusScope restoreFocus autoFocus` mounted while an outside input was focused, when the scope unmounts, then focus returns to that outside input after timers flush. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should restore focus to the previously focused node on unmount")
- Given `FocusScope restoreFocus` whose middle child has `autoFocus`, when the scope mounts focus lands on that child, and when it unmounts focus returns to the previously focused outside input. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should restore focus to the previously focused node after a child with autoFocus unmounts")
- Given `FocusScope restoreFocus` (no contain) whose last child has `autoFocus`, when Tab is pressed from inside the scope, then focus moves to the element that follows the node that was focused before the scope opened, not to the scope's own next sibling. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should move focus after the previously focused node when tabbing away from a scope with autoFocus")
- Given `FocusScope restoreFocus` whose first child has `autoFocus`, when Shift+Tab is pressed, then focus moves to the element before the previously focused node. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should move focus before the previously focused node when tabbing away from a scope with Shift+Tab")
- Given `FocusScope restoreFocus autoFocus` whose children change after mount and focus moves to a newly added child, when the scope unmounts, then focus still returns to the original outside node. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should restore focus to the previously focused node after children change")
- Given `FocusScope restoreFocus autoFocus` opened from a trigger button that sits before an `after` input, when focus is moved to the last input in the scope and Tab is pressed, then focus lands on the `after` input that follows the trigger. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should move focus to the element after the previously focused node on Tab")
- Given the same trigger setup, when Shift+Tab is pressed from the scope, then focus lands on the `before` input that precedes the trigger. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should move focus to the previous element after the previously focused node on Shift+Tab")
- Given `FocusScope restoreFocus autoFocus` rendered between the trigger and an `after` input, when Tab is pressed from the last input in the scope, then focus skips the rest of the scope's own elements and lands on `after`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should skip over elements within the scope when moving focus to the next element")
- Given `FocusScope autoFocus` without `restoreFocus`, when Tab is pressed from the last input in the scope, then focus follows plain DOM order to the `after` input and the scope does not intercept the Tab. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should not handle tabbing if the focus scope does not restore focus")
- Given three stacked dialogs from the FocusScope storybook example across all four combinations of `contain` and `isPortaled`, when each dialog is opened focus lands on its own textbox and `focusScopeTree.size` grows to 4; when the middle dialog is closed focus returns to its trigger, when the first is closed focus returns to its trigger, and the tree shrinks back to 1, so a scope whose `nodeToRestore` has been removed falls back to the previous scope's restore target. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "contain=$contain, isPortaled=$isPortaled should restore focus to previous nodeToRestore when the nodeToRestore for the unmounting scope in no longer in the DOM")
- Given `FocusScope contain` around a `role="dialog"` with `tabIndex={-1}` and three buttons that remove themselves on click, when each is removed in turn, then focus moves to the next tabbable item, then to a `tabIndex={-1}` item, and finally to the focusable-but-not-tabbable dialog container itself, so restoration prefers the first focusable or tabbable element still in the scope. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should restore focus to the first focusable or tabbable element within the scope when focus is lost within the scope")
- Given two buttons that both toggle the same `FocusScope restoreFocus`, when the second button closes the scope, then focus goes to the second button (the element actually interacted with) rather than the original opener, and the same holds when the scope closes from an Escape handler while focus was inside. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should not not restore focus when active element is outside the scope")
- Given an ancestor listening for the `react-aria-focus-scope-restore` event and calling `preventDefault()`, when a `FocusScope restoreFocus` unmounts, then no restoration happens and focus ends up on `document.body`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should allow restoration to be overridden with a custom event")
- Given an outer ancestor that cancels `react-aria-focus-scope-restore`, but with an intermediate `FocusScope` between it and the unmounting inner scope, when the inner scope unmounts, then the event does not reach the outer listener and focus is restored to the trigger button normally, so the restore event does not bubble out of a nested scope. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should not bubble focus scope restoration event out of nested focus scopes")

### Notes and edge cases

- Given a `FocusScope contain` and a set of radio buttons inside a form where only one radio of a group is selectable, when tabbing forward, then the other radios in that group are skipped. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "skips radio buttons that are in the same group and are not the selectable one forwards")
- Given the same radio-group setup outside of a `<form>`, when tabbing forward, then the non-selectable radios in the group are still skipped, so grouping is resolved by name rather than by form. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "skips radio buttons that are in the same group and are not the selectable one forwards outside of a form")
- Given a radio group inside a form, when tabbing backward, then non-selectable radios in the group are skipped. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "skips radio buttons that are in the same group and are not the selectable one backwards")
- Given a radio group outside a form, when tabbing backward, then non-selectable radios in the group are skipped. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "skips radio buttons that are in the same group and are not the selectable one backwards outside of a form")
- Given a form containing exactly one radio button, when tabbing through the scope, then nothing crashes on the single-radio edge case. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "handles forms with a single radio button without crashing")
- Given `FocusScope autoFocus` whose first child is a non-focusable div, when it mounts and timers flush, then focus lands on the first tabbable element (the first input), not the div. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should auto focus the first tabbable element in the scope on mount")
- Given `FocusScope autoFocus` where a child already carries the native `autoFocus` attribute, when it mounts, then focus stays on that child rather than being moved to the first tabbable element. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should do nothing if something is already focused in the scope")
- Given `useFocusManager().focusNext()` called from items inside a `FocusScope`, when invoked repeatedly, then focus moves item1 -> item2 -> item3 and then stops at item3 because `wrap` defaults to false. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should move focus forward")
- Given `focusNext({wrap: true})`, when invoked from the last item, then focus wraps back to the first item. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should move focus forward and wrap around")
- Given `focusNext({tabbable: true})` in a scope containing a `tabIndex={-1}` item plus items with `display: none`, `visibility: hidden`, and `visibility: collapse`, when invoked from the first tabbable item, then focus jumps straight to the next `tabIndex={0}` item, skipping all of those. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should move focus forward but only to tabbable elements")
- Given `focusNext({from: e.target, tabbable: true})` called from a `role="group"` container element, when mousedown hits the second group, then focus lands on the first tabbable item inside it; when it hits the first group, then focus lands on that group's own first tabbable item, so `from` can be a container rather than a focusable node. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should move focus forward but only to tabbable elements while accounting for container elements within the scope")
- Given `focusNext({wrap: true, accept: el => !el.getAttribute('data-skip')})`, when invoked, then the `data-skip` item is skipped and focus goes from item1 to item3 and then wraps to item1. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should move focus forward and allow users to skip certain elements")
- Given `focusPrevious()`, when invoked repeatedly from the last item, then focus moves item3 -> item2 -> item1 and stops at item1 without wrapping. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should move focus backward")
- Given `focusPrevious({wrap: true})`, when invoked from the first item, then focus wraps to the last item. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should move focus backward and wrap around")
- Given `focusPrevious({tabbable: true})` with non-tabbable and hidden items in between, when invoked from the last tabbable item, then focus jumps to the first tabbable item. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should move focus backward but only to tabbable elements")
- Given `focusPrevious({from: e.target, tabbable: true})` called from group containers, when mousedown hits the second group focus lands on the first group's tabbable item; when mousedown hits the first group focus does not move, because nothing tabbable precedes it and `wrap` is false. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should move focus backward but only to tabbable elements while accounting for container elements within the scope")
- Given `focusPrevious({wrap: true, accept: el => !el.getAttribute('data-skip')})`, when invoked from the first item, then focus wraps past the skipped item to item3, and from item3 back to item1. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should move focus backward and allow users to skip certain elements")
- Given a child `FocusScope restoreFocus contain` rendered through a React portal into `document.body` from inside a parent contained scope, when a node in the child scope is focused, then the focus is accepted, so scope ancestry follows the React tree rather than the DOM tree. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should make child FocusScopes the active scope regardless of DOM structure")
- Given a contained scope with a contained child scope as its direct child, when timers flush the inner `autoFocus` wins and tabbing cycles only through the three child inputs, wrapping at both ends. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should lock tab navigation inside direct child focus scope")
