- Given a contained child scope nested two divs deep inside a contained parent scope, when tabbing, then focus still cycles only within the child scope. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should lock tab navigation inside nested child focus scope")
- Given a plain `FocusScope` (no `contain`) nested inside a contained parent scope, when tabbing from the parent input, then focus flows through all three child inputs and then wraps back to the parent input, so the containment boundary is the outer scope. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should not lock tab navigation inside a nested focus scope without contain")
- Given a nested `FocusScope restoreFocus` without `contain` inside a contained parent, when tabbing, then focus flows through the children and wraps at the outer scope's boundary. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should not lock tab navigation inside a nested focus scope with restore and not contain")
- Given three levels of nested contained scopes mounted one at a time, when each level appears, then Tab can move focus into the newest scope but attempts to focus an ancestor scope's input are rejected; when the innermost levels unmount, focus is accepted back at the deepest still-mounted scope and ancestor focus attempts are still rejected. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should restore to the correct scope on unmount")
- Given a contained parent scope with a plain `FocusScope` child rendered into a portal on `document.body`, when the portalled child is focused focus is accepted, and when the parent input is focused afterwards that is also accepted, so a non-containing child scope adds no lock. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should not lock focus inside a focus scope with a child scope in a portal")
- Given a contained parent scope with a `FocusScope contain` child in a portal, when the portalled child is focused then focusing the parent input is rejected and focus snaps back to the child. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should lock focus inside a child focus scope with contain in a portal")
- Given a plain `FocusScope` (no contain, no restore) whose `nodeToRestore` ends up being `document.body`, when Tab and Shift+Tab are pressed from inside the scope, then focus moves to the elements immediately after and before the scope in DOM order. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should navigate in and out of scope in DOM order when the nodeToRestore is the document.body")
- Given a `FocusScope restoreFocus autoFocus` whose captured `nodeToRestore` is removed in the same commit that unmounts the scope, and whose outer scope contains nothing focusable, when timers flush, then nothing throws and focus is left on `document.body`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "does not throw when there is no focusable element to restore focus to")
- Given a Spectrum `DialogContainer` chain where opening a dialog unmounts the menu that was the restore target, when the second dialog is finally closed, then focus lands back on the original "Open Menu" button rather than on `document.body`, so the scope tree re-tracks a removed `nodeToRestore` through another part of the tree. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "tracks node to restore if the node to restore was removed in another part of the tree")
- Given `FocusScope restoreFocus contain` with two buttons, when focus moves between them and then blurs, then no requestAnimationFrame callbacks are left pending after the suite's `afterAll` timer flush, so the scope cleans up its rafs on unmount. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should not leak request animation frames")
- Given `FocusScope contain` portalled into a shadow root (React 17+ with the `enableShadowDOM` flag), when the first input is focused, then `document.activeElement` is the shadow host while `shadowRoot.activeElement` is the input, and tabbing cycles the three shadow inputs with wrap. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should contain focus within the shadow DOM scope")
- Given `FocusScope contain` portalled into a shadow root nested inside another shadow root, when the first input is focused and Tab is pressed, then focus moves to the second input within the inner shadow root. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should manage focus within nested shadow DOMs")
- Given a `FocusScope restoreFocus` in the light DOM plus a separate `FocusScope restoreFocus` portalled into a shadow root, when focus is moved from the shadow input out to the light-DOM input and the shadow scope unmounts, then focus stays on the light-DOM input. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should restore focus to the element outside shadow DOM on unmount, with FocusScope outside as well")
- Given `FocusScope contain` in a shadow root with two inputs and a button, when tabbing, then focus cycles input1 -> input2 -> button -> input1 inside the shadow root. Regression test for react-spectrum issue 1472. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should autofocus and lock tab navigation inside shadow DOM")
- Given a web-component-like tree using `UNSAFE_PortalProvider` with a contained modal scope and a separate tooltip scope each in its own portal inside one shadow root, when timers flush focus autofocuses the first modal button, tabbing cycles the three modal buttons with wrap, and programmatically focusing the tooltip button is rejected and focus snaps back to the modal. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScope.test.js: "should handle web component scenario with multiple nested portals and UNSAFE_PortalProvider")
- Given `FocusScope contain` portalled into an iframe's document, when the iframe's own `user.tab()` is used, then focus cycles the three iframe inputs with wrap in both directions, so the scope listens on the element's owner document rather than the top document. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScopeOwnerDocument.test.js: "should contain focus within the scope")
- Given `FocusScope contain` inside an iframe, when a blur with `relatedTarget: null` fires after the iframe's `activeElement` already moved, then focus is left where it went rather than being pulled back. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScopeOwnerDocument.test.js: "focus properly moves into child iframe on click")
- Given `FocusScope restoreFocus autoFocus` inside an iframe that was opened while a top-document input was focused, when the scope unmounts and the iframe is removed, then focus returns to the top-document input. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScopeOwnerDocument.test.js: "should restore focus to the previously focused node on unmount")
- Given `FocusScope contain` inside an iframe wrapping a `role="dialog"` and three self-removing buttons, when each is removed, then focus falls back to the next tabbable item, then a `tabIndex={-1}` item, then the dialog container, all resolved against the iframe's document. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScopeOwnerDocument.test.js: "should restore focus to the first focusable or tabbable element within the scope when focus is lost within the scope")
- Given `FocusScope autoFocus` inside an iframe, when it mounts and timers flush, then the iframe's first tabbable input is focused. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScopeOwnerDocument.test.js: "should auto focus the first tabbable element in the scope on mount")
- Given `FocusScope autoFocus` inside an iframe whose second input has the native `autoFocus` attribute, when it mounts, then that input keeps focus. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScopeOwnerDocument.test.js: "should do nothing if something is already focused in the scope")
- Given `useFocusManager().focusNext()` inside an iframe scope, when invoked repeatedly, then focus advances through the items and stops at the last one. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScopeOwnerDocument.test.js: "should move focus forward")
- Given `focusNext({wrap: true})` inside an iframe scope, when invoked from the last item, then focus wraps to the first. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/focus/FocusScopeOwnerDocument.test.js: "should move focus forward and wrap around")

## focus-events

### Focus management

- Given `useFocus` on a `tabIndex={-1}` div, when the element is focused then blurred, then the events fire in order `focus`, `onFocusChange(true)`, `blur`, `onFocusChange(false)`, with `e.target` being the element itself. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocus.test.js: "handles focus events on the immediate target")
- Given `useFocus` on a container with a focusable child, when the child is focused and blurred, then no events fire; only focusing the container itself fires `focus`/`onFocusChange(true)`, and moving focus from the container to the child fires `blur`/`onFocusChange(false)`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocus.test.js: "does not handle focus events on children")
- Given `useFocus` with `isDisabled`, when the element is focused and blurred, then no focus events fire at all. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocus.test.js: "does not handle focus events if disabled")
- Given a wrapper div with its own `onFocus`/`onBlur` around a `useFocus` element whose handlers call `stopPropagation()`, when the inner element is focused and blurred, then the inner handlers fire once each and the wrapper's never fire. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocus.test.js: "events do not bubble when stopPropagation is called")
- Given the same wrapper setup without `stopPropagation`, when the inner element is focused and blurred, then both inner and wrapper handlers fire once each, so React Aria's focus events bubble by default. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocus.test.js: "events bubble by default")
- Given a focused button wired with `useFocus`, when the button is re-rendered with `disabled`, then `onBlur` fires asynchronously via a MutationObserver, because browsers do not emit a real blur event when the focused element becomes disabled. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocus.test.js: "should fire onBlur when a focused element is disabled")

### Notes and edge cases

- Given a `useFocus` element portalled into a shadow root with the `enableShadowDOM` flag on, when it is focused and blurred, then the same `focus`/`focuschange(true)`/`blur`/`focuschange(false)` sequence fires with the shadow element as the target. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocus.test.js: "handles focus events")
- Given a `useFocus` element inside a shadow root with `isDisabled`, when focused and blurred, then no events fire. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocus.test.js: "does not handle focus events if disabled")
- Given a wrapper and inner `useFocus` element both inside a shadow root where the inner handlers call `stopPropagation()`, when focus moves, then only the inner handlers fire. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocus.test.js: "events do not bubble when stopPropagation is called")
- Given the same shadow-root setup without `stopPropagation`, when focus moves, then both inner and wrapper handlers fire once, so bubbling works across the shadow boundary. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocus.test.js: "events bubble by default")

## focus-within

### Focus management

- Given `useFocusWithin` on a `tabIndex={-1}` div with no children, when the div is focused then blurred, then events fire in order `focus`, `onFocusWithinChange(true)`, `blur`, `onFocusWithinChange(false)`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocusWithin.test.js: "handles focus events on the target itself")
- Given `useFocusWithin` on a container with a focusable child, when focus enters the child, moves to the container, moves back to the child, and finally leaves, then only one `focus` (targeting the child) and one `blur` fire, with no spurious events while focus moves between the container and its descendants. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocusWithin.test.js: "does handle focus events on children")
- Given `useFocusWithin` with `isDisabled`, when a child is focused and blurred, then no events fire. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocusWithin.test.js: "does not handle focus events if disabled")
- Given a wrapper with `onFocus`/`onBlur` around a `useFocusWithin` container whose handlers call `stopPropagation()`, when a child is focused and blurred, then only the inner handlers fire. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocusWithin.test.js: "events do not bubble when stopPropagation is called")
- Given the same setup without `stopPropagation`, when a child is focused and blurred, then both inner and wrapper handlers fire once. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocusWithin.test.js: "events bubble by default")
- Given a focused button inside a `useFocusWithin` container, when the button becomes `disabled`, then `onBlurWithin` fires asynchronously through a MutationObserver, covering the browser gap where disabling the focused element emits no blur. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocusWithin.test.js: "should fire onBlur when a focused element is disabled")
- Given a `useFocusWithin` container whose only focusable child removes itself on click, when the child is focused, clicked away into nothing, and then focus lands on an input outside, then `onBlurWithin` fires with the container element as the target rather than the removed child. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocusWithin.test.js: "should fire onBlur when focus occurs outside")

## focus-visible

### Keyboard

- Given `useFocusVisibleListener(fn, [])` with no text-input option, when keydown/keyup for `a`, keydown/keyup for `Escape`, then mousedown and mouseup fire on the body, then the callback is invoked 5 times with `[true, true, true, true, false]`: every keydown and keyup flips to keyboard modality, mousedown flips to pointer, and mouseup alone does not notify. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocusVisible.test.js: "emits on modality change (non-text input)")
- Given `useFocusVisibleListener(fn, [], {isTextInput: true})`, when the same key and mouse sequence fires, then the callback is invoked only 3 times with `[true, true, false]`, because printable-character keys such as `a` do not count as focus-visible modality changes for text inputs while `Escape` does. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocusVisible.test.js: "emits on modality change (text input)")

### Focus management

- Given a `useFocusVisible` element focused by Tab, when browser tabs are toggled away and back using Chrome's event order (element blur, window blur, visibilitychange hidden, visibilitychange visible, window focus, element focus), then `isFocusVisible` is still true. Pins Chrome-specific visibilitychange ordering. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocusVisible.test.js: "returns positive isFocusVisible result after toggling browser tabs after keyboard navigation")
- Given the element was last interacted with by a real click rather than the keyboard, when browser tabs are toggled with Chrome's event order, then `isFocusVisible` is false. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocusVisible.test.js: "returns negative isFocusVisible result after toggling browser tabs without prior keyboard navigation")
- Given keyboard focus, when tabs are toggled using Safari's event order (Safari fires the window/element focus pair twice and puts visibilitychange after all focus events), then `isFocusVisible` remains true. Pins Safari bug tracked as react-spectrum issue 7468. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocusVisible.test.js: "returns positive isFocusVisible result after toggling browser tabs in Safari after keyboard navigation")
- Given pointer focus, when tabs are toggled with Safari's doubled focus-event order, then `isFocusVisible` is false. Pins the same Safari quirk. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocusVisible.test.js: "returns negative isFocusVisible result after toggling browser tabs in Safari without prior keyboard navigation")
- Given keyboard focus, when the browser window loses and regains focus (window blur then window focus with no visibilitychange), then `isFocusVisible` remains true. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocusVisible.test.js: "returns positive isFocusVisible result after toggling browser window after keyboard navigation")
- Given pointer focus, when the browser window loses and regains focus, then `isFocusVisible` is false. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocusVisible.test.js: "returns negative isFocusVisible result after toggling browser window without prior keyboard navigation")

### State and controlled/uncontrolled

- Given two `useFocusRing` buttons and instrumented `changeHandlers`, when nothing is focused no modality handlers are registered; tabbing to button 1 registers exactly one handler and sets `data-focus-visible` on it; tabbing to button 2 swaps to a different single handler and moves `data-focus-visible`; a subsequent click calls only button 2's handler and never the removed button 1 handler, and neither button ends up focus-visible. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocusVisible.test.js: "does not call changeHandlers when unneeded")

### Notes and edge cases

- Given an iframe whose `HTMLElement.prototype.focus` has been redefined as an accessor-only property with no writable value, when `addWindowFocusTracking(iframeRoot)` runs, then it does not throw. Regression test for react-spectrum issue 9649, which some third-party scripts trigger by patching `focus`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocusVisible.test.js: "does not throw when HTMLElement.prototype.focus is an accessor-only property")
- Given a `useFocusVisible` element rendered into an iframe, when it is clicked before `addWindowFocusTracking` is called, then it is not focus-visible; after `addWindowFocusTracking(iframeRoot)` a key press inside the iframe makes it focus-visible, so listeners must be installed per window. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocusVisible.test.js: "sets up focus listener in a different window")
- Given tracking installed in an iframe, when a `beforeunload` event fires on the iframe window, then later keyboard events no longer flip `isFocusVisible`, so listeners are torn down on unload. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocusVisible.test.js: "removes event listeners on beforeunload")
- Given tracking installed in an iframe, when the teardown function returned by `addWindowFocusTracking` is called, then later keyboard events no longer flip `isFocusVisible`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocusVisible.test.js: "removes event listeners using teardown function")
- Given the module-level `hasSetupGlobalListeners` map with only the top window registered, when tracking is added for an iframe the map grows to 2, and when the iframe fires `beforeunload` the iframe window entry is removed, leaving only the top window. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocusVisible.test.js: "removes the window object from the hasSetupGlobalListeners object on beforeunload")
- Given the same map, when the teardown function is called preemptively, then the iframe window entry is removed. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocusVisible.test.js: "removes the window object from the hasSetupGlobalListeners object if we preemptively tear down")
- Given a keyboard-focused element inside a tracked iframe, when tabs are toggled on the iframe's own window, then `isFocusVisible` stays true because the iframe shares the tab. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocusVisible.test.js: "returns positive isFocusVisible result after toggling browser tabs after keyboard navigation")
- Given a keyboard-focused element inside a tracked iframe, when it is then clicked, then `isFocusVisible` becomes false. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocusVisible.test.js: "returns negative isFocusVisible result after toggling browser tabs without prior keyboard navigation")
- Given a keyboard-focused element inside a tracked iframe, when the top browser window is blurred and refocused, then `isFocusVisible` stays true. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocusVisible.test.js: "returns positive isFocusVisible result after toggling browser window after keyboard navigation")
- Given an element inside a tracked iframe that was clicked, when the top window is blurred and refocused, then `isFocusVisible` is false. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocusVisible.test.js: "returns negative isFocusVisible result after toggling browser window without prior keyboard navigation")
- Given a `useFocusRing` button inside a tracked iframe, when it is clicked with the left mouse button and then Escape is pressed, then it becomes focus-visible, so a keypress after a pointer interaction switches the modality even across the iframe boundary. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useFocusVisible.test.js: "correctly shifts focus to the iframe when the iframe is focused")

## focusable

### Focus management

- Given a `button` appended to the document, when `isFocusable` is called on it, then it returns true. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/isFocusable.test.tsx: "detects focusable elements in the document")
- Given a button in a detached document created via `document.implementation.createHTMLDocument('')` (so `ownerDocument.defaultView` is `null`), when `isFocusable` is called with no options and with `{skipVisibilityCheck: true}`, then neither call throws and the skip-visibility call returns true. This pins the crash that would otherwise hit the dev-only `useFocusable` assertion for elements not attached to a live window. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/isFocusable.test.tsx: "does not throw for isFocusable")
- Given a button in a detached document, when `isTabbable` is called, then it does not throw. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/isFocusable.test.tsx: "does not throw for isTabbable")
- Given a button in a detached document nested inside an `inert` wrapper, when `isFocusable(button, {skipVisibilityCheck: true})` is called, then it does not throw. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/isFocusable.test.tsx: "does not throw when an ancestor is inert")
- Given `<Focusable>` wrapping a `<span role="button">`, when rendered, then the child gets `tabindex="0"`; tabbing to it fires `onFocus`, typing fires `onKeyDown`, and tabbing away fires `onBlur`, so the wrapper injects focusable props into an arbitrary child. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/Focusable.test.js: "should apply focusable props to child element")
- Given `<Focusable isDisabled>`, when rendered, then the child has no `tabindex` at all. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/Focusable.test.js: "supports isDisabled")
- Given `<Focusable excludeFromTabOrder>`, when rendered, then the child gets `tabindex="-1"` so it is focusable but not tabbable. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/Focusable.test.js: "supports excludeFromTabOrder")
- Given `<Focusable autoFocus>`, when rendered, then the child is the active element. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/Focusable.test.js: "supports autoFocus")
- Given `<Focusable>` wrapping a `<button tabIndex={-1}>`, when rendered, then the child's own `tabIndex` wins over the injected one. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/Focusable.test.js: "should should merge with existing props")
- Given `<Focusable ref={ref1}>` wrapping a `<button ref={ref2}>`, when rendered, then both refs point at the same button element. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/Focusable.test.js: "should should merge with existing ref")
- Given `<Focusable>` wrapping a component that does not forward its ref, when rendered, then `console.error` is called with `<Focusable> child must forward its ref to a DOM element.`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/Focusable.test.js: "should error if component does not forward its ref")
- Given `<Focusable>` wrapping a component whose `useImperativeHandle` exposes a non-DOM object, when rendered, then the same `<Focusable> child must forward its ref to a DOM element.` error is logged. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/Focusable.test.js: "should error if component does not forward its ref to a DOM element")
- Given `<Focusable>` wrapping a forwarded `<span role="button">` that drops `tabIndex`, when rendered, then `console.warn` is called with `<Focusable> child must be focusable. Please ensure the tabIndex prop is passed through.`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/Focusable.test.js: "should warn if child is not focusable")
- Given `<Focusable>` inside a `hidden` subtree, when rendered, then the "child must be focusable" warning is suppressed, because hidden elements are legitimately not focusable. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/Focusable.test.js: "should not warn if focusable child is inside a hidden subtree")
- Given `<Focusable>` wrapping a `<span>` with no `role`, when rendered, then `console.warn` is called with `<Focusable> child must have an interactive ARIA role.`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/Focusable.test.js: "should warn if child does not have a role")
- Given `<Focusable>` wrapping a `<span role="presentation">`, when rendered, then the warning names the offending role: `<Focusable> child must have an interactive ARIA role. Got "presentation".`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/Focusable.test.js: "should warn if child does not have an interactive role")

## focus-safely

### Focus management

- Given `setInteractionModality('virtual')` and a button that gets unmounted inside a `requestAnimationFrame` callback, when `focusSafely(button)` is called and timers flush, then `focusWithoutScrolling` is never called, because virtual modality defers the focus to the next frame and the element is no longer connected by then. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/focusSafely.test.js: "should not focus on the element if it's no longer connected")
- Given virtual modality and a button that stays mounted, when `focusSafely(button)` is called and timers flush, then `focusWithoutScrolling` is called exactly once. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/focusSafely.test.js: "should focus on the element if it's connected")
- Given virtual modality and a button portalled into a shadow root that is removed before the deferred frame runs, when `focusSafely` is called, then `focusWithoutScrolling` is never called, so the connectedness check works across shadow boundaries. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/focusSafely.test.js: "should not focus on the element if it's no longer connected within shadow DOM")
- Given virtual modality and a button portalled into a shadow root that remains connected, when `focusSafely` is called, then `focusWithoutScrolling` is called once. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/focusSafely.test.js: "should focus on the element if it's connected within shadow DOM")

## shadow-dom-focus

### Pointer (mouse, touch, pen, virtual click from screen readers)

- Given a React Aria Components `ComboBox` mounted inside a real shadow root in a real browser, when the input is clicked focus lands on the input, when the chevron trigger is clicked the listbox opens and focus stays on the input rather than moving to the button, and when an option in the light-DOM-portalled listbox is clicked the listbox closes and the input value updates. Exercises `preventFocusOnPress` against real shadow-DOM focus retargeting, which jsdom cannot reproduce; skipped on Firefox because of a Firefox focus-event leak. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ShadowDOMFocus.browser.test.tsx: "ComboBox opens by clicking its trigger, keeps focus in the input, and selects an option inside a shadow root")
- Given a React Aria Components `NumberField` inside a real shadow root, when the increment stepper button is clicked twice, then the value goes 0 -> 1 -> 2 and focus stays in the input each time, because the stepper uses `preventFocusOnPress`. Skipped on Firefox. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ShadowDOMFocus.browser.test.tsx: "NumberField keeps focus in the input while clicking the stepper inside a shadow root")
- Given a `MenuTrigger` inside a shadow root whose popover portals into another container in the same shadow root via `UNSAFE_PortalProvider`, when the trigger is clicked the menu opens and stays open, and clicking a menu item fires `onAction` once. Skipped on Firefox. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria-components/test/ShadowDOMFocus.browser.test.tsx: "Menu opens from its trigger and fires onAction with the overlay portaled into the same shadow root")

### Focus management

- Given a shadow-free DOM of alternating divs and inputs, when a `createShadowTreeWalker` is stepped with `firstChild`, `nextNode`, `previousNode`, and `lastChild`, then every returned node and the resulting `currentNode` match a native `document.createTreeWalker` exactly. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/shadowTreeWalker.test.tsx: "walks through the dom")
- Given a shadow-free DOM and a filter function that accepts only `INPUT` elements, when the shadow tree walker is stepped, then its results match a native tree walker with the same filter. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/shadowTreeWalker.test.tsx: "walks through the dom with a filter function")
- Given nested DOM and a filter object (rather than a function), when the shadow tree walker is stepped, then its results match a native tree walker with that filter object. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/shadowTreeWalker.test.tsx: "walks through nested dom with a filter object")
- Given content portalled into a shadow root at the document root with an input-only filter, when `createShadowTreeWalker(document, document.body, undefined, filterFn)` is stepped, then it descends into the shadow root and matches a native tree walker rooted at that shadow root. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/shadowTreeWalker.test.tsx: "walks through the dom with shadow dom")
- Given two peer-level shadow roots each holding focusable content, when the shadow tree walker is stepped, then it walks into and out of both shadow roots in document order. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/shadowTreeWalker.test.tsx: "walks through the dom with multiple peer level shadow doms")
- Given shadow roots nested inside other shadow roots, when the shadow tree walker is stepped, then it descends through each level and back out again in order. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/shadowTreeWalker.test.tsx: "walks through the dom with multiple nested shadow doms")

## press

### Keyboard

- Given a non-link pressable element (a plain div with usePress), when keyDown then keyUp fire with `key: ' '` (Space), then pressstart/presschange(true)/pressup/pressend/presschange(false)/press/click fire with pointerType:'keyboard' and the payload includes the `key` field (' '), plus a synthesized `click` event. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should fire press events when the element is not a link")
- Given the pressable element is rendered as `<a href="#">` (an actual link), when Space is keyDown/keyUp'd, then NO press events fire at all (Space is a no-op on links); when Enter is keyDown'd (browser fires a native click as default action, simulated by manually firing `click` when keydown's return value allows it) and keyUp'd, then pressstart/presschange(true)/pressup/pressend/presschange(false)/press fire with key:'Enter', and the onClick handler (which calls preventDefault) fires a plain `{type:'click'}` event. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should fire press events when the element is a link")
- Given an element with `role="link"` (not a real anchor), when Space is keyDown/keyUp'd, then no press events fire (Space is ignored on role="link" just like real links); when Enter is keyDown/keyUp'd, then the full pressstart/presschange(true)/pressup/pressend/presschange(false)/press/click sequence fires with key:'Enter' -- Enter triggers press on role="link" elements without needing a real native click. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should fire press events on Enter when the element role is link")
- Given an `<a href="#" role="button">` element, when Space is keyDown/keyUp'd, then usePress explicitly invokes the DOM `.click()` method (rather than relying on native default action) producing the full pressstart/presschange(true)/pressup/pressend/presschange(false)/press/click sequence with key:' ' -- pinning that role="button" links need Space to work like a button via explicit click() since browsers don't natively fire click for Space on anchors. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should explicitly call click method when Space key is triggered on a link with href and role=\"button\"")
- Given a non-link pressable element, when keyDown fires with `shiftKey:true` and keyUp fires with `ctrlKey:true`, then pressstart reports shiftKey:true, and pressup/pressend/press/click report ctrlKey:true, matching the modifier state of each underlying keyboard event. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should handle modifier keys")
- Given `window.navigator.platform` mocked to 'Mac' (via `jest.spyOn(...'platform','get')`), when keyDown fires for 'Meta', then keyDown fires for 'Enter' with metaKey:true, then only keyUp for 'Meta' fires (no keyup for 'Enter', simulating that macOS never fires a keyup while the Meta key is held), then usePress still fires the complete pressstart/presschange(true)/pressup/pressend/presschange(false)/press/click sequence for the Enter press, treating the Meta keyup as the trigger to end the press -- pinning a macOS-specific browser bug workaround. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should fire press events when Meta key is held to work around macOS bug")
- Given a non-link pressable element, when keyDown with Space fires on the element but the matching keyUp fires on `document.body` instead (focus moved away between keydown and keyup), then pressend/presschange(false) still fire (ending the press with no pressup/press since it didn't complete on the original element); a subsequent full keyDown/keyUp Space cycle on the element then correctly fires pressstart/presschange(true)/pressup/pressend/presschange(false)/press/click, confirming internal `isPressed` state was properly reset by the body keyup rather than getting stuck. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should handle when focus moves between keydown and keyup")
- Given a non-link pressable element, when a `keyDown` fires with `repeat: true` (OS key-repeat) for Space, followed by keyUp on `document.body`, then no press events fire at all -- repeating keydown events are ignored. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should ignore repeating keyboard events")
- Given the pressable element is rendered as `<input type="checkbox">`, when Enter is keyDown/keyUp'd, then no press events fire (Enter does nothing on a checkbox); when Space is keyDown'd, then keyDown returns a truthy/allowed value (native default is NOT prevented, i.e. usePress does not call preventDefault for Space on a checkbox) and pressstart/presschange(true) fire; when Space is keyUp'd, then that also returns truthy/allowed and pressup/pressend/presschange(false)/press/click fire -- confirming checkboxes get Space-triggered press without usePress blocking the browser's native checkbox-toggling default action. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should fire press events on checkboxes but not prevent default")
- Given a mocked `getBoundingClientRect` returning a 100x100 rect at origin (0,0), in the keyboard activation path, when Space is keyDown/keyUp'd (no pointer coordinates available), then every emitted press event (pressstart/pressup/pressend/press) reports x:50, y:50 -- the center of the element's bounding rect -- rather than 0,0, confirming keyboard-triggered presses report coordinates as the target's center. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should return the center of the element when keyboard pressed")

### Pointer (mouse, touch, pen, virtual click from screen readers)

- Given a pressable element using real PointerEvent support (`installPointerEvent`, pointer-events world) with pointerType=mouse, when pointerover, pointerdown, native mousedown+focus, pointerup, native mouseup, then click fire in sequence, then onPressStart fires with {target, pointerType:'mouse', ctrlKey/metaKey/shiftKey/altKey:false, x:0,y:0}, onPressChange(true), onPressUp, onPressEnd, onPressChange(false), onPress, then the native onClick fires last, all before pointerout. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should fire press events based on pointer events with pointerType=mouse")
- Given a pressable element in the pointer-events world with pointerType=touch, when pointerover/pointerdown/touchstart fire (mousedown and focus are delayed until after pointerup on touch devices), then pointerup/pointerout/touchend/mousedown/focus/mouseup/click fire afterward, then all press events report pointerType:'touch' and the same pressstart/presschange/pressup/pressend/presschange/press/click ordering occurs. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should fire press events based on pointer events with pointerType=touch")
- Given a touch press that ends via pointerup/pointerout/touchend without the browser ever firing a compatibility mousedown/mouseup/click (simulating a long press where onClick is not fired by the browser), when the press completes, then usePress still synthesizes the full pressstart/presschange/pressup/pressend/presschange/press/click sequence and, after 90ms advance, the element still becomes document.activeElement even though no mouse events fired. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should fire press events on long press even if onClick is not fired by the browser")
- Given a nested inner element whose own onClick handler calls stopPropagation, when pointerdown/mousedown/focus/pointerup/mouseup/click fire on that inner element (with pointerType=mouse), then usePress on the outer wrapping element still receives pressstart/presschange(true)/pressend/presschange(false) but never receives onPress or onClick because the click event's propagation was stopped before reaching it. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should cancel press if onClick propagation is stopped")
- Given `el.hasPointerCapture` mocked to return true, when pointerdown fires, then usePress calls `hasPointerCapture(pointerId)` and `releasePointerCapture(pointerId)` on the target; when pointerout then fires with the pointer moved outside (clientX/Y 100,100) and pointerup is dispatched on `document` (not the element) followed by pointerover back on the element, then pressend/presschange(false) fire immediately on pointerout with x/y at the exit coordinates (not on the later document pointerup), and a fresh pointerdown/pointermove-out/pointermove-back-in/pointerover sequence produces cancel-then-restart press events (pressend at exit coords, then a new pressstart/presschange(true) on return, ending with pressup/pressend/presschange(false)/press/click on the final pointerup+click at the origin). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should fire press change events when moving pointer outside target")
- Given `el.hasPointerCapture` mocked to return false, when pointerdown fires, then usePress still calls `hasPointerCapture(pointerId)` but does NOT call `releasePointerCapture`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should not call releasePointerCapture when hasPointerCapture returns false")
- Given a pointer-events-world press in progress, when a pointercancel event fires, then onPressEnd/onPressChange(false) fire (no onPressUp, no onPress) ending the press without invoking the press action. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should handle pointer cancel events")
- Given a pointer-events-world press in progress, when a native `dragstart` MouseEvent fires on the target, then the press is cancelled: onPressEnd/onPressChange(false) fire with no onPressUp/onPress/click. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should cancel press on dragstart")
- Given `shouldCancelOnPointerExit` is set on a pointer-events-world press, when the pointer moves outside the target (pointermove then pointerout) and then re-enters (pointermove then pointerover), then the press is fully cancelled on exit (pressend/presschange(false) at the exit coordinates) and does NOT restart on re-entry, unlike the default behavior which restarts press on re-entry. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should cancel press when moving outside and the shouldCancelOnPointerExit option is set")
- Given a pointer-events-world mouse press, when pointerdown carries shiftKey:true, pointerup carries ctrlKey:true, and the subsequent click carries ctrlKey:true, then each emitted press event reports the modifier keys exactly as they were on the underlying native event at that moment (pressstart has shiftKey:true/ctrlKey:false, pressup/pressend/press have ctrlKey:true/shiftKey:false, and the onClick payload reports ctrlKey:true). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should handle modifier keys")
- Given a pointer-events-world press, when pointerdown/pointerup/click are fired with `button: 1` (non-left button), then no press events fire at all (events array stays empty), i.e. usePress only handles left clicks. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should only handle left clicks")
- Given `preventFocusOnPress` is set, when pointerdown then pointerup fire (pointer-events world), then the target element does not receive DOM focus (`document.activeElement` is not the element). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should not focus the target on click if preventFocusOnPress is true")
- Given a pointerdown/pointerup pair with `width: 0, height: 0` (the signature of a virtual/screen-reader-generated pointer event) and pointerType reported as 'mouse', when those events fire, then no press events fire yet (empty events array); only when the subsequent synthetic `click` fires does usePress synthesize the full pressstart/pressup/pressend/press/click sequence, reporting pointerType:'virtual' for every press event -- i.e. zero-size pointer events are treated as virtual clicks from assistive tech and press logic is deferred to the click event. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should ignore virtual pointer events")
- Given `navigator.userAgent` is mocked to report 'Android' (using `jest.spyOn(navigator, 'userAgent', 'get')`), when the same zero-size (`width:0,height:0`) pointerdown/pointerup/click sequence fires, then usePress does NOT treat it as virtual -- it reports pointerType:'mouse' (not 'virtual') for all press events, and fires the full pressstart/pressup/pressend/press/click sequence already on the pointerdown/pointerup pair (not deferred to click) -- pinning an Android userAgent quirk where zero width/height pointer events must be treated as real presses, not screen-reader virtual clicks. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should not ignore virtual pointer events on android ")
- Given `navigator.userAgent` mocked to 'Android', when a pointerdown/pointerup pair fires with `width:1, height:1, pressure:0, detail:0` (the shape Android TalkBack occasionally emits for a double-tap activation instead of width:0/height:0), then no press events fire on the pointer events themselves, and only the subsequent `click` (fired with pointerType:'mouse', width:1, height:1, detail:1) produces the full press sequence with pointerType reported as 'virtual' -- pinning an Android TalkBack quirk where pressure:0/detail:0 plus width/height:1 must still be detected as a virtual screen-reader click even on Android. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should detect Android TalkBack double tap")
- Given a non-Android userAgent, when a pointerdown/pointerup pair fires with `width:1, height:1, pressure:0, detail:0`, then press events DO fire immediately (events array is non-empty) -- confirming pressure:0 alone does not trigger the virtual-click heuristic off of Android. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should fire if pressure is 0 but is not android")
- Given `isDisabled` is set on the pressable element, when pointerdown/pointerup/click fire (pointer-events world), then no press or click events fire at all. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should not fire press/click events for disabled elements")
- Given a pointer-events-world press where pointerdown/pointermove/pointerup all report a large `width:20,height:20` hit-target and the pointerup coordinates (10,10) are offset from pointerdown (0,0) but still within/near the target's bounding box, when pointerup then click fire, then onPress still fires -- pressing up near (but not exactly on) the target counts as a completed press for large-hit-area pointer input. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should fire press event when pointerup close to the target")
- Given a pointer-events-world press, when pointerdown fires, then the element gets inline `user-select: none` style applied; when pointerup then click fire, then that `user-select: none` style is removed from the element. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should add/remove user-select: none to the element on pointer down/up")
- Given the fallback (non-pointer-event) mouse-events world, when mousedown/focus/mouseup/click fire on the target with detail:1, then the same pressstart(pointerType:'mouse')/presschange(true)/pressup/pressend/presschange(false)/press/click sequence fires as in the pointer-events world, and both mouseDown and click return `true` (were not prevented) confirming default browser focus/click behavior is preserved. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should fire press events based on mouse events")
- Given the mouse-events world, when mousedown then mouseleave fire, then pressend/presschange(false) fire immediately on mouseleave (before any mouseup), and a mouseup dispatched on `document.body` (outside the element) with different coordinates does not restart the press, and a following mouseenter on the element also does not restart it -- the press stays cancelled once the mouse leaves. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should fire press change events when moving mouse outside target")
- Given the mouse-events world, when mousedown/mouseleave/mouseenter fire and THEN mouseup/click fire on the element itself, then the first mouseleave cancels the press (pressend/presschange(false)) but the subsequent mouseenter while still logically "down" restarts a fresh pressstart/presschange(true), and the final mouseup+click on the element completes that second press with pressup/pressend/presschange(false)/press/click -- i.e. re-entering the element (mouse events world, no shouldCancelOnPointerExit) resumes the press. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should fire press change events when moving mouse outside target")
- Given `shouldCancelOnPointerExit` in the mouse-events world, when mousedown/mouseleave/mouseenter fire, then the press is cancelled on mouseleave (pressend/presschange(false)) and does NOT restart on the following mouseenter, unlike the default mouse-events behavior. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should cancel press when moving outside and the shouldCancelOnPointerExit option is set")
- Given the mouse-events world, when mousedown carries metaKey:true and mouseup/click carry shiftKey:true, then pressstart reports metaKey:true, and pressup/pressend/press/click report shiftKey:true, each reflecting the modifier state of its own underlying native event. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should handle modifier keys")
- Given the mouse-events world, when mousedown/mouseup/click all fire with `button:1`, then no press events fire at all (only left-button clicks are handled). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should only handle left clicks")
- Given `preventFocusOnPress` in the mouse-events world, when mousedown/mouseup/click fire, then `document.activeElement` is not the target. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should not focus the element on click if preventFocusOnPress is true")
- Given default props in the mouse-events world, when mousedown returns a truthy "should focus" signal and the test manually focuses the element, followed by mouseup/click, then `document.activeElement` becomes the target -- confirming default (non-prevented) click focuses the element. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should focus the element on click by default")
- Given the mouse-events world, when mousedown fires then a native `dragstart` MouseEvent fires, then the press is cancelled (pressend/presschange(false), no pressup/press/click). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should cancel press on dragstart")
- Given the touch-events world (fireEvent.touchStart/touchEnd with targetTouches/changedTouches), when touchStart then touchEnd fire on the same coordinates, then pressstart/presschange(true)/pressup/pressend/presschange(false)/press/click fire with pointerType:'touch'. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should fire press events based on touch events")
- Given the touch-events world, when touchStart then touchMove to (100,100) then touchEnd at (100,100) fire, then the press is cancelled the moment the touch moves outside the target (pressend/presschange(false)) with no pressup/press; a following fresh touchStart/touchMove-back-in/touchMove-to-origin/touchEnd sequence produces cancel followed by a full new pressstart/presschange(true)/pressup/pressend/presschange(false)/press/click, mirroring the pointer-events re-entry restart behavior for touch. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should fire press change events when moving touch outside target")
- Given `shouldCancelOnPointerExit` in the touch-events world, when touchStart/touchMove-outside/touchMove-back-inside fire, then the press stays cancelled after leaving and does not restart on returning (pressend/presschange(false) once, nothing further). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should cancel press when moving outside and the shouldCancelOnPointerExit option is set")
- Given a touch-events-world press in progress, when the browser also fires emulated compatibility mouse events (mousedown/mouseleave/mouseenter/mouseup/click) interleaved with the touch sequence (touchStart/touchMove-out/touchMove-in/touchEnd), then usePress ignores those emulated mouse events entirely and derives press state purely from the touch sequence, still reporting pointerType:'touch' throughout and producing the same cancel-on-exit/restart-on-reentry/complete-on-touchEnd event sequence as pure touch interaction. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should ignore emulated mouse events")
- Given a touch-events-world press in progress, when a `touchcancel` event fires followed by `touchend`, then the press is cancelled on touchcancel (pressend/presschange(false)) and the subsequent touchend does not re-fire pressup/press. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should handle touch cancel events")
- Given a touch-events-world press in progress, when a `scroll` event fires on `document.body`, then the press is cancelled (pressend/presschange(false)) even though the touch never left the target and touchend follows. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should cancel press after scroll events")
- Given a touch-events-world press in progress and a sibling scrollable element exists elsewhere in the DOM, when a `scroll` event fires on that unrelated sibling element (not an ancestor of the pressed target), then the press is NOT cancelled -- pressup/pressend/press/click all still fire normally on touchend, showing scroll-cancellation only applies to scrolls that could affect the pressed target. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should not cancel press after scroll events in unrelated scrollable regions")
- Given a touch-events-world press in progress, when a native `dragstart` MouseEvent fires, then the press is cancelled (pressend/presschange(false), no pressup/press/click). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should cancel press on dragstart")
- Given `preventFocusOnPress` in the touch-events world, when touchStart then touchEnd fire, then `document.activeElement` is not the target. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should not focus the target if preventFocusOnPress is true")
- Given default props in the touch-events world, when touchStart and touchEnd both return a truthy "should focus" signal and the element is then focused, then `document.activeElement` becomes the target, confirming default touch interaction focuses the pressed element. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should focus the target on touch by default")
- Given a touch-events-world press where touchStart/touchMove/touchEnd all report a `radiusX:15, radiusY:15` touch contact area and the touchEnd coordinates (10,10) are offset from touchStart (0,0), when touchEnd fires, then onPress still fires -- lifting near (not exactly on) the start point within the touch contact radius still counts as a completed press. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should fire press event when touchup close to the target")
- Given a plain pressable div (no real pointerdown/mousedown precedes it) in the fallback events world, when only a `click` event fires (no prior pointer/mouse/touch/keyboard event, simulating a virtual click dispatched directly by a screen reader), then usePress synthesizes the full pressstart/presschange(true)/pressup/pressend/presschange(false)/press/click sequence with pointerType:'virtual' and x/y:0. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should fire press events events for virtual click events from screen readers")
- Given an `onPressUp` handler that itself calls `e.target.click()` synchronously (simulating a synthetic click triggered from within a press-up handler, as could happen when a screen reader replays an event), when `mouseUp` is fired directly on the element (without a preceding mousedown, simulating the event arriving from elsewhere), then only the single pressup event fires and no additional/duplicate press cycle is triggered by the synthetic click dispatched inside the handler -- usePress ignores synthetic events fired reentrantly during its own onPressUp callback. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should ignore synthetic events fired during an onPressUp event")
- Given a pressable element rendered inside a real Shadow DOM (`createShadowRoot()` container, `enableShadowDOM()` flag on, pointer-events world), when pointerdown/pointerup/click fire on the shadow-root-scoped element (looked up via `shadowRoot.getElementById`), then pressstart/presschange(true)/pressup/pressend/presschange(false)/press fire with pointerType:'mouse', matching the light-DOM pointer-events behavior across the shadow boundary. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should fire press events based on pointer events")
- Given the same Shadow DOM setup with `hasPointerCapture`/`releasePointerCapture` mocked, when pointerdown fires then the element's `hasPointerCapture`/`releasePointerCapture` are called with the pointerId, and moving the pointer out (pointerout) while a pointerup is dispatched on the outer `document` (crossing the shadow boundary) still correctly cancels the press (pressend/presschange(false)); re-entering afterward (a second pointerdown/pointermove-out/pointermove-in/pointerover/pointerup/click cycle) restarts and completes a new press -- confirming pointer-exit/re-entry press semantics work correctly across the shadow DOM boundary. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should fire press change events when moving pointer outside target")
- Given the Shadow DOM setup, when a pointercancel fires after pointerdown inside the shadow root, then the press is cancelled (pressend/presschange(false), no pressup/press) same as in light DOM. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should handle pointer cancel events")
- Given the Shadow DOM setup, when a native `dragstart` fires after pointerdown inside the shadow root, then the press is cancelled (pressend/presschange(false)), matching light-DOM dragstart-cancels-press behavior inside a shadow tree. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should cancel press on dragstart")
- Given `shouldCancelOnPointerExit` inside Shadow DOM, when pointerdown fires inside the shadow root, pointerout moves outside, and a pointerup is dispatched on `document.body` (outside the shadow tree entirely), then the press correctly cleans up (pressend/presschange(false)) rather than getting stuck; a second identical pointerdown-then-outside-pointerup cycle also cleans up correctly -- pinning that press state does not leak/get stuck when the terminating pointerup lands outside the shadow DOM's boundary. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should clean up press state if pointerup was outside the shadow dom")
- Given `shouldCancelOnPointerExit` inside Shadow DOM, when pointerdown/pointerout/pointerover fire (pointer leaves then returns) within the shadow root, then the press is cancelled on exit and does not restart on return, matching the light-DOM shouldCancelOnPointerExit behavior inside a shadow tree. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should cancel press when moving outside and the shouldCancelOnPointerExit option is set")
- Given the Shadow DOM setup, when pointerdown carries shiftKey:true and pointerup/click carry ctrlKey:true, then the emitted press events reflect those modifier keys exactly as in the light-DOM modifier-key test, confirming modifier key propagation works through the shadow boundary. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should handle modifier keys")
- Given the Shadow DOM setup, when pointerdown/pointerup fire with `button:1` inside the shadow root, then no press events fire, matching the left-click-only restriction in light DOM. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should only handle left clicks")
- Given the Shadow DOM setup with a zero-size (`width:0,height:0`) pointerdown/pointerup pair reporting pointerType 'mouse', when those fire inside the shadow root, then no press events fire yet; only the subsequent `click` produces the full press sequence with pointerType:'virtual' -- virtual/screen-reader click detection works the same inside a shadow tree. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should ignore virtual pointer events")
- Given `navigator.userAgent` mocked to 'Android' inside the Shadow DOM setup, when the same zero-size pointerdown/pointerup/click sequence fires inside the shadow root, then press events fire immediately on the pointerdown/pointerup pair with pointerType:'mouse' (not deferred/virtual), matching the light-DOM Android exemption from the virtual-click heuristic. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should not ignore virtual pointer events on android ")
- Given `navigator.userAgent` mocked to 'Android' inside Shadow DOM, when a pointerdown/pointerup pair with `width:1,height:1,pressure:0,detail:0` fires inside the shadow root, then no press events fire on the pointer events themselves, and only the subsequent click (with matching TalkBack-shaped payload) produces the full press sequence with pointerType:'virtual' -- the Android TalkBack double-tap detection quirk also applies inside a shadow tree. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should detect Android TalkBack double tap")
- Given `isDisabled` set inside the Shadow DOM setup, when pointerdown/pointerup/click fire inside the shadow root, then no press or click events fire, matching the light-DOM disabled-element behavior. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should not fire press/click events for disabled elements")
- Given the Shadow DOM setup with a large hit area (`width:20,height:20`) and pointerup/click landing at an offset (10,10) from pointerdown, when those fire inside the shadow root, then onPress still fires, matching the light-DOM "close to target" tolerance. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should fire press event when pointerup close to the target")
- Given the Shadow DOM setup, when pointerdown fires inside the shadow root, then the element gets inline `user-select:none`; when pointerup then click fire, then that style is removed -- verified via `not.toHaveAttribute('style', 'user-select: none')` rather than `toHaveStyle` due to a noted jsdom 26 caching bug in `@testing-library/jest-dom`'s `toHaveStyle` matcher (linked to testing-library/jest-dom#673) that made `toHaveStyle` unreliable for this negative assertion in a shadow tree. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should add/remove user-select: none to the element on pointer down/up")
- Given a mocked `getBoundingClientRect` returning a 100x100 rect at origin, in the pointer-events world, when pointerdown fires at clientX:25,clientY:0 and pointerup/click fire at clientX:75,clientY:75 (pointerType:'mouse'), then pressstart reports x:25,y:0 while pressup/pressend/press report x:75,y:75 -- each press event's x/y reflects the actual pointer coordinates of its own underlying native event, not a fixed start or end position. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "mouse pointer events should have coordinates")
- Given the same mocked bounding rect, in the pointer-events world with pointerType:'touch', when pointerdown fires at (25,0) and pointerup/click fire at (75,75), then pressstart reports x:25,y:0 and pressup/pressend/press report x:75,y:75, mirroring the mouse-pointerType coordinate behavior for touch-pointerType pointer events. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "pointer touch events should have coordinates")
- Given the same mocked 100x100 bounding rect, in the touch-events world, when touchStart fires at (25,25) and a `scroll` event on `document.body` cancels the press before touchEnd fires (touchEnd's own coordinates of (25,0) are irrelevant since the press already ended), then pressstart reports the real touch coordinates x:25,y:25 but pressend reports x:50,y:50 -- the element's center -- because the press was cancelled by a scroll rather than by a pointer end position, and no real end coordinate exists to report. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "cancel from scroll events should have coordinates")

### Focus management

- Given `preventFocusOnPress` is set (top-level test, outside any pointer/mouse/touch describe, exercised via a plain `click` event with no prior pointerdown), when `click` fires, then `document.activeElement` is not the target -- preventFocusOnPress also suppresses focus for the virtual/click-only activation path. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should not focus the target if preventFocusOnPress is true")

### Notes and edge cases

- Given `window.navigator.platform` mocked to 'iPhone' (iOS) and `document.documentElement.style.webkitUserSelect` pre-set to a sentinel value, when `touchStart` fires, then `document.documentElement.style.webkitUserSelect` is set to `'none'` (disabling text selection on the whole page, not just the element) while the pressed element itself does NOT get an inline `user-select: none` style -- pinning an iOS-specific page-wide text-selection-disable workaround. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should add user-select: none to the page on press start (iOS)")
- Given `window.navigator.platform` mocked to 'Android' (non-iOS), when `touchStart` fires, then `document.documentElement.style.webkitUserSelect` is NOT changed (stays at the sentinel value) but the pressed element itself DOES get inline `user-select: none` -- confirming the page-wide iOS workaround is not applied on Android, where per-element user-select is used instead. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should not add user-select: none to the page when press start (non-iOS)")
- Given iOS platform mocking, when touchStart then touchEnd fire and fake timers are advanced by 316ms, then `document.documentElement.style.webkitUserSelect` reverts to its pre-press value (the page-wide none is removed after a delay following press end); a further touchStart/touchEnd and a touchStart/touchMove-elsewhere/touchEnd (advancing 300ms twice) both leave `webkitUserSelect` correctly restored afterward, guarding against a regression (linked to adobe/react-spectrum#862) where a checkbox-like element failed to remove the page-wide `user-select:none` style. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should remove user-select: none from the page when press end (iOS)")
- Given Android platform mocking and the element's own style set to `userSelect:'text'`, when touchStart fires, then the element gets inline `user-select:none`; when touchEnd fires, then it reverts to `user-select:text` immediately (no delay, unlike iOS's page-wide removal); a further touchStart/touchEnd and touchStart/touchMove/touchEnd cycle also correctly restore `user-select:text` afterward (regression guard for adobe/react-spectrum#862 on non-iOS). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should remove user-select: none from the element when press end (non-iOS)")
- Given iOS platform mocking and two separate pressable elements, when the first element's touchStart+touchEnd complete (webkitUserSelect becomes 'none') and then the second element's touchStart begins before advancing timers, then `webkitUserSelect` is still 'none' after 300ms because a second press is active; only after the second element's touchEnd fires and another 300ms elapses does `webkitUserSelect` revert to the pre-press sentinel value -- pinning that rapidly pressing a second element keeps the page-wide user-select suppression active until ALL presses have ended (this `it` appears twice with the identical name/body in the suite, effectively duplicated). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should not remove user-select: none when pressing two different elements quickly (iOS)")
- Given Android platform mocking and two separate pressable elements both with `userSelect:'text'` starting style, when both elements' touchStart fire (both get `user-select:none`), then when the first element's touchEnd fires it individually reverts to `user-select:text` while the second (still pressed) element stays at `user-select:none`, and only after the second element's touchEnd does it also revert -- confirming per-element user-select cleanup is independent per pressed element on non-iOS. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should clean up user-select: none when pressing and releasing two different elements (non-iOS)")
- Given iOS platform mocking, when touchStart fires (setting page-wide `webkitUserSelect:'none'`) and then the pressable component is unmounted mid-press, then after advancing timers by 316ms `document.documentElement.style.webkitUserSelect` still reverts to its original value -- the page-wide user-select cleanup runs even if the component unmounts while pressed. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should remove user-select: none from the page if pressable component unmounts (iOS)")
- Given Android platform mocking and a test component whose `onPressStart` schedules (via `setTimeout`) an unrelated style change (`background:'red'`) applied through React re-render (not through usePress's internal style mutation), when touchStart fires then all timers run, then the element has both `user-select:none` (set by usePress) and `background:red` (set by the app) simultaneously -- an unrelated React-driven style update during the press does not clobber usePress's own `user-select:none` mutation; when touchEnd fires, then `user-select` is cleared but `background:red` remains, confirming usePress only touches the user-select property and leaves other inline styles the app set alone. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "non related style changes during press down shouldn't overwrite user-select on press end (non-iOS)")
- Given Android platform mocking and a test component whose `onPressStart`-triggered re-render explicitly sets `userSelect:'text'` (along with `background:'red'`) on the same element usePress is managing, when touchStart fires then all timers run, then the app's `user-select:'text'` wins over usePress's internal `none` (the app's explicit value is respected once applied); when touchEnd fires, then `user-select:'text'` and `background:'red'` both persist -- usePress does not force-clear a user-select value the app itself set after press start. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "changes to user-select during press down remain on press end (non-iOS)")
- Given nested outer/inner elements each using usePress, parameterized across three interaction styles via `describe.each` (Mouse Events using `installMouseEvent`+mouseDown/mouseUp, Pointer Events using `installPointerEvent`+pointerDown/pointerUp, and Touch Events using touchStart/touchEnd with no special install), when the start action, end action, and a final `click` all fire on the INNER element, then by default the inner element's onPressStart/onPressEnd/onPress all fire (3 calls) but the OUTER element's handlers never fire (0 calls) -- press events do not bubble to ancestor usePress hooks by default, for all three input modalities. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should stop propagation by default")
- Given the same nested outer/inner usePress setup across Mouse Events/Pointer Events/Touch Events variants, when the inner element's press handlers call `e.continuePropagation()`, then both inner (4 calls: start/end/press/up) and outer (4 calls) handlers fire -- calling `continuePropagation()` on the press event lets it bubble up to an ancestor usePress hook, for all three input modalities. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should allow propagation if continuePropagation is called")
- Given a pressable element wrapped in a `DialogTrigger`/`Dialog` portal (`Provider`+`ActionButton`+`Dialog`), parameterized across Mouse Events/Pointer Events/Touch Events, when the trigger button is clicked to open the dialog and then the start/end press actions fire on content rendered inside the portal, then the outer wrapping element's press handlers never fire (0 calls) for any of the three input modalities -- press events do not bubble up through a React portal boundary to an ancestor usePress hook, even though a portal is not a DOM ancestor. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "stop event bubbling through portal")
- Given a pressable element rendered via `createPortal` into an `<iframe>`'s own document (a separate window/document from the top-level test document), in the pointer-events world (`installPointerEvent`), when pointerdown/pointerup/click fire on the element inside the iframe's contentWindow.document, then the full pressstart/presschange(true)/pressup/pressend/presschange(false)/press sequence fires correctly with pointerType:'mouse' -- confirming usePress correctly resolves the owner document/window of a target living in a different document than the one usePress's listeners were presumably attached from. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should fire press events based on pointer events")
- Given the same iframe-portaled pressable element, when keyDown/keyUp with `key:' '` fire on the element inside the iframe's document, then the full pressstart/presschange(true)/pressup/pressend/presschange(false)/press sequence fires with pointerType:'keyboard' -- keyboard press handling also correctly targets the iframe's own document rather than the top-level document. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should handle keyboard events")
- Given the same iframe-portaled pressable element, in the fallback mouse-events world (`installMouseEvent`), when mousedown/mouseup/click fire inside the iframe's document, then the full press sequence fires correctly with pointerType:'mouse', confirming mouse-event fallback handling also resolves the iframe's owner document/window correctly. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should fire press events based on mouse events")
- Given the same iframe-portaled pressable element, when only a `click` event fires inside the iframe's document with no preceding pointer/mouse event, then the full pressstart/pressup/pressend/press sequence is synthesized with pointerType:'virtual', confirming virtual/screen-reader click detection also works correctly for elements owned by a different document (iframe) than the top-level one. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should fire press events events for virtual click events from screen readers")
- Given the "usePress with Shadow DOM" describe block, this particular `it` actually renders a plain light-DOM `<Example>` (not via `setupShadowDOMTest`), when a pointerdown/pointerup pair fires with `width:1,height:1,pressure:0,detail:0`, then press events fire immediately (non-empty events array), confirming pressure:0 alone (off Android) doesn't trigger virtual-click detection -- this duplicates the earlier "should fire if pressure is 0 but is not android" pointer-events test verbatim, misplaced inside the Shadow DOM describe block. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/usePress.test.js: "should fire if pressure is 0 but is not android")
- Harness: Fake timers are installed suite-wide (`jest.useFakeTimers()` in `beforeAll`, `act(() => jest.runAllTimers())` in `afterEach`), with individual tests also calling `jest.advanceTimersByTime(ms)` to deterministically drive the iOS/Android user-select cleanup delays (e.g. 300-316ms) and long-press focus timing (90ms).
- Harness: `installPointerEvent()` / `installMouseEvent()` (from `@react-spectrum/test-utils-internal`) toggle jsdom's PointerEvent polyfill on/off so the same press logic can be exercised in a "pointer events" world and a "mouse/touch events" fallback world, since a file comment notes "JSDOM doesn't yet support pointer events" (jsdom/jsdom#2527) and the tests convert once real support lands.
- Harness: A local `pointerEvent(type, opts)` helper builds a real DOM `Event` (bubbles/cancelable/composed:true) and merges default modifier keys (ctrlKey/metaKey/shiftKey/altKey:false), `button:0`, and `width:1,height:1` with the per-call `opts`, used via `fireEvent(el, pointerEvent(...))` rather than testing-library's built-in pointer helpers.
- Harness: `fireEvent` (testing-library) is used exclusively over `user-event` throughout, giving tests precise control to fire individual low-level DOM events (pointerdown/pointerup/mousedown/touchstart/scroll/dragstart/keydown) independently and out of realistic order to probe edge cases.
- Harness: Platform/userAgent mocking uses `jest.spyOn(navigator, 'userAgent', 'get')` and `jest.spyOn(window.navigator, 'platform', 'get')`, frequently via the `using` declaration (`using uaMock = jest.spyOn(...)`) for automatic restoration at block scope end, to simulate iOS/Android/Mac.
- Harness: `describe.each` with a tagged-template table (`type | prepare | actions`) parameterizes identical `it` bodies across Mouse Events / Pointer Events / Touch Events variants for the event-bubbling and portal-bubbling suites.
- Harness: `createShadowRoot()` plus the private `enableShadowDOM()` feature flag (from `react-stately/private/flags/flags`) render tests inside a real Shadow DOM tree via `render(..., {container: shadowRoot})`, with elements looked up via `shadowRoot.getElementById`.
- Harness: iframe/owner-document tests use `waitFor` to await the async mount of `createPortal`-rendered content inside a manually created `<iframe>`'s `contentWindow.document`.
- Harness: `toHaveStyle`/`toHaveAttribute('style', ...)` jest-dom matchers assert inline `user-select` styling; one Shadow DOM test explicitly avoids a negative `toHaveStyle` assertion due to a documented jsdom 26 caching bug in `@testing-library/jest-dom` (testing-library/jest-dom#673).

## press-responder

### Roles and attributes
