- Given `<Pressable>` wraps a plain `<span>Trigger</span>` with no ARIA role at all, when rendered, then `console.warn` is called with `'<Pressable> child must have an interactive ARIA role.'`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/Pressable.test.js: "should warn if child does not have a role")
- Given `<Pressable>` wraps a `<span role="presentation">Trigger</span>` (a non-interactive ARIA role), when rendered, then `console.warn` is called with `'<Pressable> child must have an interactive ARIA role. Got "presentation".'`, naming the offending role in the message. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/Pressable.test.js: "should warn if child does not have an interactive role")

### Pointer (mouse, touch, pen, virtual click from screen readers)

- Given `<Pressable onPress={onPress}>` wraps a `<button>`, when the button is clicked via `userEvent.click`, then `onPress` is called exactly once. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/Pressable.test.js: "should apply press events to child element")
- Given `<PressResponder onPress={onPress}>` wraps a `<div>` containing a nested `<Pressable><button></Pressable>` (with intervening non-pressable DOM), when the button is clicked, then the `onPress` declared on the ancestor `PressResponder` is called exactly once — press context is threaded down through arbitrary intermediate elements to the nearest `Pressable`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/PressResponder.test.js: "should handle press events on nested pressable children")

### Focus management

- Given `<Pressable ref={ref1}>` wraps a `<button ref={ref2}>`, when rendered, then both `ref1.current` and `ref2.current` resolve to the same button DOM node — `Pressable` merges its own ref with the child's existing ref rather than overwriting it. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/Pressable.test.js: "should should merge with existing ref")
- Given `<Pressable>` wraps a `<span role="button">` with no `tabIndex`, when rendered, then the span is given `tabindex="0"` automatically, making it focusable. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/Pressable.test.js: "should automatically make child focusable")
- Given `<Pressable>` wraps a plain function component that renders a `<button>` but does not forward the ref it receives (does not forward any ref at all), when rendered, then `console.error` is called with `'<Pressable> child must forward its ref to a DOM element.'`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/Pressable.test.js: "should error if component does not forward its ref")
- Given `<Pressable>` wraps a `forwardRef` component that calls `useImperativeHandle` to expose a non-DOM object (`{something: true}`) instead of the underlying `<button>` DOM node, when rendered, then `console.error` is called with `'<Pressable> child must forward its ref to a DOM element.'` — an imperative handle that isn't a DOM element is rejected the same as no ref at all. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/Pressable.test.js: "should error if component does not forward its ref to a DOM element")
- Given `<Pressable>` wraps a `forwardRef` component whose rendered `<span role="button">` never receives a `tabIndex` (not focusable), when rendered, then `console.warn` is called with `'<Pressable> child must be focusable. Please ensure the tabIndex prop is passed through.'`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/Pressable.test.js: "should warn if child is not focusable")
- Given the same non-focusable `<Pressable><span role="button"></Pressable>` structure is rendered inside a `<div hidden>` ancestor, when rendered, then the "child must be focusable" `console.warn` is NOT called — the focusability check is skipped for elements inside a hidden subtree. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/Pressable.test.js: "should not warn if focusable child is inside a hidden subtree")
- Given `<Pressable isDisabled>` wraps a `<span role="button">`, when rendered, then the span has no `tabindex` attribute at all (not even `-1`) and no focusability warning is logged — disabled Pressables are intentionally left out of the tab order without triggering the "must be focusable" check. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/Pressable.test.js: "supports isDisabled")
- Given `<PressResponder ref={ref}>` wraps a `<div>` containing a nested `<Pressable><button></Pressable>`, when rendered, then `ref.current` resolves to the actual button DOM element, deep through the intermediate `div` and `Pressable` — `PressResponder` forwards refs through to the nested pressable's DOM node. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/PressResponder.test.js: "should handle forward refs to nested pressable children")

### Notes and edge cases

- Given `<Pressable onPress={onPress}>` wraps a `<button onClick={onClick}>` that already has its own `onClick`, when the button is clicked, then both `onPress` and the original `onClick` each fire exactly once — `Pressable` merges injected press handling with existing DOM event handlers instead of overwriting them. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/Pressable.test.js: "should should merge with existing props, not overwrite")
- Given `<PressResponder>` wraps a `<div><button>Button</button></div>` with no `Pressable` anywhere in its subtree, when rendered, then `console.warn` is called exactly once — `PressResponder` warns when it has no `Pressable` descendant to consume its press context. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/PressResponder.test.js: "should warn if there is no pressable child")
- Given `<PressResponder>` wraps a `<div>` containing a nested `<Pressable><button></Pressable>`, when rendered, then `console.warn` is never called — the warning about a missing pressable child is suppressed once one is found anywhere in the subtree. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/PressResponder.test.js: "should not warn if there is a pressable child")
- Given `<PressResponder>` wraps a `<div>` containing `<Pressable onPress={onPress}><button onClick={onClick}></Pressable>` (both layers have their own handlers), when the button is clicked, then both `onPress` and `onClick` fire exactly once each — props merge correctly through both the `PressResponder` and `Pressable` composition layers. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/PressResponder.test.js: "should merge with existing props, not overwrite")

## hover

### Pointer (mouse, touch, pen, virtual click from screen readers)

- Given a pointer-events environment, when `pointerover` then `pointerout` fire on the target with `pointerType: 'mouse'`, then events fire in order `hoverstart` (`{target, pointerType: 'mouse'}`), `hoverchange` (`isHovering: true`), `hoverend` (`{target, pointerType: 'mouse'}`), `hoverchange` (`isHovering: false`). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useHover.test.js: "should fire hover events based on pointer events")
- Given hoverProps are attached to an outer element that contains an inner `data-testid="inner-target"` child, when `pointerover`/`pointerout` fire on the inner child, then the reported `target` on `hoverstart`/`hoverend` is the outer element the listeners were attached to, not the inner child. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useHover.test.js: "hover event target should be the same element we attached listeners to even if we hover over inner elements")
- Given a pointer-events environment, when `pointerover`/`pointerout` fire with `pointerType: 'touch'`, then no hover events fire. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useHover.test.js: "should not fire hover events when pointerType is touch")
- Given a pointer-events environment, when a touch `pointerdown`/`pointerover`/`pointerout`/`pointerup` sequence is immediately followed by a `pointerover`/`pointerout` with `pointerType: 'mouse'` (simulating an iOS Safari bug that fires a `pointerType="mouse"` event on focus after a touch interaction, per WebKit bug 214609), then no hover events fire at all for the emulated mouse events. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useHover.test.js: "ignores emulated mouse events following touch events")
- Given a pointer-events environment, when a touch `pointerdown`/`pointerover`/`pointerout`/`pointerup` sequence is followed by advancing timers 600ms and then a `pointerover`/`pointerout` with `pointerType: 'mouse'` (the iOS Safari emulated-mouse-after-touch suppression window, WebKit bug 214609, has elapsed), then the mouse hover events fire normally (`hoverstart`, `hoverchange(true)`, `hoverend`, `hoverchange(false)`). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useHover.test.js: "ignores supports mouse events following touch events after a delay")
- Given a pointer-events environment, when `pointerover` then `pointerout` fire with `pointerType: 'mouse'`, then the rendered text content toggles from "test" to "test-hovered" and back to "test". (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useHover.test.js: "should visually change component with pointer events")
- Given a pointer-events environment, when `pointerover`/`pointerout` fire with `pointerType: 'touch'`, then the rendered text content never changes from "test". (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useHover.test.js: "should not visually change component when pointerType is touch")
- Given a hovered child button is removed from the DOM via a click handler (the browser does not fire `pointerout`/`pointerleave` for a removed element, but does fire `pointerover` on the new target), when a subsequent `pointerover` fires on `document.body`, then `onHoverEnd`/`onHoverChange(false)` fire and the `data-hovered` attribute is removed from the original (now-detached) element. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useHover.test.js: "should trigger onHoverEnd after an element is removed")
- Given a mouse-events environment (no pointer events installed), when `mouseEnter`/`mouseLeave` fire, then events fire in order `hoverstart` (`pointerType: 'mouse'`), `hoverchange(true)`, `hoverend`, `hoverchange(false)`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useHover.test.js: "should fire hover events based on mouse events")
- Given a mouse-events environment, when `mouseEnter` then `mouseLeave` fire, then the rendered text toggles between "test" and "test-hovered". (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useHover.test.js: "should visually change component with mouse events")
- Given a mouse-events environment, when `touchStart`, `mouseEnter`, `mouseLeave`, `touchEnd` fire followed by an extra `mouseEnter`/`mouseLeave` (simulating an iOS Safari bug that fires a mouse event on focus after touch, WebKit bug 214609), then no hover events fire at all. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useHover.test.js: "ignores emulated mouse events following touch events")
- Given a mouse-events environment, when a touch sequence (`touchStart`, `mouseEnter`, `mouseLeave`, `touchEnd`) is followed by advancing timers 600ms and then a real `mouseEnter`/`mouseLeave`, then the mouse hover events fire normally since the emulated-mouse-after-touch suppression window has elapsed. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useHover.test.js: "ignores supports mouse events following touch events after a delay")
- Given a touch-only interaction, when `touchStart`, `touchMove`, `touchEnd` fire followed by `mouseEnter`/`mouseLeave`, then no hover events fire (the touch sequence suppresses the subsequent emulated mouse events). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useHover.test.js: "should not fire hover events based on touch events")
- Given a touch-only interaction, when `touchStart`, `touchMove`, `touchEnd`, `mouseEnter`, `mouseLeave` fire in sequence, then the rendered text content never changes from "test" at any step. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useHover.test.js: "should not visually change component with touch events")

### State and controlled/uncontrolled

- Given useHover is passed `isDisabled`, when `mouseEnter`/`mouseLeave` fire on the element, then no `onHoverStart`, `onHoverEnd`, or `onHoverChange` callback fires at all. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useHover.test.js: "does not handle hover events if disabled")
- Given hover is active and `onHoverStart` has already fired (pointer events environment), when the component rerenders with `isDisabled` set to true, then `onHoverEnd` and `onHoverChange(false)` fire immediately (without a `pointerout`) and the visual "-hovered" text is removed. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useHover.test.js: "should end hover when disabled")
- Given hover is active and `onHoverStart` has already fired (mouse events environment, no pointer events installed), when the component rerenders with `isDisabled` set to true, then `onHoverEnd` and `onHoverChange(false)` fire immediately and the visual "-hovered" text is removed. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useHover.test.js: "should end hover when disabled")

### Notes and edge cases

- Given a hover-enabled wrapper element containing a `DialogTrigger`/`ActionButton` that opens a `Dialog` rendered in a portal, when hover start/end (tested with both real mouse events and pointer events) fire on the dialog's portaled content, then the wrapper's `onHoverStart`/`onHoverEnd`/`onHoverChange` handlers are never called, because hover event bubbling through the portal boundary is stopped. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useHover.test.js: "stop event bubbling through portal")

## keyboard

### Keyboard

- Given useKeyboard with `onKeyDown`/`onKeyUp` attached to a focused element, when the user types "A" (via `userEvent.keyboard`), then a `keydown` event followed by a `keyup` event both fire with `target` equal to that element. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useKeyboard.test.js: "should handle keyboard events")
- Given a nested useKeyboard element (no user-provided handlers stopping propagation) inside a plain `<button>` with its own native `onKeyDown`/`onKeyUp`, when a key is typed on the focused inner element, then the inner `onKeyDown`/`onKeyUp` each fire exactly once but the outer wrapper's `onKeyDown`/`onKeyUp` are never called — keyboard events do not bubble past useKeyboard by default. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useKeyboard.test.js: "events do not bubble by default")
- Given the inner useKeyboard's `onKeyDown`/`onKeyUp` handlers each call `e.continuePropagation()`, when a key is typed, then both the inner and the outer wrapper's `onKeyDown`/`onKeyUp` fire exactly once each — calling `continuePropagation()` re-enables bubbling to ancestor DOM listeners. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useKeyboard.test.js: "events bubble when continuePropagation is called")
- Given two useKeyboard hooks chained via `keyboardProps` composition — one bound to `shortcuts: {ArrowLeft: () => {}}` (no return value), the other to `shortcuts: {Enter: () => {}}` — wrapped in a `div` with `onKeyDown`, when `ArrowLeft` is pressed, then the outer div's `onKeyDown` is never called: an action that returns `undefined` stops propagation for the key it handles even when chained with another hook's shortcuts. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useKeyboard.test.js: "should stop propagation if any shortcut handling that key stops propagation")
- Given two chained useKeyboard hooks where the ArrowLeft handler returns `{shouldContinuePropagation: true}` and the other only handles `Enter`, when `ArrowLeft` is pressed, then the outer div's `onKeyDown` fires exactly once with `key: 'ArrowLeft'` — the only handler that actually matches the key controls propagation. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useKeyboard.test.js: "should continue propagation if all shortcuts that handle that key agree to continue propagation")
- Given two chained useKeyboard hooks both bound to `ArrowLeft`, one returning `{shouldContinuePropagation: true}` and the other returning `undefined` (default stop), when `ArrowLeft` is pressed, then the outer div's `onKeyDown` is never called — if any of multiple handlers matching the same key stops propagation, propagation is stopped overall. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useKeyboard.test.js: "should stop propagation if any shortcut stops propagation")
- Given two chained useKeyboard hooks both bound to `ArrowLeft` and both returning `{shouldContinuePropagation: true}`, when `ArrowLeft` is pressed, then the outer div's `onKeyDown` fires exactly once with `key: 'ArrowLeft'` — propagation continues only when every handler matching that key agrees. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useKeyboard.test.js: "should continue propagation if all shortcuts agree to continue propagation")
- Given a shortcut bound to key `'a'` with default `allowRepeats` (false), when `keyDown` fires with `{key: 'a', repeat: true}`, then the shortcut action is never called. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useKeyboard.test.js: "ignores repeated keydown events by default (allowRepeats: false)")
- Given a shortcut bound to key `'a'` with `allowRepeats` set to true, when `keyDown` fires with `{key: 'a', repeat: true}`, then the shortcut action is called exactly once. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useKeyboard.test.js: "handles repeated keydown events when allowRepeats is true")
- Given a shortcut bound to key `'a'` with default `allowComposing` (false), when `keyDown` fires with `{key: 'a', isComposing: true}` (an IME composition keystroke), then the shortcut action is never called. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useKeyboard.test.js: "ignores composing keydown events by default (allowComposing: false)")
- Given a shortcut bound to key `'a'` with `allowComposing` set to true, when `keyDown` fires with `{key: 'a', isComposing: true}`, then the shortcut action is called exactly once. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useKeyboard.test.js: "handles composing keydown events when allowComposing is true")
- Given a shortcut bound to key `'a'`, when `keyUp` fires (plain, then with `repeat: true`, then with `isComposing: true`), then the action is never invoked in any of the three cases — shortcuts only run on `keydown`, never `keyup`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useKeyboard.test.js: "does not run shortcuts on keyup, including repeated and composing keyups")
- Given both a `shortcuts` map and separate `onKeyDown`/`onKeyUp` handlers are provided on the same useKeyboard call, when key `'a'` is typed, then the shortcut action fires once and the user-provided `onKeyDown`/`onKeyUp` each also fire once — shortcut handling composes with, rather than replaces, plain key handlers. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useKeyboard.test.js: "chains a user-provided onKeyDown and onKeyUp with shortcuts")
- Given `navigator.platform` is mocked to `'MacIntel'` and a shortcut `'Mod+s'`, when `Meta+s` is typed, then the action fires once, the wrapper's `onKeyDown` fires exactly once (the meta keydown; the 's' keydown is consumed) and its `onKeyUp` fires twice (both the 's' keyup and the meta keyup); when `Control+s` or plain `'s'` are subsequently typed (non-matching combinations), then the action is not called and none of those keydowns/keyups are intercepted (preventDefault/stopPropagation are not applied) — `Mod` resolves to `Meta` on Mac. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useKeyboard.test.js: "matches Mod+key with metaKey")
- Given `navigator.platform` is mocked to `'MacIntel'` and a shortcut bound to the plain key `'s'` (no `Mod`), when `Meta+s` is typed, then the action is never called and the wrapper's `onKeyDown`/`onKeyUp` both fire twice (once for the meta keydown/up, once for the 's' keydown/up) — a plain-key shortcut does not match when an unrelated modifier is also held. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useKeyboard.test.js: "plain key ignores meta")
- Given `navigator.platform` mocked to `'MacIntel'` and shortcuts for both `'Mod+Shift+a'` and `'Control+Shift+a'`, when `Meta+Shift+a` is typed, then only the `Mod+Shift+a` action fires; when `Control+Shift+a` is typed, then only the `Control+Shift+a` action fires — `Mod` (Meta on Mac) and `Control` are distinct modifiers even though a user might conflate them. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useKeyboard.test.js: "Control+Shift distinct from Mod+Shift")
- Given `navigator.platform` mocked to `'MacIntel'` and a shortcut `'Meta+Control+Alt+z'`, when `Meta+Control+Alt+z` is typed, then the handler fires exactly once — three-modifier combinations are matched correctly. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useKeyboard.test.js: "Meta+Control+Alt combination")
- Given `navigator.platform` mocked to `'MacIntel'` and a shortcut `'Shift+Alt+down'` (using the `down` key alias), when `Shift+Alt+ArrowDown` is typed, then the action fires exactly once — key aliases like `down` resolve to the real `ArrowDown` key. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useKeyboard.test.js: "Shift+Alt and key aliases")
- Given `navigator.platform` mocked to `'MacIntel'` and shortcuts for both `'Mod+a'` and `'Mod+Shift+a'`, when `Shift+Meta+a` is typed, then only `Mod+Shift+a`'s action fires (not `Mod+a`'s); when `Meta+a` alone is typed, then only `Mod+a`'s action fires — the more specific modifier combination is matched exclusively, not both. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useKeyboard.test.js: "Mod+Shift+a matches only that binding, not Mod+a")
- Given `navigator.platform` mocked to `'MacIntel'` and a shortcut for `Escape`, when `Escape` is typed, then the handler is called and receives an event whose `key` is `'Escape'`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useKeyboard.test.js: "passes event to handler")
- Given `navigator.platform` mocked to `'MacIntel'` and an `Escape` shortcut handler that returns `false` (did not handle), when `Escape` is typed, then the wrapper's received keydown event reports `isDefaultPrevented()` as `false`, and the wrapper's `onKeyDown`/`onKeyUp` each still fire once — a handler declining to handle the event neither prevents default nor stops propagation. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useKeyboard.test.js: "continues propagation if the function did not handle the event")
- Given `navigator.platform` mocked to `'MacIntel'` and an `Escape` shortcut handler returning `{shouldPreventDefault: false, shouldContinuePropagation: true}`, when `Escape` is typed, then the wrapper observes `isDefaultPrevented()` as `false` even though the handler matched and ran — preventDefault and propagation can be finely controlled independently via the return value. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useKeyboard.test.js: "prevent default and stop propagation can both be finely controlled")
- Given `navigator.platform` is mocked to `'Win32'` and a shortcut `'Mod+s'`, when `Control+s` is typed, then the action fires once, the wrapper's `onKeyDown` fires once and `onKeyUp` fires twice; when `Meta+s` or plain `'s'` are subsequently typed (non-matching on Windows), then the action is never called — `Mod` resolves to `Control` on Windows, not `Meta`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useKeyboard.test.js: "matches Mod+key with ctrlKey")
- Given a shortcut spec containing only modifiers and no non-modifier key (`'Mod+Shift'`), when `parseKeyboardShortcut` is called, then it throws an error matching `/Invalid keyboard shortcut/`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/createKeyboardShortcutHandler.test.js: "throws when the shortcut has no non-modifier key")
- Given an empty string shortcut spec, when `parseKeyboardShortcut` is called, then it throws an error matching `/Invalid keyboard shortcut/`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/createKeyboardShortcutHandler.test.js: "throws on an empty string")
- Given the shortcut spec `'SHIFT+mod+A'`, when parsed, then the result is `{shift: true, alt: false, ctrl: false, meta: false, mod: true, key: 'A'}` — modifier names are matched case-insensitively and independent of their order in the string. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/createKeyboardShortcutHandler.test.js: "parses modifiers case-insensitively and ignores order")
- Given the shortcut spec `'Control+a'`, when parsed, then `ctrl` is `true` and `meta` is `false` — `Control` is recognized as an alias for the `ctrl` modifier (distinct from `Mod`). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/createKeyboardShortcutHandler.test.js: "treats control as an alias for ctrl")
- Given `navigator.platform` mocked to `'MacIntel'`, when `canonicalKeyboardShortcut` is computed for parsed `'Shift+Mod+a'` and separately for parsed `'Mod+Shift+a'`, then both produce an identical canonical string — modifier order in the input spec does not affect the canonical form. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/createKeyboardShortcutHandler.test.js: "is order-independent (Shift+Mod+a === Mod+Shift+a)")
- Given `navigator.platform` mocked first to `'MacIntel'` then to `'Win32'`, when `canonicalKeyboardShortcut` is computed for parsed `'Mod+s'` under each, then it resolves to `'Meta+s'` on Mac and `'Control+s'` on Windows. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/createKeyboardShortcutHandler.test.js: "expands Mod to Meta on Mac and Control on Windows")
- Given each key alias in turn — `space`, `esc`, `del`, `ins`, `left`, `right`, `up`, `down`, `pageup`, `pagedown` (parameterized), when passed through `parseKeyboardShortcut`/`canonicalKeyboardShortcut`, then each resolves to its canonical key name (`' '`, `escape`, `delete`, `insert`, `arrowleft`, `arrowright`, `arrowup`, `arrowdown`, `pageup`, `pagedown` respectively). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/createKeyboardShortcutHandler.test.js: "maps key alias \"$alias\" to \"$canonical\"")
- Given a synthetic keyboard event with `key: 'A'`, `shiftKey: true`, `metaKey: true`, when converted via `keyboardEventToCanonicalShortcut`, then the result is the string `'Meta+Shift+a'` — the key is lowercased and modifiers are emitted in a fixed canonical order. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/createKeyboardShortcutHandler.test.js: "lowercases the key and sorts modifiers into canonical order")
- Given a handler created from `{'Mod+s': action}` with `navigator.platform` mocked to `'MacIntel'`, when invoked with a synthetic event for `'s'` plus `meta`, then the action is called once, `e.preventDefault()` is called once, and `e.continuePropagation()` is never called — an action returning `undefined` defaults to prevent-default-and-stop-propagation. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/createKeyboardShortcutHandler.test.js: "runs the matching action and, on undefined return, prevents default and stops propagation")
- Given the same `'Mod+s'` handler on Mac, when invoked with a synthetic event for `'s'` plus `ctrl` (which does not match `Meta+s`), then the action is not called, `preventDefault` is not called, and `continuePropagation()` is called once. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/createKeyboardShortcutHandler.test.js: "does not run the action and continues propagation when there is no match")
- Given a handler for `Escape` whose action returns the boolean `true`, when invoked, then `preventDefault` is called once and `continuePropagation` is never called. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/createKeyboardShortcutHandler.test.js: "treats a boolean true return as preventDefault + stop propagation")
- Given a handler for `Escape` whose action returns the boolean `false`, when invoked, then `preventDefault` is never called and `continuePropagation` is called once. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/createKeyboardShortcutHandler.test.js: "treats a boolean false return as continue propagation without preventDefault")
- Given a handler for `Escape` whose action returns `{shouldPreventDefault: false, shouldContinuePropagation: true}`, when invoked, then `preventDefault` is never called and `continuePropagation` is called once — object returns finely control both behaviors independently. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/createKeyboardShortcutHandler.test.js: "lets an object return finely control preventDefault and propagation")
- Given a handler for `'Shift+Mod+a'` on Mac, when invoked with an event for `'a'` plus `meta` and `shift`, then the action fires once regardless of the order the modifiers were written in the binding spec. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/createKeyboardShortcutHandler.test.js: "matches regardless of modifier order in the spec")
- Given a handler created from an object literal containing both `'Mod+Shift+a'` and `'Shift+Mod+a'` as keys (which canonicalize to the identical shortcut), when invoked with an event for `'a'` plus `meta` and `shift`, then only the second (later) binding's action fires, not the first — later duplicate bindings in the spec object win. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/createKeyboardShortcutHandler.test.js: "lets later duplicate bindings win")
- Given a handler for `Escape`, when invoked with a synthetic event, then the exact same event object instance is passed through to the action function. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/createKeyboardShortcutHandler.test.js: "passes the event through to the action")

### State and controlled/uncontrolled

- Given useKeyboard is passed `isDisabled`, when the user types "A" on the focused element, then no `keydown`/`keyup` events are recorded by the hook's handlers at all. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useKeyboard.test.js: "should not handle events when disabled")

## long-press

### Keyboard

- Given a long-press listener with `threshold={800}`, when `keyDown`/`keyUp` fire with `key: ' '` (space) and 600ms elapse in between, then no long-press events (`onLongPressStart`/`onLongPressEnd`/`onLongPress`) fire — long press does not respond to keyboard interaction. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useLongPress.test.js: "should not fire any events for keyboard interactions")

### Pointer (mouse, touch, pen, virtual click from screen readers)

- Given a long-press listener with default threshold, when `pointerdown` fires with `pointerType: 'touch'`, then `onLongPressStart` fires immediately with `{target, pointerType: 'touch', ctrlKey, metaKey, shiftKey, altKey, x, y}`; when 400ms elapse nothing further fires; when a further 200ms elapse (600ms total) then `onLongPressEnd` and `onLongPress` both fire with the same payload shape; and firing `pointerup` afterward produces no additional events. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useLongPress.test.js: "should perform a long press")
- Given a long-press listener, when `pointerdown` (touch) fires, 200ms elapse, then `pointerup` fires before the long-press threshold (500ms) is reached, then only `onLongPressStart` and `onLongPressEnd` fire (no `onLongPress`), even after advancing timers by a further 800ms. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useLongPress.test.js: "should cancel if pointer ends before timeout")
- Given both `useLongPress` and `usePress` are merged onto the same element, when `pointerdown` (touch) fires and 600ms elapse (past the long-press threshold) before `pointerup`, then events fire in order `longpressstart`, `pressstart`, `longpressend`, `pressend`, `longpress` — i.e. long press completing does not suppress the underlying `pressstart`/`pressend` events, but no `onPress` (press "click") event fires since a completed long press cancels the press's own "press" callback. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useLongPress.test.js: "should cancel other press events")
- Given both `useLongPress` and `usePress` are merged onto the same element, when `pointerdown` (touch) fires, only 300ms elapse (before the long-press threshold), then `pointerup` and a `click` fire, then events fire in order `longpressstart`, `pressstart`, `longpressend`, `pressend`, `press` — releasing before the threshold does not cancel the ordinary press/click. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useLongPress.test.js: "should not cancel press events if pointer ends before timer")
- Given a custom `threshold={800}` prop, when `pointerdown` (touch) fires and 600ms elapse, then only `longpressstart` has fired; when a further 800ms elapse (1400ms total, past the custom threshold), then `longpressend` and `longpress` also fire — confirming the long-press delay is configurable via `threshold`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useLongPress.test.js: "allows changing the threshold")
- Given `onLongPress` is provided with no `accessibilityDescription`, when the long-press threshold is reached via `pointerdown` (touch) and 600ms elapse, then a native `contextmenu` event on the element is prevented (`fireEvent.contextMenu` returns `false`), i.e. the long press suppresses the platform's touch-and-hold context menu. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useLongPress.test.js: "prevents context menu events on touch")

### Accessibility tooling

- Given `accessibilityDescription="Long press to open menu"` and an `onLongPress` handler are provided, when the element renders, then it has an `aria-describedby` attribute pointing to a hidden element whose text content is "Long press to open menu". (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useLongPress.test.js: "supports accessibilityDescription")
- Given `accessibilityDescription` and `onLongPress` are provided together with `isDisabled`, when the element renders, then it has no `aria-describedby` attribute — the description is suppressed while disabled. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useLongPress.test.js: "does not show accessibilityDescription if disabled")
- Given `accessibilityDescription` is provided but no `onLongPress` handler, when the element renders, then it has no `aria-describedby` attribute — the description is only exposed when a long-press handler is actually registered. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useLongPress.test.js: "does not show accessibilityDescription if no onLongPress handler")

## move

### Keyboard

- Given useMove, when `keyDown` fires with `key` set to each of `ArrowUp`, `ArrowDown`, `ArrowLeft`, `ArrowRight` (parameterized), then a single synchronous triplet fires: `movestart` (`pointerType:'keyboard'`), `move` (`pointerType:'keyboard'`, `deltaX`/`deltaY` set to ±1 matching the arrow direction: up=`{0,-1}`, down=`{0,1}`, left=`{-1,0}`, right=`{1,0}`), then `moveend` — all from one keydown, with no separate keyup needed. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useMove.test.js: "responds to keypresses: $Key")
- Given a parent element with its own `onKeyDown` handler wrapping a useMove element, when `keyDown` fires with `key: 'PageUp'` (a key useMove does not handle), then useMove fires no move events and the keydown event still bubbles up to be observed by the parent's `onKeyDown` handler. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useMove.test.js: "allows handling other key events")

### Pointer (mouse, touch, pen, virtual click from screen readers)

- Given a mouse-events environment, when `mouseDown` (button 0) fires at `pageX:1,pageY:30` producing no events yet, then `mouseMove` to `pageX:10,pageY:25` fires `movestart` (`pointerType:'mouse'`, no modifiers) followed by `move` (`deltaX:9, deltaY:-5`), and then `mouseUp` fires `moveend` — with all three sharing `pointerType:'mouse'` and default (false) modifier keys. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useMove.test.js: "responds to mouse events")
- Given a mouse-events environment, when `mouseDown`/`mouseMove`/`mouseUp` all fire with `button: 2` (right click), then no `movestart`/`move`/`moveend` events fire. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useMove.test.js: "doesn't respond to right click")
- Given a mouse-events environment, when `mouseDown` and `mouseUp` fire at the same coordinates with no intervening `mouseMove`, then no move events fire at all. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useMove.test.js: "doesn't fire anything when clicking")
- Given a touch-events environment, when `touchStart` fires producing no events yet, then `touchMove` fires `movestart`+`move` (`pointerType:'touch'`, `deltaX:9, deltaY:-5`), and `touchEnd` fires `moveend`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useMove.test.js: "responds to touch events")
- Given an in-progress touch move (after `touchStart`+`touchMove`), when `touchCancel` fires instead of `touchEnd`, then `moveend` still fires, ending the move sequence the same way `touchEnd` would. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useMove.test.js: "ends with touchcancel")
- Given a touch-events environment, when `touchStart` and `touchEnd` fire at the same coordinates with no intervening `touchMove` (a tap), then no move events fire. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useMove.test.js: "doesn't fire anything when tapping")
- Given an in-progress move tracking touch `identifier: 1`, when a second touch (`identifier: 2`) independently starts, moves, and ends concurrently, then that second touch produces no move events at all; a subsequent `touchMove`/`touchEnd` on the original `identifier: 1` still fires `move`/`moveend` normally — additional simultaneous touches are ignored. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useMove.test.js: "ignores additional touches")
- Given a pointer-events environment, when `pointerDown` (`pointerType:'pen'`) fires producing no events yet, then `pointerMove` fires `movestart`+`move` (`pointerType:'pen'`, `deltaX:9, deltaY:-5`), and `pointerUp` fires `moveend`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useMove.test.js: "responds to pointer events")
- Given a pointer-events environment, when `pointerDown`/`pointerMove`/`pointerUp` all fire with `button: 2` (right click) and `pointerType:'pen'`, then no move events fire. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useMove.test.js: "doesn't respond to right click")
- Given an in-progress pointer move (pen), when `pointerCancel` fires instead of `pointerUp`, then `moveend` still fires, ending the sequence the same way `pointerUp` would. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useMove.test.js: "ends with pointercancel")
- Given a pointer-events environment, when `pointerDown` and `pointerUp` (pen) fire at the same coordinates with no intervening `pointerMove`, then no move events fire. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useMove.test.js: "doesn't fire anything when tapping")
- Given an in-progress move tracking `pointerId: 1`, when a second pointer (`pointerId: 3`) independently does its own down/move/up sequence concurrently, then it produces no move events; a subsequent `pointerMove`/`pointerUp` on the original `pointerId: 1` still fires `move`/`moveend` normally — additional simultaneous pointers are ignored. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useMove.test.js: "ignores any additional pointers")

### Notes and edge cases

- Given `navigator.platform` is mocked to `'iPhone'` and the document already has a `webkitUserSelect` style value, when `touchStart` begins a move interaction, then `document.documentElement.style.webkitUserSelect` is forced to `'none'` for the duration of `touchStart`/`touchMove`/`touchEnd`, and is restored to its original value only after an additional 316ms elapse post-`touchEnd` — an iOS Safari-specific workaround to prevent text selection during a touch drag. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useMove.test.js: "adds and removes user-select: none to the body (iOS)")
- Given `navigator.platform` is mocked to `'Android'` (non-iOS), when the same `touchStart`/`touchMove`/`touchEnd` sequence fires, then `document.documentElement.style.webkitUserSelect` is never modified — the user-select-none workaround is iOS-only. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useMove.test.js: "doesn't add user-select: none to the body (non-iOS)")
- Given a useMove element nested inside another useMove element (parent wrapping child), when a touch move sequence (`touchStart`/`touchMove`/`touchEnd`) fires on the child, then only the child's `onMoveStart`/`onMove`/`onMoveEnd` fire and the parent's handlers receive zero events — move events do not bubble to an ancestor's useMove. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useMove.test.js: "doesn't bubble to useMove on parent elements")

## context-menu

### Keyboard

- Given `navigator.platform` is mocked to `'MacIntel'` and the target element's `getBoundingClientRect` is mocked, when `keyDown` fires with `key: 'Enter', ctrlKey: true` and no native `contextmenu` event follows, then `onContextMenu` is NOT called synchronously but fires exactly once after a further 10ms elapse, with payload `{target: el, x: rect.width / 2, y: rect.height / 2}` — Ctrl+Enter is the macOS keyboard equivalent for opening a context menu, positioned at the element's center, deferred via a short timeout so a genuine native `contextmenu` event can preempt it. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useContextMenu.test.tsx: "triggers onContextMenu via Ctrl+Enter on macOS when no contextmenu event fires")
- Given `navigator.platform` mocked to `'MacIntel'`, when `keyDown` fires with `key: 'Enter', ctrlKey: true` and a native `contextmenu` event is also dispatched immediately afterward (some browsers fire a real `contextmenu` event for Ctrl+Enter), then after 10ms elapse `onContextMenu` has fired only once in total — the real event and the deferred keyboard-triggered one are deduplicated to avoid a double call. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useContextMenu.test.tsx: "does not double-fire when Ctrl+Enter also triggers a contextmenu event")
- Given `navigator.platform` is mocked to `'Win32'` (non-macOS), when `keyDown` fires with `key: 'Enter', ctrlKey: true` and 10ms elapse, then `onContextMenu` is never called — the Ctrl+Enter shortcut for opening a context menu is macOS-specific. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useContextMenu.test.tsx: "does not trigger on Ctrl+Enter on non-macOS")
- Given `navigator.platform` mocked to `'MacIntel'`, when `keyDown` fires with `key: 'Enter', ctrlKey: false` (plain Enter, no Ctrl) and 10ms elapse, then `onContextMenu` is never called. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useContextMenu.test.tsx: "does not trigger on Enter without Ctrl on macOS")

### Pointer (mouse, touch, pen, virtual click from screen readers)

- Given the target element's `getBoundingClientRect` is mocked to `{x:10, y:20, width:100, height:50, ...}`, when a native `contextmenu` event fires with `clientX: 30, clientY: 40` (a mouse right-click), then `onContextMenu` is called exactly once with `{target: el, x: 20, y: 20}` — the reported `x`/`y` are the click's client coordinates translated into element-relative coordinates (`clientX - rect.x`, `clientY - rect.y`). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useContextMenu.test.tsx: "calls onContextMenu on right click")
- Given a native `contextmenu` `MouseEvent` (cancelable, bubbling) is dispatched directly on the element, when dispatched, then `event.preventDefault()` and `event.stopPropagation()` are both called by the hook — useContextMenu always suppresses the browser's native context menu and stops it from bubbling further, regardless of whether an `onContextMenu` handler is registered. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useContextMenu.test.tsx: "prevents default and stops propagation on contextmenu event")
- Given `navigator.platform` mocked to `'iPhone'` and a pointer-events environment, when `pointerDown` fires with `pointerType: 'touch'` and 500ms elapse before `pointerUp`, then `onContextMenu` fires exactly once with `target` equal to the element — long-pressing for 500ms on iOS opens the context menu via touch, since iOS does not emit a native `contextmenu` event for touch-and-hold. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useContextMenu.test.tsx: "triggers onContextMenu via long press on iOS")
- Given the same iOS (`'iPhone'`) touch/pointer-events setup, when `pointerDown` (touch) fires, only 200ms elapse, then `pointerCancel` fires (followed by a further 400ms elapse), then `onContextMenu` is never called — canceling the press before the long-press threshold aborts the pending context-menu trigger. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useContextMenu.test.tsx: "does not trigger if the press is cancelled before the long press threshold")
- Given `navigator.platform` mocked to `'Android'` (within the touch/pointer-events long-press setup), when `pointerDown` (touch) fires, 500ms elapse (crossing the long-press threshold), a native `contextmenu` event also fires (simulating Android Chrome firing its own contextmenu event during a long press), and then `pointerUp` fires, then `onContextMenu` has still fired only once in total — the long-press-triggered invocation and Android's native long-press contextmenu event are deduplicated. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useContextMenu.test.tsx: "does not double-fire when long press and contextmenu event both occur (Android)")

### Notes and edge cases

- Given no `onContextMenu` prop is passed to `useContextMenu` at all, when a native `contextmenu` event fires on the element, then nothing throws and no call is recorded — the hook safely no-ops when no handler is registered. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useContextMenu.test.tsx: "does not call onContextMenu when prop is not provided")

## interact-outside

### Pointer (mouse, touch, pen, virtual click from screen readers)

- Given `useInteractOutside` on an element with pointer events installed, when pointerdown/pointerup/click happen on the element itself, then `onInteractOutside` is not called; when the same sequence happens on `document.body`, then it fires once. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useInteractOutside.test.js: "should fire interact outside events based on pointer events")
- Given pointer events, when the outside pointer sequence uses `button: 1` (middle button), then `onInteractOutside` is not called, and only a `button: 0` sequence fires it. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useInteractOutside.test.js: "should only listen for the left mouse button")
- Given a pointerdown fired on `document.body` before the hook's component mounts, when the matching pointerup and click arrive after mount, then `onInteractOutside` is not called, so the down and up must be paired within one lifetime. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useInteractOutside.test.js: "should not fire interact outside if there is a pointer up event without a pointer down first")
- Given mouse events (no pointer events), when mousedown/mouseup happen on the element, then nothing fires; when they happen on `document.body`, then `onInteractOutside` fires once. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useInteractOutside.test.js: "should fire interact outside events based on mouse events")
- Given mouse events, when the outside mousedown/mouseup use `button: 1`, then nothing fires; only `button: 0` fires it. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useInteractOutside.test.js: "should only listen for the left mouse button")
- Given a mousedown fired before mount, when only the mouseup arrives after mount, then `onInteractOutside` is not called. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useInteractOutside.test.js: "should not fire interact outside if there is a mouse up event without a mouse down first")
- Given touch events, when touchstart/touchend happen on the element, then nothing fires; when they happen on `document.body`, then `onInteractOutside` fires once. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useInteractOutside.test.js: "should fire interact outside events based on mouse events")
- Given a touch sequence followed by a synthetic mouseup, when the sequence is inside the element, then nothing fires, and when it is outside, `onInteractOutside` fires exactly once rather than twice. This pins the browser behavior of emulated mouse events after touch on mobile. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useInteractOutside.test.js: "should ignore emulated mouse events")
- Given a touchstart fired before mount, when only the touchend arrives after mount, then `onInteractOutside` is not called. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useInteractOutside.test.js: "should not fire interact outside if there is a touch end event without a touch start first")

### State and controlled/uncontrolled

- Given `isDisabled`, when a pointer/mouse sequence happens outside, then `onInteractOutside` is not called. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useInteractOutside.test.js: "does not handle pointer events if disabled")
- Given `isDisabled`, when a touch sequence happens outside, then `onInteractOutside` is not called. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useInteractOutside.test.js: "does not handle touch events if disabled")
- Given `isDisabled`, when a mouse sequence happens outside, then `onInteractOutside` is not called. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useInteractOutside.test.js: "does not handle mouse events if disabled")

### Notes and edge cases

- Given the hook's element portalled into an iframe, when a pointer sequence targets the element inside the iframe, then nothing fires; targeting the iframe's own document body fires `onInteractOutside` once, so listeners are attached to the element's owner document rather than the top document. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useInteractOutside.test.js: "should fire interact outside events based on pointer events")
- Given the iframe setup with pointer events, when the outside sequence uses `button: 1`, then nothing fires; `button: 0` fires it once. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useInteractOutside.test.js: "should only listen for the left mouse button")
- Given the iframe setup, when a pointerdown lands on the iframe body before mount and only the pointerup after, then nothing fires. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useInteractOutside.test.js: "should not fire interact outside if there is a pointer up event without a pointer down first")
- Given the iframe setup with mouse events, when a mouse sequence lands on the element, nothing fires; on the iframe body it fires once. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useInteractOutside.test.js: "should fire interact outside events based on mouse events")
- Given the iframe setup with mouse events, when the outside sequence uses `button: 1`, nothing fires; `button: 0` fires it once. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useInteractOutside.test.js: "should only listen for the left mouse button")
- Given the iframe setup, when the mousedown happens before mount and only the mouseup after, then nothing fires. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useInteractOutside.test.js: "should not fire interact outside if there is a mouse up event without a mouse down first")
- Given the iframe setup with touch events, when the touch sequence lands on the element, nothing fires; on the iframe body it fires once. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useInteractOutside.test.js: "should fire interact outside events based on mouse events")
- Given the iframe setup, when a touch sequence is followed by an emulated mouseup, then `onInteractOutside` still fires only once for an outside interaction. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useInteractOutside.test.js: "should ignore emulated mouse events")
- Given the iframe setup, when a touchstart happens before mount and the touchend after, then nothing fires. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useInteractOutside.test.js: "should not fire interact outside if there is a touch end event without a touch start first")
- Given the iframe setup with `isDisabled`, when pointer, touch, or mouse sequences happen on the iframe body, then `onInteractOutside` is never called. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useInteractOutside.test.js: "does not handle pointer events if disabled")
- Given the iframe setup with `isDisabled`, when a touch sequence happens on the iframe body, then nothing fires. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useInteractOutside.test.js: "does not handle touch events if disabled")
- Given the iframe setup with `isDisabled`, when a mouse sequence happens on the iframe body, then nothing fires. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useInteractOutside.test.js: "does not handle mouse events if disabled")
- Given the hook's element portalled into a shadow root, when a mouse sequence targets a child inside the tracked element, then `onInteractOutside` is not called, so composed-path retargeting is handled. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useInteractOutside.shadow.test.js: "does not trigger when clicking inside popover")
- Given the same shadow-root setup, when the mouse sequence targets the tracked element itself, then nothing fires. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useInteractOutside.shadow.test.js: "does not trigger when clicking the popover")
- Given the same shadow-root setup, when the mouse sequence targets `document.body` outside the shadow tree, then `onInteractOutside` fires once. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useInteractOutside.shadow.test.js: "triggers when clicking outside the popover")
- Given the same shadow-root setup, when the mouse sequence targets a button appended to the light DOM entirely outside the shadow tree, then `onInteractOutside` fires once. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useInteractOutside.shadow.test.js: "triggers when clicking a button outside the shadow dom altogether")
- Given a shadow-root setup that appends an extra element to `document.body` after mount, when that dynamically added element is clicked, then `onInteractOutside` fires once. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useInteractOutside.shadow.test.js: "correctly identifies interaction with dynamically added external elements")
- Given `useInteractOutside` inside a shadow root with `UNSAFE_PortalProvider` pointing at that shadow root and the `enableShadowDOM` flag on, when a real `user.click` lands on a button inside the tracked element or on the element itself, then nothing fires; when it lands on a sibling button also inside the shadow root but outside the tracked element, then `onInteractOutside` fires. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/interactions/useInteractOutside.shadow.test.js: "should handle interact outside events with UNSAFE_PortalProvider in shadow DOM")

## id-generation

### SSR and hydration

- Given two components calling `useId()` inside one `SSRProvider`, when rendered, then both ids start with `react-aria` and differ from each other; on React versions without `React.useId` they are exactly `react-aria-1` and `react-aria-2`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/ssr/SSRProvider.test.js: "it should generate consistent unique ids")
- Given five `useId` consumers spread across nested `SSRProvider`s, when rendered, then all five ids are unique; on React versions without `React.useId` the ids follow the path-prefixed scheme `react-aria-1`, `react-aria-2-1`, `react-aria-2-2-1`, `react-aria-3`, `react-aria-4-1`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/ssr/SSRProvider.test.js: "it should generate consistent unique ids with nested SSR providers")
- Given an `SSRProvider` inside `React.StrictMode` (which double-renders), when rendered, then the two ids are still distinct and not doubled up. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/ssr/SSRProvider.test.js: "it should generate consistent unique ids in React strict mode")
- Given `React.StrictMode` with nested `SSRProvider`s each wrapping a `React.Suspense` boundary, when rendered, then the ids stay distinct (`react-aria-1-1` and `react-aria-2-1` on pre-`useId` React). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/ssr/SSRProvider.test.js: "it should generate consistent unique ids in React strict mode with Suspense")
- Given `NODE_ENV=production` and no server rendering, when a `useId` consumer renders, then the id gets a random numeric prefix matching `/^react-aria\d+-/` so ids from separate app instances on a page cannot collide. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/ssr/SSRProvider.test.js: "should generate a random prefix when not server rendered")
- Given `NODE_ENV=test`, when a `useId` consumer renders, then no random prefix is added and the id matches `/^react-aria-/`, keeping test snapshots stable. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/ssr/SSRProvider.test.js: "should not generate a random prefix in tests")
- Given an `SSRProvider` with two `useSSRSafeId` consumers, when server-rendered and hydrated, then there are no hydration errors or id mismatches. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/ssr/SSRProvider.ssr.test.js: "should render without errors")
- Given the same setup wrapped in `React.StrictMode`, when server-rendered and hydrated, then there are no hydration errors. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/ssr/SSRProvider.ssr.test.js: "should render without errors in StrictMode")
- Given `React.StrictMode` with nested `SSRProvider`s, when server-rendered and hydrated, then there are no hydration errors. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/ssr/SSRProvider.ssr.test.js: "should render without errors in StrictMode with nested SSRProviders")

### Notes and edge cases

- Given a mocked `FinalizationRegistry`, when a `useId` component re-renders three times, then exactly one registration is made, the held value is the generated id string, and the unregister token is the same object as the registration target; on unmount exactly one `unregister` call happens with that same token. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/useId.test.jsx: "registers once per mounted id and uses an unregister token")
- Given a mounted `useId` whose id is later changed through `mergeIds(firstId, 'changed-id')`, when the next render flushes, then the previous registration is unregistered with the original token, the new id is registered, and the registration target object stays identical so the WeakMap key is stable. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/useId.test.jsx: "unregisters the previous id and re-registers when the id changes")
- Given `mergeProps({id: 'id1'}, {id: 'id2'})` where the second id comes from `useId`, when the component renders, then the `useId`-generated id is rewritten to the other id on a later render, so both props collapse to one shared id. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/mergeProps.test.jsx: "combines ids with aria ids")
- Given `mergeProps` called with the same generated id appearing twice plus a second id, when rendered, then no error is thrown (no infinite id-merge loop). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/mergeProps.test.jsx: "combines reoccuring ids")

## ssr

### SSR and hydration

- Given a `useViewportSize()` consumer, when server-rendered and hydrated, then no errors occur. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/useViewportSize.ssr.test.tsx: "should render without errors")
- Given a component rendering the viewport size from `useViewportSize()`, when the server HTML is inspected before hydration, then it reports `0x0`; after hydration it reports real non-zero dimensions. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/useViewportSize.ssr.test.tsx: "should update dimensions after hydration")

## i18n

### RTL and i18n

- Given an `I18nProvider` with no explicit locale and `navigator.language` set to `en-US`, when `navigator.language` changes to `pt-PT` and a `languagechange` event fires, then `useLocale()` re-renders with locale `pt-PT` and direction `ltr`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/i18n/languagechange.test.js: "should update locale when languagechange event is triggered")
- Given an `I18nProvider` with no explicit locale, when the language changes to `he-IL`, then `useLocale()` reports locale `he-IL` with direction `rtl`; changing back to `pt-PT` returns direction to `ltr`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/i18n/languagechange.test.js: "should update locale direction when changing from LTR to RTL language")
- Given `I18nProvider locale="fr-FR"`, when `navigator.language` changes to `ja-JP` and `languagechange` fires, then `useLocale()` still reports `fr-FR`, so an explicit locale wins over the browser locale. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/i18n/languagechange.test.js: "should not change displayed locale when explicit locale is provided via I18nProvider")

### SSR and hydration

- Given `getPackageLocalizationScript('en-US', {...})` with two packages sharing the same string value, when it generates the inline script, then the output sets `window[Symbol.for('react-aria.i18n.locale')]` to the locale and `window[Symbol.for('react-aria.i18n.strings')]` to the package map, deduplicating repeated string literals into a shared local variable. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/i18n/server.test.js: "should generate a script with localized strings")

## utils

### Forms (name, value, validation, form reset)

- Given `useFormReset(ref, '', onReset)` inside a form with a reset button, when the reset button is clicked, then `onReset` is called. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/useFormReset.test.tsx: "should call onReset on reset")
- Given a form whose own `onReset` handler calls `stopPropagation()`, when reset is clicked, then `onReset` from the hook still runs, so the hook does not depend on the reset event bubbling. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/useFormReset.test.tsx: "should call onReset on reset even if event is stopped")
- Given two `useFormReset` registrations in one form, when reset is clicked, then both `onReset` callbacks run. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/useFormReset.test.tsx: "should call every onReset on reset")
- Given a form whose `onReset` handler calls `preventDefault()` in the bubble phase, when reset is clicked, then `onReset` is expected not to run, but the test is disabled because matching native cancel semantics would require a capture listener. [test is skipped in the suite] (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/useFormReset.test.tsx: "should not call onReset if reset is cancelled")
- Given a form whose `onResetCapture` handler calls `preventDefault()`, when reset is clicked, then the hook's `onReset` does not run. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/useFormReset.test.tsx: "should not call onReset if reset is cancelled in capture phase")
- Given two `useFormReset` registrations and an `onResetCapture` that calls `preventDefault()`, when reset is clicked, then neither `onReset` runs. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/useFormReset.test.tsx: "should not call any onReset if reset is cancelled")

### Positioning

- Given a scrolling document root with a 100px border on all sides, a 500x500 client box, and a target whose rect is at (100, 2100), when `scrollIntoView(scrollView, target, {block: 'start', inline: 'start'})` runs, then `scrollLeft` becomes 100 and `scrollTop` becomes 2100, so the root's border is excluded from the scroll port. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/scrollIntoView.test.ts: "excludes root border from scroll port when scrolling to start")
- Given the same 100px-bordered root fixture, when `scrollIntoView(scrollView, target, {block: 'end', inline: 'end'})` runs, then `scrollLeft` becomes 600 and `scrollTop` becomes 2600. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/scrollIntoView.test.ts: "excludes root border from scroll port when scrolling to end")
- Given a div attached to the body, when `getScrollParents` runs on it, then the result includes `document.documentElement`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/getScrollParents.test.ts: "includes root as a scroll parent for a node in the document")
- Given a document root whose computed `overflow` is `hidden`, when `getScrollParents` runs on a body child, then the root is not included. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/getScrollParents.test.ts: "does not include root when root has overflow: hidden")
- Given an intermediate div with computed `overflow: auto`, when `getScrollParents` runs on its child, then both that div and the root are included. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/getScrollParents.test.ts: "includes a scrollable intermediate parent")
- Given a plain non-scrollable wrapper div, when `getScrollParents` runs on its child, then neither the wrapper nor `document.body` is included. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/getScrollParents.test.ts: "excludes non-scrollable ancestors")

### Animation and presence

- Given no CSS transition in flight, when `runAfterTransition(callback)` is called and pending timers flush, then the callback runs. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/runAfterTransition.test.ts: "calls callback immediately when no transition is running")
- Given an element that has dispatched a bubbling `transitionrun` for `opacity`, when `runAfterTransition(callback)` is called and timers flush, then the callback does not run until the matching `transitionend` is dispatched. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/runAfterTransition.test.ts: "defers callback until transition end when a transition is running")
- Given an in-flight `width` transition and two callbacks queued at different times, when the `transitionend` fires, then both queued callbacks run. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/runAfterTransition.test.ts: "calls multiple queued callbacks after transition ends")
- Given an element that started a transition and was then removed from the document, when `runAfterTransition(callback)` is called and timers flush, then the callback runs immediately because detached elements are pruned from the transition bookkeeping instead of blocking it forever. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/runAfterTransition.test.ts: "clears out detached elements from transitionsByElement")

### Notes and edge cases

- Given `mergeProps` with a single argument, when merged, then `onClick`, `className`, and `id` pass through by identity. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/mergeProps.test.jsx: "handles one argument")
- Given three prop objects each with an `onClick`, when the merged `onClick` is invoked, then all three handlers run in argument order. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/mergeProps.test.jsx: "combines callbacks")
- Given prop objects with a mix of overlapping and unique keys, when the merged handlers are invoked, then same-named callbacks chain in argument order, unique callbacks are preserved, and non-function props such as `styles` pass through. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/mergeProps.test.jsx: "merges props with different keys")
- Given three prop objects with `className`, when merged, then the result equals `clsx` of all three. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/mergeProps.test.jsx: "combines classNames")
- Given three prop objects with `id`, when merged, then the result equals a left-fold of `mergeIds`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/mergeProps.test.jsx: "combines ids")
- Given two prop objects with a non-special key such as `data`, when merged, then the later value wins. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/mergeProps.test.jsx: "overrides other props")
- Given two prop objects each carrying a `ref`, when the merged ref callback is invoked with a value, then both refs' `current` are set to that value. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/mergeProps.test.jsx: "merges refs")
- Given `mergeRefs(ref1, ref2)` attached to an input, when mounted, then both refs point at the same non-null DOM node. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/mergeRefs.test.tsx: "merge Refs")
- Given React 19 and `mergeRefs(ref1, ref2, callbackRefReturningCleanup)`, when mounted, then the cleanup function has not run and both object refs match the callback's node; on unmount the cleanup runs exactly once. Guarded to React 19+ where ref callbacks may return cleanup functions. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/mergeRefs.test.tsx: "merge Ref Cleanup")
- Given `filterDOMProps({id: 'foo'})`, when filtered, then `id` is always kept. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/filterDOMProps.test.ts: "always includes id")
- Given `filterDOMProps` with `dir`, `hidden`, and `suppressHydrationWarning` but no options, when filtered, then all global attributes are stripped and the result is empty. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/filterDOMProps.test.ts: "strips global attributes when global is not requested")
- Given `filterDOMProps(props, {global: true})` with `dir`, `lang`, `hidden`, `inert`, `translate`, and `suppressHydrationWarning`, when filtered, then every one of those is preserved. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/filterDOMProps.test.ts: "includes global attributes when global is requested")
- Given `filterDOMProps({suppressHydrationWarning: true}, {global: true})`, when filtered, then the prop is kept. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/filterDOMProps.test.ts: "includes suppressHydrationWarning when global is requested")
- Given `filterDOMProps({suppressHydrationWarning: true})` without `global`, when filtered, then the prop is stripped. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/filterDOMProps.test.ts: "strips suppressHydrationWarning when global is not requested")
- Given `useEvent(ref, 'customevent', listener)`, when the event is dispatched, then the listener fires once; after unmount, dispatching again does not call it. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/useEvent.test.tsx: "subscribes on mount and unsubscribes on unmount")
- Given a `useEvent` subscription whose listener prop becomes `undefined`, when the event is dispatched, then nothing is called. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/useEvent.test.tsx: "removes the listener when it becomes undefined")
- Given `useEvent` re-rendered with a different event name, when the old event name is dispatched nothing fires, and when the new one is dispatched the listener fires once. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/useEvent.test.tsx: "re-subscribes when the event name changes")
- Given `useEvent(ref, 'customevent', listener, {once: true})`, when the event is dispatched twice, then the listener fires once, so listener options are forwarded to `addEventListener`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/useEvent.test.tsx: "forwards listener options such as once")
- Given `useObjectRef()` with no argument, when the hook runs, then it returns a defined object ref whose `current` is `null`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/useObjectRef.test.js: "returns an empty object ref by default")
- Given a `forwardRef` component using `useObjectRef(forwardedRef)` with an object ref passed in, when mounted, then the outer ref points at the DOM node. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/useObjectRef.test.js: "should support React.forwardRef for an object ref")
- Given the same component with a function ref passed in, when mounted, then the function ref receives the DOM node. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/useObjectRef.test.js: "should support React.forwardRef for a function ref")
- Given a `forwardRef` component using `useObjectRef` with a jest.fn function ref, when mounted, then the ref callback is called exactly once rather than repeatedly. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/useObjectRef.test.js: "should only be called once")
- Given two components sharing one outer ref where the consumer reads it in `useEffect` and the `useObjectRef` component renders second, when mounted, then the consumer still sees the updated ref value, so `useObjectRef` updates before other `useEffect`s run. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/useObjectRef.test.js: "takes precedence over useEffect")
- Given two components sharing one outer ref where the consumer reads it in `useLayoutEffect` and the `useObjectRef` component renders first, when mounted, then the consumer sees the updated ref value, so `useObjectRef` batches with layout effects in DOM order. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/useObjectRef.test.js: "batches up with useLayoutEffect")
- Given a function ref that returns a cleanup function, when the `useObjectRef` component mounts, then the setup branch runs once; on unmount the cleanup runs once and the ref callback is never invoked with `null`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/useObjectRef.test.js: "calls cleanup function on unmount")
- Given no nonce configured anywhere, when `getNonce()` is called, then it returns `undefined`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/getNonce.test.js: "returns undefined when no nonce is configured")
- Given a `<meta property="csp-nonce">` with a `nonce` IDL property set, when `getNonce()` is called, then that value is returned. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/getNonce.test.js: "reads nonce from meta tag nonce attribute")
- Given a `<meta property="csp-nonce" content="...">`, when `getNonce()` is called, then the content value is returned. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/getNonce.test.js: "reads nonce from meta tag content attribute")
- Given `globalThis.__webpack_nonce__` set and no meta tag, when `getNonce()` is called, then the webpack nonce is returned. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/getNonce.test.js: "reads nonce from **webpack_nonce** global")
- Given both a meta nonce and `__webpack_nonce__`, when `getNonce()` is called, then the meta tag wins. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/getNonce.test.js: "prefers meta tag nonce over **webpack_nonce**")
- Given one meta tag with both a `nonce` property and a `content` attribute, when `getNonce()` is called, then the `nonce` property wins. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/getNonce.test.js: "prefers nonce attribute over content attribute on the same meta tag")
- Given a resolved nonce, when the meta tag is removed and `getNonce()` is called again, then the cached value is still returned, so the lookup is cached per document. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/getNonce.test.js: "caches the nonce per document")
- Given a cached nonce, when the meta value changes and `resetNonceCache()` is called, then the next `getNonce()` returns the new value. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/getNonce.test.js: "resetNonceCache clears the cached value")
- Given a meta tag whose `nonce` and `content` are both empty strings, when `getNonce()` is called, then it returns `undefined`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/getNonce.test.js: "treats empty string nonce as no nonce")
- Given a meta tag with an empty `nonce` property but a non-empty `content`, when `getNonce()` is called, then the content value is used. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/getNonce.test.js: "falls back to content when nonce attribute is empty")
- Given a first `getNonce()` call that finds nothing, when a `__webpack_nonce__` is set afterwards, then the next call picks it up, so a miss is not cached. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/getNonce.test.js: "does not cache a missing nonce")
- Given a first `getNonce()` miss, when a meta nonce tag is added afterwards, then the next call finds it. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/getNonce.test.js: "detects a meta nonce added after an initial miss")
- Given different `__webpack_nonce__` values on the top window and an iframe window, when `getNonce(iframe.contentDocument)` is called, then the iframe's own nonce is returned. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/getNonce.test.js: "reads **webpack_nonce** from the provided document window")
- Given no matching meta tag, when `getMetaValue('theme-color')` is called, then it returns `undefined`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/getMetaValue.test.js: "returns undefined when no matching meta tag exists")
- Given `<meta name="theme-color" content="#ff0000">`, when `getMetaValue('theme-color')` is called, then `#ff0000` is returned, so the `name` attribute is matched. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/getMetaValue.test.js: "reads a value from the name attribute for an arbitrary key")
- Given `<meta property="og:title" content="Hello">`, when `getMetaValue('og:title')` is called, then `Hello` is returned, so the `property` attribute is matched too. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/getMetaValue.test.js: "reads a value from the property attribute for an arbitrary key")
- Given `__webpack_nonce__` set, when `getMetaValue('theme-color')` is called, then it returns `undefined`, so the webpack fallback is specific to the nonce lookup. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/getMetaValue.test.js: "does not fall back to **webpack_nonce** for non-nonce keys")
- Given `<meta name="my:weird.key" content="escaped">`, when `getMetaValue('my:weird.key')` is called, then the value is found, so `:` and `.` in the key are escaped before building the CSS selector. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/getMetaValue.test.js: "escapes special characters in the key when building the selector")

## dom-helpers

### Notes and edge cases

- Given content rendered into a shadow root with the `enableShadowDOM` flag on, when `nodeContains` is asked whether the shadow root or the shadow host contains a button inside the shadow tree, then both answer true. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/DOMFunctions.test.tsx: "can tell if a node is contained even if it is within a shadow DOM")
- Given a `<slot>` inside a container in a shadow root and a light-DOM button projected into that slot, when `nodeContains(container, button)` is called, then it returns true by following `assignedSlot` even though the button's DOM parent is the host; a sibling outside the slotted subtree returns false. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/DOMFunctions.test.tsx: "can tell if slotted light DOM content is contained by a node inside the shadow root")
