- Given `getPropagationTargets` called with `null`, the `document`, or the `window`, when evaluated, then the result is always `[window]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/DOMFunctions.test.tsx: "can just get the global window")
- Given a target inside one shadow root, when `getPropagationTargets(target)` is called, then the result is `[window, shadowRoot]`; passing an explicit destination of `document` or `window` substitutes that destination in the first slot. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/DOMFunctions.test.tsx: "can get the propagation targets from a shadow root")
- Given a target inside two nested shadow roots, when `getPropagationTargets(target)` is called, then the result is `[window, innerShadowRoot, outerShadowRoot]` (innermost first); passing the intermediate node as the destination truncates the chain to `[intermediateNode, innerShadowRoot]`. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/DOMFunctions.test.tsx: "can get the propagation targets for multiple nested shadow roots")
- Given a target inside a shadow root, when `getPropagationTargets(target, null)` is called, then the result is an empty array. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/DOMFunctions.test.tsx: "does not return propagation targets when given a null destination")
- Given `getOwnerDocument(null)` and `getOwnerDocument(undefined)`, when called, then the global `document` is returned. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/domHelpers.test.js: "returns the window if the argument is %p")
- Given `getOwnerDocument(document)`, when called, then the same document is returned. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/domHelpers.test.js: "returns the document if the argument is the document")
- Given `getOwnerDocument(window)`, when called, then the window's document is returned. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/domHelpers.test.js: "returns the document if the argument is the window")
- Given an element attached to the main document, when `getOwnerDocument` is called on it, then the main document is returned. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/domHelpers.test.js: "returns the element's document if the element is in the document")
- Given an iframe's own document, when `getOwnerDocument` is called on it, then the iframe document (not the top document) is returned. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/domHelpers.test.js: "returns the iframe's document if the argument is the iframe's document")
- Given an element inside an iframe, when `getOwnerDocument` is called on it, then the iframe's document is returned. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/domHelpers.test.js: "returns the iframe's document if the element is in the iframe")
- Given `getOwnerWindow(null)` and `getOwnerWindow(undefined)`, when called, then the global window is returned. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/domHelpers.test.js: "returns the window if the argument is %p")
- Given an element in the main window, when `getOwnerWindow` is called, then the global window is returned. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/domHelpers.test.js: "returns the window if the element is in the window")
- Given the window itself, when `getOwnerWindow(window)` is called, then the window is returned. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/domHelpers.test.js: "returns the window if the element is the window")
- Given an element inside an iframe, when `getOwnerWindow` is called, then the iframe's `contentWindow` is returned. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/domHelpers.test.js: "returns the iframe's window if the element is in the iframe")
- Given nothing focused, when `getActiveElement()` is called, then `document.body` is returned. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/domHelpers.test.js: "returns the body as the active element by default")
- Given a focused button in the light DOM, when `getActiveElement()` is called, then that button is returned. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/domHelpers.test.js: "returns the active element in the light DOM")
- Given a focused button inside an open shadow root, when `getActiveElement()` is called, then the shadow button is returned rather than the host, so the helper walks into shadow roots. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/domHelpers.test.js: "returns the active element inside a shadow DOM")
- Given a focused input inside two levels of nested shadow roots, when `getActiveElement()` is called, then that input is returned. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/domHelpers.test.js: "returns the active element from within nested shadow DOMs")
- Given focus moved into a shadow-root input and then back to a light-DOM input, when `getActiveElement()` is called, then the light-DOM input is returned. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/domHelpers.test.js: "returns the active element in document after focusing an element in shadow DOM and then in document")
- Given a focused input inside an iframe, when `getActiveElement(iframe.contentWindow.document)` is called, then that input is returned. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/domHelpers.test.js: "returns the active element within an iframe")
- Given `setStyle(null, 'opacity', '0')`, when called, then it returns a function and calling that cleanup does not throw. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/domHelpers.test.js: "returns a no-op cleanup and does not throw when the target is null")
- Given an element with no prior `opacity`, when `setStyle(el, 'opacity', '0')` is applied and then cleaned up, then the property is set to `0` and cleanup removes it entirely so the `style` attribute is empty. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/domHelpers.test.js: "sets a CSS property on a single element and removes the property on cleanup when there was no initial value")
- Given an element whose `opacity` is already `0.5`, when `setStyle(el, 'opacity', '0')` is applied and cleaned up, then the original `0.5` is restored. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/domHelpers.test.js: "restores the previous value on cleanup when one existed")
- Given an element with `color: red !important`, when `setStyle(el, 'color', 'blue', 'important')` is applied and cleaned up, then the new value and priority take effect and both the original value and its `important` priority are restored. (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/domHelpers.test.js: "applies the given priority and restores the previous priority on cleanup")
- Given an array of two elements, one with a prior `display: flex` and one without, when `setStyle([a, b], 'display', 'none')` is applied and cleaned up, then both are set to `none` and each is restored to its own prior state (`flex` and empty respectively). (react-spectrum: packages/react-aria/test/utils/domHelpers.test.js: "ets the property on every element in an array and restores on cleanup, preserving each prior value")

## Test infrastructure notes

### Runners and environments

There are three separate runs, each with its own config:

- **Unit and component tests** (`yarn test` → `jest.config.js`, displayName `component and hooks`). Runner is Jest 30 with `jest-environment-jsdom`. Transform is `@swc/jest` with the automatic JSX runtime. `testMatch` is `**/packages/**/*.test.[tj]s?(x)`; `.ssr.test.*` and `.browser.test.*` are excluded via `testPathIgnorePatterns`. `testTimeout` is 20000ms and `clearMocks: true`. A custom resolver (`lib/jestResolver.js`) resolves the in-repo `"source"` export condition so tests run against TypeScript source rather than built output, and it also synthesizes modules for glob imports like `../intl/*.json`. CSS is stubbed with `identity-obj-proxy`; SVGs and binary assets go to `__mocks__`. The npm script sets `STRICT_MODE=1` and `VIRT_ON=1`, so the default CI run double-renders everything under `React.StrictMode` (see below).
- **SSR tests** (`yarn test:ssr` → `jest.ssr.config.js`, displayName `SSR`). Matches `**/packages/**/*.ssr.test.[tj]s?(x)`, runs `--runInBand`, timeout 30000ms, and uses `globalSetup`/`globalTeardown` from `@react-spectrum/test-utils-internal/src/ssrSetup` and `ssrTeardown`, which spin up an HTTP worker on `localhost:18235` that does the actual server render in a Node environment.
- **Real-browser tests** (`yarn test:browser` → `vitest.browser.config.ts`). Vitest 4 with `@vitest/browser-playwright`, matching `packages/**/test/**/*.browser.test.{ts,tsx}` and running the same suite headless in three engines: `chromium-desktop`, `firefox-desktop`, and `webkit-desktop`, all at 1280x720. `process.env.NODE_ENV` is forced to `"development"` so virtualizer and other test-environment shortcuts stay off. Setup file is `test/browser/setup.ts`.

### Strict mode

`packages/dev/test-utils/src/StrictModeWrapper.tsx` wraps every `render` and `renderHook` in `React.StrictMode` when `process.env.STRICT_MODE` is set, and the default `yarn test` script sets it. `mockImplementation()` in the same package exists purely to cope with this: it registers each `mockImplementationOnce` twice when `STRICT_MODE` is on, because strict mode calls the mocked getter twice. Several suites also assert that exactly one specific `console.warn` fired under strict mode (for example `DialogTrigger` unmount warnings).

### Console errors fail tests

`scripts/setupTests.js` replaces `console.error` and `console.warn` with versions that **throw**, so any unexpected React warning fails the test. There are two small allowlists: React 18 `ReactDOM.render` deprecation messages and `caniuse-lite is outdated` browserslist noise. Tests that legitimately expect a warning have to spy on `console.warn`/`console.error` themselves first (often with the `using` declaration for automatic restore). The browser setup file does the same thing differently: it records errors and throws in `afterEach` with the accumulated messages.

### Global environment shims

`scripts/setupTests.js` also: imports `mock-match-media/jest-setup` and cleans it up after each test (so `matchMedia` queries work); imports `@testing-library/jest-dom` for matchers; mocks `window.IntersectionObserver` with a no-op before each test and deletes it after; and defines `document.documentElement.clientWidth`/`clientHeight` as 1024x768 because jsdom otherwise reports 0. It converts unhandled promise rejections into thrown errors. It registers one custom matcher, `toContainObject(received, argument)`, which asserts an array contains an object matching the given subset.

### user-event vs fireEvent

Both are used deliberately. `userEvent.setup({delay: null, pointerMap})` is the default for realistic interaction (`user.click`, `user.tab`, `user.hover`, `user.keyboard`, `user.pointer`), with `delay: null` so it works with fake timers. `fireEvent` is reached for when a test needs to fabricate an event sequence a real browser produces but user-event does not, for example a `blur` with `relatedTarget: null` (focus moving into an iframe), untrusted vs trusted focus events, `focusIn`/`focusOut` that jsdom does not emit automatically, `visibilitychange`, `languagechange`, `beforeunload`, or a `fireEvent.click` on a hidden dismiss button that must not change the interaction modality. Some tests explicitly note that `userEvent` fires a doubled close event and switch to `fireEvent.mouseDown`/`mouseUp` to get exactly one.

`pointerMap` (from `@react-aria/test-utils/src/userEventMaps.ts`) declares the pointer devices user-event may use: `MouseLeft`/`MouseRight`/`MouseMiddle` (mouse, primary/secondary/auxiliary) and `TouchA`/`TouchB`/`TouchC` (touch; `TouchA` has `height: 1, width: 1`). Every `userEvent.setup` call in the suite passes it.

### Fake timers

`jest.useFakeTimers()` is near-universal in overlay, tooltip, toast, and focus suites, with `act(() => jest.runAllTimers())` in `afterEach` to drain requestAnimationFrame-based focus restoration and exit animations. FocusScope's suite explicitly comments that this is needed "to clean up any raf's that may be running to restore focus on unmount", and has a dedicated test that fails in `afterAll` if any rafs leak. `jest.advanceTimersByTime(n)` is used where a specific threshold matters (long-press 500ms, tooltip/preview warmup delays).

### Simulating pointer types and virtual clicks

- **Pointer events vs mouse/touch fallback.** jsdom has no `PointerEvent` (jsdom issue 2527, cited in comments across the suite). `installPointerEvent()` from `@react-aria/test-utils/src/testSetup.ts` installs a `FakePointerEvent extends MouseEvent` in `beforeAll` exposing `pointerType`, `pointerId`, `pageX`, `pageY`, `width`, `height`, and deletes it in `afterAll`. `installMouseEvent()` similarly installs a `FakeMouseEvent` so `pageX`/`pageY` can be read back. Because of this, interaction suites run the same `it` blocks two or three times via `describe.each` over `{Mouse Events, Pointer Events, Touch Events}`, each with its own `prepare` function and press start/end actions. The two worlds genuinely assert different event sequences.
- **Touch.** `triggerTouch(element)` fires `pointerDown`/`pointerUp` with `pointerType: 'touch'` plus a `click`. `triggerLongPress({element, advanceTimer, pointerOpts})` in `@react-aria/test-utils/src/utils.ts` fires the full compatibility-event cascade a real browser produces (pointerdown, then touchstart or mousedown depending on `pointerType`, advance by `DEFAULT_LONG_PRESS_TIME` of 500ms, pointerup, touchend, mousedown, focus, mouseup, then a `click` with `detail: 1`), honoring each event's `defaultPrevented` return so suppressed compatibility events are skipped. `window.ontouchstart` is defined and deleted to mark a device touch-capable.
- **Virtual clicks from screen readers.** These are modelled as a bare `fireEvent.click(el)` with no preceding pointer or mouse events (and `detail: 0`), which is what VoiceOver and TalkBack produce. `usePress` has a dedicated `describe('virtual click events')` block for this path. `pressElement()` in test-utils deliberately adds coordinates and pressure for mouse presses "so this isn't detected as a virtual click".
- **Platform.** `getAltKey()` and `getMetaKey()` in test-utils resolve to `Alt`/`MetaLeft` on Mac and `ControlLeft` on Windows by sniffing `navigator.userAgentData.platform` or `navigator.platform`, so keyboard-shortcut tests assert the platform-correct modifier. Suites that need a specific OS or browser mock `navigator.userAgent`/`platform` directly.
- **Viewport.** `simulateMobile(width = 700)` and `simulateDesktop(width = 701)` from `@react-spectrum/test-utils/src/testSetup.ts` spy on `window.screen.width` (clamping to <=700 or >=701), which is how `DialogTrigger` popover-to-modal/tray fallbacks and Tray rendering get tested. Overlay positioning suites additionally stub `window.visualViewport`, `HTMLElement.prototype.getBoundingClientRect`, `offsetWidth`/`offsetHeight`, `offsetParent`, `clientWidth`/`clientHeight`, and `window.getComputedStyle`.
- **Shadow DOM.** `createShadowRoot(attachTo = document.body)` returns `{shadowHost, shadowRoot, cleanup}`; content is put inside with `ReactDOM.createPortal`. Shadow behavior is gated behind a feature flag turned on per-suite with `enableShadowDOM()` from `react-stately/private/flags/flags`. Queries inside shadow trees use `shadow-dom-testing-library` (`screen.getByShadowRole`, `getAllByShadowRole`). Shadow suites are often wrapped in `if (parseInt(React.version, 10) >= 17)` with a trivial placeholder test in the `else` branch, because Jest requires at least one test per file.
- **Iframes.** Tests create an `<iframe>`, append a root div to `iframe.contentWindow.document`, and portal into it, then set up a _separate_ `userEvent.setup({document: iframeDocument})`. This is how owner-document handling is verified for FocusScope, `useInteractOutside`, and `useFocusVisible` (which also needs `addWindowFocusTracking(iframeRoot)` and a `beforeunload` teardown).
- **Real-browser-only gestures.** `vitest.browser.config.ts` registers custom Playwright commands the browser tests call: `mouseDownOnElement(selector, offsetX, offsetY)` and `mouseUp()` (for a trusted press that is held, used by the modal scroll-jump test), `lockClipboard`/`unlockClipboard`, and `setComposition`/`commitComposition` which drive a real IME through a cached Chromium CDP session (`Input.imeSetComposition`, `Input.insertText`) to emulate soft-keyboard input.

### How SSR is tested

`testSSR(filename, source, runAfterServer?)` in `packages/dev/test-utils/src/testSSR.tsx` takes the component tree as a **source string**. It transforms that string with Babel, POSTs `{filename, source}` to the SSR worker on `localhost:18235`, which renders it in a Node environment and returns HTML. The test then sets `document.body.innerHTML` to that HTML, optionally runs the `runAfterServer` callback so assertions can be made against the pre-hydration markup (this is how "the server HTML contains no dialog" and "viewport reports 0x0" are checked), evaluates the source to get a real element, and hydrates it with `ReactDOMClient.hydrateRoot` inside an `SSRProvider`. During hydration `console.error` and `console.warn` are captured into an array and the returned promise rejects with the first one, so any hydration mismatch fails the test. Most `.ssr.test.js` files are therefore a single "should render without errors" smoke test; the interesting ones pass a `runAfterServer` callback and then assert the post-hydration DOM.

### Exported testing utilities: @react-aria/test-utils

The public package `@react-aria/test-utils` exports:

- `User` — the entry point. `new User({interactionType, advanceTimer})` wraps `userEvent.setup({delay: null, pointerMap})` once. `interactionType` is `'mouse' | 'touch' | 'keyboard'` (default mouse) and `advanceTimer` is how the testers advance time (pass `jest.advanceTimersByTime` with fake timers, or a `setTimeout` promise with real ones). `user.createTester(patternName, opts)` builds a pattern tester inheriting those options.
- Pattern testers, one class per ARIA pattern: `CheckboxGroup`, `ComboBox`, `Dialog`, `GridList`, `ListBox`, `Menu`, `RadioGroup`, `Select`, `Table`, `Tabs`, `Tree`. Each takes `{root, interactionType?, advanceTimer?, direction?}` plus pattern-specific options (`Dialog` takes `overlayType: 'modal' | 'popover'`; `GridList`/`ListBox` take `layout`; `ComboBox` takes an optional explicit `trigger`).
- `installMouseEvent`, `installPointerEvent`, `pointerMap`, and `triggerLongPress`.

**How a tester works**, using `DialogTester` as the model. The constructor takes the trigger `root`; if the root contains exactly one button it uses that, if it contains several it picks the one with `aria-haspopup`, otherwise it treats the root itself as the trigger. `open()` dispatches the interaction appropriate to the interaction type (`user.click`, `user.pointer({keys: '[TouchA]'})`, or focus plus `user.keyboard('[Enter]')`), then **asserts the pattern's contract while waiting**: for a popover it waits for `aria-controls` to appear on the trigger and for an element with that id to exist; for a modal it waits for a `[role=dialog], [role=alertdialog]` element and then throws unless that dialog contains the active element and the trigger is no longer focused. `close()` presses Escape and waits for the dialog to leave the document. Getters (`getTrigger()`, `getDialog()`) return live elements or null. So the testers are not just element locators: their built-in waits encode accessibility expectations, and a component that opens a dialog without moving focus into it fails inside `open()` rather than at an assertion. Several tests use a tester purely to locate elements and then drive the interaction themselves with raw browser events, with a comment explaining that test-utils' user-event and vitest's browser user-event differ for focus events.

`act` is re-exported from a wrapper (`@react-aria/test-utils/src/act.ts`) that prefers `React.act`, falls back to `ReactDOMTestUtils.act`, and becomes a pass-through when `IS_REACT_ACT_ENVIRONMENT` is false, so the same testers work in the Vitest browser run.

### Internal render override

Tests import `render`, `renderHook`, `act`, `fireEvent`, `within`, `screen`, `waitFor` from `@react-spectrum/test-utils-internal` (source at `packages/dev/test-utils/src`), not from `@testing-library/react` directly. That package's `renderOverride.tsx` wraps `render` and `renderHook` with `StrictModeWrapper`, and also exports `renderv3`, which additionally wraps in a Spectrum `Provider` with the default theme. It re-exports everything from `@react-aria/test-utils` plus `testSSR`, `createShadowRoot`, `triggerTouch`, `mockClickDefault` (a window-level click listener that calls `preventDefault`, used to assert link behavior), `mockImplementation`, `simulateMobile`, and `simulateDesktop`.

### Other patterns worth copying

- Feature-flagged behavior is enabled per-suite in `beforeAll` (`enableShadowDOM()`), not globally.
- Suites reach into module internals to assert bookkeeping does not leak: `focusScopeTree.size`, `hasSetupGlobalListeners`, and `changeHandlers` are imported directly and asserted on before and after interactions. That is how "no listener leaks" and "the scope tree shrinks back to 1" are pinned.
- Animations are neutralized where they would otherwise need real time: `Element.prototype.animate` is replaced with a mock returning `{finished: Promise.resolve()}` (Spectrum 2 CoachMark), and `requestAnimationFrame` is sometimes swapped for `setTimeout(cb, 0)` so a dialog can be removed from the DOM before the callback runs (ContextualHelp).
- `it.each` with tagged-template tables is the standard way to run one behavior across library versions, placements, overlay types, or `contain`/`isPortaled` combinations. The test _name_ then contains `$Name`-style placeholders rather than the resolved values, which makes exact-name matching in a conformance suite awkward.
